# Known issues

Confirmed problems only. Severity: Blocker / High / Medium / Low.

## Native PC build (branch `claude/native-pc`)

| ID | Severity | Issue | Reproduction | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| N1 | High | Crash in Professor Birch's intro: `Task_HandleMonAnimation` read `gBattleStruct` (NULL outside battle). Harmless on the GBA (reads BIOS), fatal natively. | New game, advance Birch's speech until Lotad appears. | Fixed with `NATIVE_NULL_GUARD` (`src/pokemon_animation.c`). |
| N2 | High | Naming screen sprite and VBlank callbacks ran one frame after the screen data was freed (7 NULL accesses). | Confirm the player's name. | Fixed: callbacks return when the data is gone (`src/naming_screen.c`). |
| N3 | High | Menu cursor (▶) invisible and stray glyph pieces ("NO" showed as "NOI"); text at tile-aligned positions was also drawn into the next tile. Cause: `1 << 32` and `x >> 32` in the glyph copy, 0 on ARM but unchanged on x86. | Any YES/NO or list menu. | Fixed with `ARM_LSL32`/`ARM_LSR32` in `src/text.c`. Re-checked 2026-10-09: start menu cursor and save YES/NO correct. |
| N4 | Medium | The RHH "Powered by" logo in the intro looked corrupted. Cause: the Linux build used the "fast draw" GBA renderer, which draws every text background as 16-colour; this screen uses a 256-colour background. Decompression is not involved: all 7469 compressed assets decode correctly natively (`check-decompress`). | Launch; first intro screen. | Fixed: Linux now uses the same "easy draw" renderer as the Windows build (`Makefile_pc`). Visual re-check pending. |
| N5 | High | Every player would get the same Trainer ID and RNG seed: the GBA hardware timers used for seeding do not run natively. | New game. | Fixed: host-clock entropy seeds the RNG (`Platform_GetEntropy32`). |
| N6 | Medium | The game's own soft reset (`DoSoftReset`, used by the clear-save screen, credits, save-failure screen and others) exited the program. | Title screen, hold B+SELECT+UP, answer NO. | Fixed: in-process reset (`src/platform/game_memory.c`) restores all game memory to its boot state and restarts the game thread; window, audio, save image and clock survive. Verified on Linux: the intro restarts, the process stays up, the save file is unchanged. Note: in this build the A+B+START+SELECT chord (and Ctrl+R, which sends it) belongs to the multiplayer Local Soft Reset handler and never reboots, by design. |
| N7 | Medium | Integer division by zero crashes on x86 but returns a value on the GBA. | Any such code path (none seen yet in play). | Mitigated at runtime by `fault_compat.c`; sites are logged and must be fixed in source (the compiler may still mis-optimise them). |
| N8 | Low | `src/platform/bios.c` contains code derived from VBA-M (GPL-2.0+) and mGBA (MPL-2.0). | — | Open: replace with an independent GBATEK-based implementation or comply before giving the build to anyone. Owner's decision 2026-11-04: "Leave for now" - nothing is changed yet, and the PC build is not given to anyone outside until the owner decides. |
| N9 | High | New game on an empty save crashed at random natively. An erased flash sector reads id 0xFFFF, which indexed the 14-entry save-location table far out of bounds (`CopySaveSlotData`; corrupt ids in `GetSaveValidStatus` likewise). Harmless stray read on the GBA; a fault natively whenever that address is unmapped. | Start with no save file (failed about 1 run in 2). | Fixed: such sectors are treated as invalid (`src/save.c`, PORTABLE only). |
| N10 | Blocker | New game hung at the move into the truck. The synchronous map loader (`DoMapLoadLoop`) busy-waits for DMA3 requests that the GBA's VBlank interrupt drains during the loop; natively interrupts only run between frames. | Finish Birch's intro. | Fixed: `Platform_SpinWaitForVBlank` lets the frame end when a poll keeps spinning within one frame (like a GBA lag frame); callers that poll once per frame are unaffected (`src/bg.c`, `src/platform/sdl2.c`). |
| N11 | High | Wild battle start crashed natively: the "slice" battle transition's VBlank callback ran once more after the transition data was freed (NULL reads; harmless on the GBA, the screen is already black). Found by the NULL trap. | Walk into a wild Pokémon. | Fixed: after release the transition callbacks read a zeroed stand-in (`src/battle_transition.c`, covers all transition types). |
| N12 | Medium | Assembler-built script data was writable natively (it is ROM on the GBA), so it was outside the soft-reset memory and a stray write would silently change a script. | — (found by `check-game-sections`). | Fixed: script data is read-only natively (`tools/pc_port/asm_to_x86.sed`); `make -f Makefile_pc check-game-sections` fails if any game object keeps a writable section outside the reset memory. |
| N13 | High | Opening the Pokédex crashed natively. `CreatePokedexList`'s Hoenn loop runs one entry past the Hoenn dex (`HoennToNationalOrder(HOENN_DEX_COUNT)` returns 0, an upstream off-by-one), and `GetSetPokedexFlag(0, ...)` then reads index 0x1FFFFFFF. A stray read on the GBA. Found by UBSan. | Start menu, POKéDEX. | Fixed natively: dex number 0 or out of range reads as "not seen/caught" and is never written (`src/pokedex.c`). Upstream bug; GBA build unchanged. |
| N14 | High | Pokédex list: `UpdateSelectedMonSpriteId` reads `gSprites[0xFFFF]` before checking for 0xFFFF (vanilla code). Megabytes out of range natively. Found by UBSan. | Pokédex, open an entry. | Fixed natively by testing the id first (`src/pokedex.c`). |
| N15 | Medium | Summary screen passes `WINDOW_NONE` (255) to `PutWindowTilemap` for windows it never added (vanilla code); the GBA reads unrelated memory as a window, natively that memory differs and could draw garbage. Found by UBSan. | Party menu, SUMMARY, change pages. | Fixed natively: window tilemap calls ignore invalid ids (`src/window.c`). |
| N16 | Low | The overworld time-of-day form check computed battle party state from `gBattleStruct` while it is NULL (outside battle); natively only an optimisation kept it from reading address ~NULL. Found by UBSan. | Walk around (runs every frame). | Fixed natively: no battle party state outside battle (`src/pokemon.c`). |
| N17 | High | Windows build: MinGW uses the MSVC struct layout by default (bit-fields not packed across types, 8-byte members 8-aligned), which breaks save compatibility; the engine's own static asserts failed. | Build the .exe without the flags. | Fixed: `-mno-ms-bitfields -mno-align-double`; `abi_layout.py` now also checks the Windows layout (172 types match). |
| N18 | Medium | Windows build: assembler "read-only" data (`.rodata`) was linked into a writable section of its own. | `objdump -h pokeemerald-mp.exe`. | Fixed: converted to `.rdata` (read-only) on Windows; `check-game-sections` passes on Windows. |
| N19 | Medium | Windows build: `RandomUniform` and three other functions are weak aliases, which MinGW does not resolve (link failure); `gTestRunnerEnabled` is weak data. | Link the .exe. | Fixed natively: plain functions/data (`src/random.c`, `src/test_runner_stub.c`). |
| N20 | High | Windows (Wine): one crash during the intro after loading a save, in about ten launches; not reproduced since. Cause unknown. | Launch with a save; unknown trigger. | **Closed: not reproduced** (2026-10-21; the `crash.log` reporter, `src/platform/crash_report.c`, stays for a recurrence). Stress on the current Windows build under Wine (the Classic 2D game of engine `7223275b`, whose game code is the candidate's; L), each launch from a save in a folder of its own, as a player starts it: **250 launches, all 250 reached the field** (read from the game's memory), no crash during the intro. 50 with the first key 15 s in; 200 with a key at a seeded random moment 1-14 s in - 42 on the copyright screen, 94 in the intro, 64 in the expansion intro before its first cry (N29's path, guarded since `31d15a804`, the likely cause). At the original rate of about one crash in ten launches, 250 clean launches would have a chance of about 4 in 10^12; at one in fifty, about 0.6%. One other fault, at a close: N42 (1 of 250). |
| N21 | Info | Title screen B+SELECT opens the Berry Program Update, which waits for a link cable forever natively, exactly as on a GBA with no cable attached. | Title screen, hold B+SELECT. | By design (vanilla); close the game to leave. |
| N22 | Medium | Visible wild spawns: when no object had the new spawn's local id, `UpdateOverworldWildEncounter` used `gObjectEvents[OBJECT_EVENTS_COUNT]` (the memory after the array) and could pass it to `RemoveObjectEvent`, corrupting whatever follows (upstream expansion bug, also present in the GBA build). Found by AddressSanitizer. | Walk among visible wild Pokémon. | Fixed natively (`src/wild_encounter_ow.c`); GBA build unchanged (candidate fix for the emulator build). |
| N23 | Low | Benign over-reads seen by AddressSanitizer: the quickstart HUD loads a 16-colour palette from a 6-colour array; the smol decoder reads one word past compressed data. Both read adjacent read-only data; output is unaffected (all 7469 assets decode correctly). | Title screen; any compressed graphic. | Accepted; revisit if a crash ever points there. |
| N24 | Blocker | Entering any Pokémon Center crashed natively. The Center's resume script (`CableClub_OnResume`) starts the Union Room player scan (`InitUnionRoom` -> `Task_InitUnionRoom`), which drives the GBA Wireless Adapter library; natively that library is never set up (no adapter), so it dereferenced NULL `gSTWIStatus`. | Walk into the Oldale Town Pokémon Center. | Fixed natively (engine `5b2bcb7ce`): no Union Room scan on PC (`ShouldCheckForUnionRoom`, `src/link.c`); GBA ROM unchanged. Verified on Linux and Windows (Wine): enter, heal at the nurse, save, leave, re-enter. **The Windows test build in checkpoint `native-wip-20261009-2` still has this crash.** |
| N25 | High | The clear-save screen (title screen, B+SELECT+UP) crashed natively after a few seconds. Verified on Linux (2D and 3D builds) and Windows (Wine): screen stays up, NO soft-resets, Continue works, save unchanged. The test builds in `native-wip-20261009-3` still have this crash. |

Under Wine the DLL exists, so no test here could show it. | `objdump -p pokeemerald-mp.exe` lists `XINPUT1_3.dll` (builds before `34ddb17a6`). | Fixed (engine `34ddb17a6` on `claude/native-pc`, in `claude/native-graphics` since `bf1107902`): the game loads XInput when it first reads the controller (`xinput1_4.dll`, then `xinput1_3.dll`, then `xinput9_1_0.dll`, present on every Windows since Vista) and has no controller if none loads. Imports now: KERNEL32, SDL2, SHELL32, WS2_32, msvcrt (and OPENGL32 for 3D). Starts and closes under Wine; no controller tested. All earlier Windows test builds have the import. |
| N35 | High | Opening the PC's Pokémon boxes crashed natively (depending on the heap): `CreateDisplayMonSprite` loads the display Pokémon's palette from `sStorage->displayMonPalette` before anything set it - `sStorage` is not zeroed, so the pointer is whatever the heap held. The GBA loads 32 stray bytes (the real palette replaces them before the sprite shows); natively the address can be unmapped. | PC, Move Pokémon (with the NULL trap off). | Fixed natively (engine `389d744b6` on `claude/battle-3d-integration`; `ae6533e25` on `claude/native-pc-box-fixes`, on `claude/native-pc` `1579e7fd8`, not merged yet): a blank palette loads instead (PORTABLE only). Reproduced before the fix (gdb), alive after. |
| N36 | High | Leaving the PC's boxes crashed natively, every time: `Task_ChangeScreen` frees `sStorage` when it hands over to the field, a summary, the naming screen or the bag, and `CB2_PokeStorage` then runs that frame's sprite callbacks, which read through it (the cursor's shadow first; vanilla). Harmless on the GBA. | PC, Move Pokémon: close the box, or open a Pokémon's Summary. | Fixed natively (engine `fae86b6c1` on `claude/battle-3d-integration`; `aa2841d7a` on `claude/native-pc-box-fixes`, not merged yet): that last frame skips its sprite update, the next screen resets the sprites (PORTABLE only). Reproduced with gdb before; alive after: closing the box, the summary and back, leaving the PC. |
| N37 | High | A battle against two trainers at once (two trainers see the player together, Emerald's "two trainers" double battle) aborted natively at its start ("stack smashing detected"); on the GBA the same code writes one byte past an array. `BufferBattlePartyOrderBySide` (`party_menu.c`), double-battle branch: in this game each trainer has a party of its own (`gParties[B_TRAINER_OPPONENT_B]`), so the left and right opponents both have party index 0 and the loop ran on to `partyIndexes[6]`. Game logic, not graphics: found by the battle-environment survey. | Sootopolis Gym B1F, stand at 8,13 (two trainers face that cell); also Victory Road 1F, the Abandoned Ship's rooms 1F at 10,15, the Trick House's seventh puzzle at 8,12. | **Fixed** on `claude/native-pc-n37-fix` (`a49b89b15`, `PORTABLE` only: the loop stops when the six places are full; the six kept are the ones the game always kept, so party order, switching, double and Multi battles, saves and link data are unchanged; GBA ROM unchanged). Regression test `make -f Makefile_pc check-party-order` (1119 checks; aborts on the commit before the fix as the game did). Fought through with the fix: the four two-trainer spots, a single (Roxanne), a double (Tate and Liza), a Multi with a partner (Steven) and a wild double, all won; all eight again on the integration candidate `claude/battle-environments-2` (`49c47919e`, its TEST build, with N39 and N40 fixed, found by the round's regression survey and the first fight-through on the candidate). Not merged into `claude/native-pc` (the owner's decision). |
| N38 | High | Mossdeep's Space Center Multi battle (Maxie and Tabitha against the player and Steven) aborted natively as it began ("stack smashing detected"): `BattleStringExpandPlaceholders` (`battle_message.c`) puts each placeholder's text together in a 32-byte buffer from where the previous one ended, so "You are challenged by {B_TRAINER1_NAME_WITH_CLASS} and {B_TRAINER2_NAME_WITH_CLASS}!" wrote the second class and name past the buffer (any battle whose message names two trainers with their classes). Found while fighting the Multi battle through for N37. | Mossdeep Space Center 2F, the Multi battle with Steven. `--selftest-battle-names` on a build without the fix aborts the same way (AddressSanitizer: stack-buffer-overflow in `BattleStringExpandPlaceholders`). | **Fixed** on `claude/native-pc-n37-fix` (`479a58aac`, `PORTABLE` only: each placeholder's text starts at the buffer's start, copied out before the next, so every message reads the same; GBA ROM unchanged). Regression test `make -f Makefile_pc check-battle-names` (1724 checks: every trainer's class and name against the longest, both placeholders together against each alone). The Multi battle fought through and won with the fix. Not merged into `claude/native-pc`. |
| N39 | High | A trainer's double battle (two trainers at once, or Tate and Liza at the Mossdeep Gym) crashed natively (segmentation fault) as the opponents' Pokemon were sent out, when no Pokemon cry had played since the game started (right after continuing a save): `Task_PlayCryWhenReleasedFromBall` stops the cry playing before the doubles' cries (`StopCryAndClearCrySongs`), and with none played yet `gMPlay_PokemonCry` is still NULL; `m4aMPlayStop` (the PC's sound driver, `m4a_pc.c`) read through it. The GBA reads its BIOS there and does nothing. Found by the battle-environment regression survey once N37 let these battles start (a battle started straight from the field after a continued save). | Start the game, skip the intro, continue a save and start a trainer's double battle before any Pokemon cry has played (the battle survey's TEST driver did so at Sootopolis Gym B1F 8,13, Victory Road 1F 31,17, the Abandoned Ship's rooms 1F 10,15 and with Tate and Liza; the fight-through runs with the TEST driver's autopilot did not crash there; what differed was not looked into). `--selftest-cry-stop` on a build without the fix crashes the same way. | **Fixed** on `claude/native-pc-n37-fix` (`8af5b8769`, the PC sound driver only: `m4aMPlayStop` returns for a NULL player, as `IsPokemonCryPlaying` already did; no game code, the GBA build does not compile the file). Regression test `make -f Makefile_pc check-cry-stop` (5 checks; it crashed on the commit before the fix). The three maps photographed again with the fix, and Tate and Liza's battle (it crashed the same way without the fix): each battle drawn in 3D. Merged into `claude/battle-environments-2` (`f0e0e4723`). Not merged into `claude/native-pc`. |
| N40 | High | A move animation that shakes a battler or the battle's background (Rock Throw, Rock Slide, Metal Claw, Dragon Claw, Ancient Power, Superpower, Snore, Avalanche, Barrage...) could crash natively (segmentation fault) as the move played: `AnimShakeMonOrBattlePlatforms` (`battle_anim_normal.c`) keeps the address of what it shakes (`gBattle_BG3_X`/`_Y`, `gSpriteCoordOffsetX`/`_Y`) in two s16 sprite fields and puts it back together as `lo | hi << 16`; on the GBA those variables are in IWRAM, whose addresses never have bit 15 set, natively they can, and the negative low half sign-extends over the high one (0xFFFFxxxx). Whether a build crashes depends on where the linker put the four variables: all four in the 3D build, two in some 2D builds. Found by N37's verification (Mossdeep's Space Center Multi battle crashed in the candidate's fight-through). | A battle where a Pokemon uses Rock Slide (or another move above) in the 3D build. `--selftest-shake-anim` on a build without the fix crashes. | **Fixed** on `claude/native-pc-n37-fix` (`ab4c6ac1c`, `PORTABLE` only: the low half is read unsigned, as `SetCallbackToStoredInData6` already does; the shake is the same; `#line` keeps the GBA's line numbers, the GBA ROM is unchanged). Regression test `make -f Makefile_pc check-shake-anim` (11 checks: each of the four values, and a value at an address with bit 15 set in any layout; it crashed on the commit before the fix). Merged into `claude/battle-environments-2` (`06a972aed`, `49c47919e`). Not merged into `claude/native-pc`. |
| N41 | Low | Ally Switch with no partner on the field crashed the native game (SIGSEGV): the animation's tasks read `gSprites[gBattlerSpriteIds[partner]]` with the id 0xFF, and the data swap reloads the absent partner's picture from garbage party data (`DecodeInstructions` <- `BattleLoadMonSpriteGfx` <- `ReloadBattlerSprites`). In a battle the move fails before its animation (`BS_TryAllySwitch`), so only the Battle FX test gallery reached it (Battle FX INTEGRATION_REPORT item 9). | `tools/battlefx/gallery.sh OUT SAVE wild:263:30 MOVE_ALLY_SWITCH` (test build): 3 of 3 exit 139. | Fixed (eng `a70687a2da`, PC only, GBA lines and ROM unchanged): without the partner's sprite both tasks end at once. After: exit 0, parity 211/211; with a partner (wild2 double) unchanged, 211/211 in 3 runs. Separate and test builds only: the test drivers end a run with `exit()` on the game's thread while the main thread may be drawing (a crash in the OpenGL driver after every result is written; 2 of about 12 gallery runs on 2026-10-14; Battle FX report item 15). |
| N42 | Medium | Under Wine, closing the Windows game (Alt+F4) after a normal session on the field once ended with glibc's "double free or corruption (fasttop)" and exit -11: 1 of 98 closes in the N20 stress (2026-10-21, Windows Classic 2D build of engine `7223275b`); none in the 152 closes after it (1 of 250 in all). The message is glibc's, so it came from Linux-side code under Wine (its X11 driver, its graphics stack), not the game's Windows heap; the save was untouched (byte-identical); no `crash.log` (the game's own reporter never saw it). Not seen on Windows itself so far. | Wine on L: start with a save, play, Alt+F4; 1 close in 250 so far. | Open. The Windows runner's jobs close every game normally and check its exit code (the multiplayer and Mega checks): evidence for Windows itself. |
| U13 | High | (The native UI session's number: `docs/native-ui.md` on its app branch `claude/native-ui`, from `ui-native-wip-20261013-2` on; not the UI's code.) A battle message laid out automatically can crash natively (segmentation fault in `BuildNewString`, `line_break.c`): `BreakSubStringAutomatic` counts its lines first and can count more than the layout then fills; `BuildNewString` reads `stringLines[i].words[0]` of the empty last lines, which were never set. On the GBA it reads old memory, and the line before writes a line break over the string's end. | Route 110: beat Psychic Edward (alone, or with Triathlete Alyssa: both see (6,39)); printing his defeat line crashes, with the native UI on and off (the UI session, on the combined trial, Linux). | Fixed on `claude/emeraldtogether-expansion` (engine `ad3995ba64`: the UI session's guard in `BuildNewString`, PC build only; GBA ROM byte-identical). Verified 2026-10-13: `make -f Makefile_pc check-line-break` (engine, `--selftest-line-break`) runs the eight defeat lines the UI session's scan found (Edward, Maura, Melissa, Alberto, Lila's rematch page, Santiago, Mitchell, the secret base trainer type 4) as trainer A's and B's line through the real `BattleStringExpandPlaceholders`: 54 checks pass; the same test without the guard crashes at Edward's line (segmentation fault). In the game: Psychic Edward and Triathlete Alyssa's two-trainer battle on Route 110 in the native 3D build with the new menus on, won: their lines, "You got P960 for winning!", back on Route 110, no crash (`/home/claude/proof/u13/edward-2`, cloud). The other seven trainers were not battled in the game (the self-test covers their lines). |

## Native multiplayer (Milestone C, engine `src/platform/mp_bridge.c`)

| ID | Severity | Issue | Reproduction | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M1 | Medium | The companion verifies a GBA ROM and its manifest, never the native executable: a native client is paired with the ROM built from the same engine source by whoever starts it. A mismatched pair would show up only as a capability or mailbox refusal, not as an identity error. | Start a native client next to a companion given another build's ROM. | Mitigated for games the launcher starts: a native runtime bundle names the ROM build it was made with and the launcher refuses another before recovery or start (app `player_runtime.py`, run native-launcher-4). Open for a native game started by hand; before release: embed the build id in the executable and have the bridge report it, or give the companion a native manifest. |

| V9 | Info | 3D battles (branch `claude/battle-3d-integration`): animated tiles (water, flowers) stood still in a battle's world, which is the field's last snapshot. | Any battle near water. | Fixed where practical (engine `79fc306f3`, `0fa2bf87a`; paced by measured frame times since `e1903a302`): the field's tile animations run into the battle world's tiles and the world is redrawn when they change. Every battle frame is timed (present to present): the glide's frames choose how often the tiles may redraw the world (at every change while they stay within a 60 Hz frame, every 4 or 16 game frames up to two or four times that, not at all beyond), and later redraw frames move that up or down. On software OpenGL here (glide 38-67 ms a frame): every 16 game frames at first, then 32, then still; at 1280x720 still from the start. Forced on with `PKMN_DIORAMA_BATTLE_TILES=1`: Oldale Town's flowers and Route 110's sea move (Route 102's pond uses tiles the GBA does not animate either). A real graphics card: the owner's run of `battle3d-test-runs\wip-20261010-3` (V3). |

| V37 | Info | The Lavaridge tileset's stepped relief (Mt. Chimney, Jagged Pass, Fiery Path, Magma Hideout) is the heaviest terrain: about 135,000 triangles in a battle's view against 2,000-3,000 in other caves (the battle session's survey); on software OpenGL the field there takes very long to reach a battle. Reduced in graphics `graphics-wip-20261012-2`: every chunk drawn from indices, a quad's four corners once (`a742038b0`: Jagged Pass at 1280x720, the same spot, 948,870 vertices built, 632,580 uploaded with 16-bit indices, the game's GPU buffers 33.9 MB to 24.8 MB; pixel-identical but for the ash motes), and no undersides where a column stands on its own floor (`9d63e60f5`: Jagged Pass from 2219k to 1993k vertices a frame). The second did change the picture (thin sky-coloured lines along the steps, V47) and is undone in `graphics-handoff-20261012` (`1d54639cf`): with the undersides the benchmark's Jagged Pass scene at 1280x720 builds 1,055,694 vertices and uploads 703,796 (948,870 and 632,580 without them, `PKMN_DIORAMA_CHUNK_LOG=1`), draws 936.8k triangles a frame (842.5k) and the game's GPU memory is 55.6 MB (52.7 MB, the benchmark's `vram_game_mb`): about 11 % more terrain work there, the indexing's third less still kept; a Jagged Pass battle's view 188,086 triangles (168,812 on the earlier trial). On software OpenGL the process's RAM at Mt. Chimney rose (1080p benchmark, five runs each: 427-466 MB before indexing, 513-516 MB after): that is the software renderer's own queue (llvmpipe's 64 KB blocks of binned triangles waiting for its rasterizer threads, 147 MB to 233 MB at the end of a run, measured by caller), which runs further ahead now that the vertex work is a third less. The game's allocations are the same, its scratch buffer 0.5 MB more resident (the indices), its GPU buffers 18.6 MB smaller (74.5 MB to 55.9 MB); with llvmpipe on one thread (`LP_NUM_THREADS=0`, no queue) the indexed build uses less (355 MB to 329 MB). A graphics card has no such queue in the game's memory; its RAM there is the owner's benchmark's `ram_mb`. The relief itself (its visible steps) is unchanged: making it coarser changed the look. | Jagged Pass, Mt. Chimney. | Reduced; open until the owner's GPU benchmark (V39) shows whether it still costs. |

## Animated battle sprites (branch `claude/sprite-integration`, native build)

| ID | Severity | Issue | Reproduction | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | Info | The library sprites are drawn one pixel to one GBA pixel, the same grid as the healthboxes and text: a Black/White Pokémon is about 7–9 % taller than its GBA picture (median of the opaque heights: front 1.086, back 1.068), much more for the big ones (the GBA pictures stop at 64 pixels). | Any battle; `play-gba-sprites.bat` to compare. | By design (no resampling of the pixel art). |
| S2 | Low | Taller sprites reach further: in double battles the player's left Pokémon's head can pass behind the foe's lower healthbox (healthboxes stay in front, as on the GBA). | `wild2:` battle with Gardevoir in the first slot. | Open: a layout question for the battle HUD (the native UI's HUD hides the GBA healthboxes). |
| S3 | Low | Spinda's spots are the animation's own pattern, not the individual's (the GBA draws them from the personality). | Any Spinda. | Open (the library has one Spinda). |
| S4 | Low | A battler whose palette a move changes in a way that is not a fade or tint (greyscale, inverted colours) shows its GBA sprite while that lasts; fades, flashes and tints are carried over. | Moves that grey or invert a battler. | By design (the safe fallback). |
| S5 | Low | A Pokémon taller than the room above its feet, or wider than the picture, is cut at the picture's edge in 2D battles; in 3D battles it goes on above the picture up to the window's top (at least 20 GBA pixels at the default scaling; none with "fill" scaling at exactly 3:2) and past the picture's sides above the text box's rows (Lugia's wings). Measured over every species: 181 of 730 animated fronts reach above the picture at rest (at most 45 GBA pixels), 3 fronts and 1 back past its sides. | Wild Lugia or Rayquaza with `PKMN_DIORAMA_BATTLE=0`. | Open for 2D battles. |
| S6 | Info | Gen 6 species, every Mega and Primal form and some forms keep the GBA sprite (their art is community art without a recorded permission), so Mega Evolution and Primal Reversion switch from an animated to a GBA picture. | Mega Evolution with a Mega Stone and the Mega Ring. | By design (`runtime-permission-gate`); `BATTLE_SPRITES_REPORT.md` lists them. |
| S7 | Low | Role Play and Snatch build a new sprite from a freshly decompressed picture (not a copy of the battler's tiles): that effect shows the GBA art. Copies of the battler's tiles (Double Team, afterimages, stat-change silhouettes) use the library sprite. | Use Role Play or Snatch. | Open (two moves). |
| S8 | Medium | 3D battles: a move's copy of a battler on BG1 or BG2 (Bite, Slam, Dig, Dragon Dance and every move with `monbg`) that reached the picture's left or right edge was carried out across the world to the window's edge as flat lines (the "dark line" beside large Pokemon: Wailord's back, Rayquaza's tail, Lugia's wing; also the GBA pictures without the pack). `gba_easy_draw.c` `SceneEdge` left sprites out but not that copy; `gl_battle.c` `DrawCarriedOut` smears each row's edge colour (Battle FX COORDINATION section 3). | `tools/battlefx/gallery.sh` with `wild2:384:50:249:50`, `PKMN_TEST_PARTY=321:50,249:50` and those moves, then `tools/battle_env/carried_edges.py` on the shots. | Fixed (eng `f8dbffa88a`, PC only): the copy's pixels (library frame or own tiles) at the edges are passed over, so the colour carried out is the background's. Before: 4 of 21 shots with the pack, 2 of 21 without; after: 0 of 21 and 0 of 21 at 640x360, 0 of 12 at 960x540 (both), 0 of 12 at 1264x711; Battle FX parity exact. Not seen on a graphics card (the cloud draws with software OpenGL; the edge colours are computed before scaling, the same on any GPU). |

## Emulator build and gameplay (from the 2026-10-09 audit)

| ID | Severity | Issue | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| G1 | Medium | `trade_effect_choice.py:53` still refuses a trade where a Pokémon that knows a post-Gen-6 move evolves and must choose a move; the owner decided post-Gen-6 moves trade as-is. | **Fixed** 2026-11-04 (app: this change): a move the Pokémon knows may be any the engine has (to `MAX_STORED_MOVE`, 847, as the rest of the trade's checks and the engine allow); the move it is learning stays a Gen 6 one (the engine's learnset check, `multiplayer_trade_effects.c`). `tests/test_trade_effect_choice.py`: moves 622, 700 and 847 kept, 848 refused, a post-Gen-6 move to learn refused; the trade suites passed (69 tests, 454 subtests). Not run in a real trade (no such Pokémon in a fixture). |
| G2 | Medium | `docs/balance-defaults.md`, cited by four source files, does not exist. | Open |
| G2 | (update 2026-11-03; **fixed**, records only) | `docs/balance-defaults.md` written from the code as it is: the host's values (`balance.py`, every field: the world clock, World Events, friendship, Lucky trades, wagers) and the game's (`include/constants/multiplayer_balance.h`: the Shiny Charm's and Mega Ring's coin prices, the coin pack, the Mega Stone price, the Lucky IV floor), with the owner's later changes noted; no value changed. `tests/test_balance_doc.py` fails when the page's host table differs from `balance.py`. | **Fixed** (the page; the values are the code's) |
| G3 | Medium | The 120-second World Event claim release after a disconnect could let two players catch the same event Pokémon. | Improved, still open: the 120 s now count active seconds only (paused while nobody is online) and survive a host restart (Phase 11, app `467743f`, tests in `test_shared_world.py`); a claimant who stays away longer, finishes the battle offline and catches it can still end up with a second copy. Shared wild spawns use a 600 s grace for the same reason (`docs/shared-spawn-architecture.md`, engine). Audit 2026-10-13: a claimant reconnecting within its grace now keeps the claim, and its late catch is accepted while nobody else holds it (M16, M20). **Decided 2026-11-05** (the owner, `docs/owner-decisions.md` #38): the encounter is reserved for five minutes after its claimant disconnects, the claimant may reconnect and reclaim it then, it is released afterwards, and no claim is ever duplicated. **Built 2026-11-05** (app `9ee9899`, `6e56b1d`; `docs/legendary-catch-resolution.md`): `BALANCE.event_claim_grace_seconds` 300 (was 120), counted while anybody is online as every World Event timer; one deadline checked on every path (a reconnection at 299 s keeps the claim, at 300 s finds it released, whichever comes first, the reconnection or the host's tick); reclaimed on reconnection; one holder at a time; a catch reported by an earlier claimant while another player holds the claim is not accepted ("busy", logged "deferred", no verdict decided: its game reports again until the claim ends or the owner is decided, and a later winner's catch makes it late and released, Update 17); grants, reclaims and releases saved at once; claims stored by older builds load with five minutes. L: `EventReservationTests` (13, with a 3,000-step randomized model against the rule: 29 lapses, 49 reclaims, 9 waiting catches, 90 restarts), 13 mutants all caught, 824 passed over the 51 test files that use these modules; with the location privacy change, 328 passed over the 14 files both touch; and over the real host's connections (app `18c067d`, `test_world_server.py`, loopback): a claimant whose connection closes keeps the event Pokemon (another trainer told "busy" at 250 and 299 s), reclaims it by reconnecting, and after a second drop it is released at exactly 300 s, the other trainer claims it and the first, back late, is told "busy"; a claimant that goes silent without closing is ended by the heartbeat and the same five minutes follow (both fail with the old 120 s). W: run 325's `app-tests` (app `1807d43`, the rule's unit tests) passed; run 334's `app-tests` (app `2aecaea`) passed with the wire tests (as first written, at exactly 300 s; their step just past it, `ad45793`, passed in `python-tests` runs 583-585). Left as they were (no second copy can result): when the event's own time runs out while another player holds the claim, the first valid report still decides after it, so an earlier claimant whose reservation lapsed can win over the last holder if its report comes first; a shiny event knocked out by such a late report still ends (the owner's rule of 2026-10-24); a game closed while the world stays connected (an abandoned battle's or RESET's restart in the same session) is not a disconnect, the 60 s away-from-the-map rule applies (closing the game ends the session and its connection: a disconnect). |
| G4 | Low | World Event timers keep counting while nobody is online (the world clock pauses). | Fixed (Phase 11, app `da6b2fb`, `467743f`): event, spawn and claim timers use the shared world's active clock, started and stopped at the exact join/leave moment, and are saved as remaining durations. |
| G5 | Info | The in-game clock runs 12x for everything time-based, including berries and daily events (consequence of 1 world day = 2 real hours). | Fixed in the native game for the daily reset and berries (expansion Phase 11, eng 8b049791c8; test `check-clock`, 45 checks, eng 41f3cc2d27): they follow the player's real clock while connected. Open: other RTC consumers (tides, time-based evolutions follow the world clock by design) and the emulator build. |
| G6 | High | The expansion's Phase 1 "permanent Gen 9 Exp. Share, all six slots" was not in effect: with I_EXP_SHARE_FLAG 0 IsGen6ExpShareEnabled is FALSE, so only a Pokémon holding the Exp. Share item gained experience without battling; the checklist had marked it implemented from a code audit. Found 2026-10-13 while verifying the link-battle exclusions. | Fixed in the native game (eng 17e82d85ae, PORTABLE): always on. Verified in the game: native-exp-share-5 (the Pokémon not sent out +7, the game's Exp. Share message) against native-exp-share-before-1 (+0). The emulator build (GBA ROM byte-identical) keeps the held item only. |
| G7 | Low | The host's times of day differed from the game's: evening (and the shared wild Pokémon's night weights) from world hour 17, morning until 10; the game (OW_TIMES_OF_DAY GEN_6, whose clock is the world clock while connected) uses 18 and 11. For one world hour a day shared spawns used night weights while the game showed day. Found by the engine-host weight check. | Fixed (app 12efd12: `time_of_day_for_hour` with the game's hours; test_shared_world.py covers all 24 hours; `check_engine_spawn_weights.py`: 0 differences). |
| G8 | Info | Co-op Gym battles (and PvP) are link battles: no experience for anyone (BattleTypeAllowsExp) and the personal party is restored afterwards. | Owner decision 2026-10-13: co-op battles award experience, PvP none. Implemented in the combined game (engine `5a2e4f20c8`): each game awards its own party after the battle (FEATURE_STATUS). Tested by `check-coop-exp`, the award's screens in the game, and a real two-player co-op battle through the launcher (native-launcher-coop-6, after M22-M24 were fixed). PvP unchanged (none). |
| G9 | Medium | Six of the project's own GBA tests fail (`make check`, 2026-10-13): `test/multiplayer_battle_controller.c` lines 39, 62, 95, 123, 156 (native battle controller validation: roles and padding, GET ALL, owner DONE, malformed text, forbidden setters) and `test/multiplayer_local_reset.c` line 572 (Gen 6 personal shiny rolls in a static reset). They fail on the base game too (the test ROM is unchanged by the expansion work; TEST_PLAN section 4). Either the tests are out of date or the code regressed; the multiplayer proofs with real clients pass. | Fixed (tests out of date, not game defects): the five controller tests and the local reset test brought up to date (engine `fa0d9fd462`, `ee8d059a91`). Retested 2026-10-14: `make check TESTS="Multiplayer"` 133 of 133 pass. Cause: the controller tests called the validator with no battle bound (since `761c9c27ff` it counts cores from the binding); the local reset test expected the Gen 3 shiny rate outside a static reset (`80fef1a823`, owner-approved, made every personal roll Gen 6). |

So every one follows from the GEN_6 profile the tests do not pin (weather abilities, Charge stacking, Disguise, Parting Shot, trainer slides, messages, the trainer EXP multiplier...), none from a regression. Not checked one by one: whether each GEN_6 result is what the Gen 6 games do. Open as expected differences; pinning the tests' generation would make them pass. |
| G11 | Medium | Saves played while connected before the Phase 11 clock fix hold world days (1000 and later) in VAR_DAYS and the berry clock; real time counts from 0, so the daily reset and berry growth stalled for years on those saves. Found by the 2026-10-13 audit. | Fixed natively (eng `db6d6591b3`): rebased onto real time once, with no reset or growth for the gap. `check-clock` (58 checks; the fix undone: 10 fail). |
| G12 | Medium | Phase 2 treated `WEATHER_SUNNY` as sun. In Emerald it is the ordinary clear sky, the map weather of most routes; only `WEATHER_DROUGHT` is harsh sun. Fire and Grass Pokémon were therefore tripled (and doubled) on nearly every route in clear weather, in the game and on the host. Found by the audit. | Fixed: engine (eng `909a923ea8`) and host (app `cf363ee`, `spawn_director.WEATHER_TYPES`) use drought only. `check-wild-weather` (139 checks; every route's own weather changes nothing), `check_engine_spawn_weights.py` 0 differences. |
| G13 | Low | Other day counters still run on world days while connected: the Pacifidlog TM gift, Mossdeep's weekly events and match-call rematch timers read the RTC's day (the world clock), so connected they advance 12x as fast as offline. Found by the audit (code reading); not run. | **Closed** (the owner, 2026-11-05: keep the 12x world clock and the daily and weekly event timing, no real calendar days; "Keep existing behavior"): nothing changed (`docs/owner-decisions.md` #36) |

Decided 2026-10-13 (was pending): ordinary visible wild Pokémon are **shared** whenever
the game is connected to a world (host-authoritative, 8 per map, 12 during outbreaks);
fully offline play keeps the personal ones. See FEATURE_STATUS and the engine's
`docs/shared-spawn-architecture.md`.

Owner-reported (battle clipping). | **Fixed** (the text box's top edge is the top of the HUD's bottom panel when the native HUD replaces it; a first version used the window's bottom edge and hid the lower half behind the panel at 1280x720+) |
| P6 | Medium (visual) | 3D: tall grass drawn twice (flat and as standing half-tile cards) became dense dark stripes; battle grass jumped between poses at each world redraw. Owner-reported. | **Fixed** (flat GBA art, a card only at a walker's feet; still grass in the battle's kept world) |
| P7 | Medium | 3D: a vehicle the GBA draws as several OAM parts (a subsprite table) was refused by the 3D view, and one refused object on screen took the whole view back to the boxed 2D picture: the moving trucks (Littleroot), the S.S. Tidal (Slateport, Lilycove, Southern/Faraway/Birth Island, Navel Rock, the Battle Frontier dock), the submarine's shadow (Slateport harbour, Aqua Hideout B2F); in a FRLG build also the S.S. Anne. The Cable Car and Rayquaza (one centred 64x64 part) and the Seagallop (FRLG only) were never refused. Owner-escalated 2026-10-17. | **Fixed** (engine `62f086c0f1`): the parts are assembled into one picture and drawn as one card; checked in 12 scenes incl. the submarine's escape and the ferry's departure, 4 window sizes, borderless fullscreen. The Cable Car *ride* is a separate full-screen GBA cutscene (cable_car.c), shown as the GBA picture like every cutscene - not a fallback |
| P8 | Info | Developer harness: 32 `PKMN_DEBUG_GLSHOTS_GAME` captures ended the program at once (`1u << 32`). | **Fixed** |
| P9 | Medium | Native bag: every pocket switch reached the player about 0.27 s late (the hidden GBA list's 16-frame repaint ran first). Owner-reported. | **Fixed** (immediate with the native bag; the classic slide kept) |
| P10 | High | The character-setup game crashed at boot when the companion was already connected (world messages before the save existed: `RepostCatchRecord` read through the null save pointer); the app flattened it to "Setting up your character could not finish", recorded nothing, and every later try refused (`recovery_required`). Owner-reported. | **Fixed** (engine guard + selftest; app: `game_failed`/`game_closed` kept, `shell/last-error.txt` with the crash report, abandoned pre-grant attempts set aside into `abandoned-enrollments/` and restarted by the menu's setup) |
| P11 | Medium | Pause menu LINK said OFFLINE in the setup game and could not tell "starting", "never connected" and "lost" apart; with P10 the owner's host never started. Owner-reported. | **Fixed** (SETUP / CONNECTING / NOT CONNECTED / HOSTING / CONNECTED / LINK LOST; `--link-role`, `--setup` arguments) |
| P12 | Medium | Native interface: shapes wider than its 1024-pixel atlas were never drawn - the battle's message plate was missing at window heights under 720 (x1) and at 3840x1080. Found by the resolution matrix. | **Fixed** (drawn as atlas-sized tiles) |
| P13 | Info (owner) | Clock: owner-confirmed 12x (1 real minute = 12 world minutes, 5 real minutes = 1 world hour, a day and night = 2 real hours); new worlds and the character-setup game start at 12:00 noon; damaged stored clocks are repaired to noon, valid ones never touched. | **Done** (`balance.py` clock_rate 12 unchanged; app `70536a5`) |
| P14 | Info; **fixed 2026-11-05** (engine `6ce2648c`, PC only; L; W: the Windows runner, runs 319 and 320) | `test_shell_window ... long_list_scrolls` saw 23 rows moved for 20 Down presses once while the machine was fully loaded (Wine and two builds); it passes alone (twice) and in the final suite. Seen again 2026-11-05 in a loaded suite run. | **Fixed** (not load): the shell's rows take the focus when the mouse moves over them, and SDL follows every crossing of the window's edge with a motion event at the pointer. A window opening over a mouse at rest is such a crossing (Xvfb's pointer starts in the middle of the screen, where the window opens; on Windows the cursor stays wherever the player left it). When the crossing came after the list was shown, the row under the pointer (r3) took the focus from the screen's own row, so 20 Down ended on r23 and Enter would have chosen the row under the mouse. Reproduced on demand by moving the pointer out of the window and back at once (r0, r3, r23). Engine `6ce2648c` (`shell_menu.c`): a crossing forgets where the pointer was, and a motion event moves the focus only once the pointer has moved from a known place; a moving mouse, clicks, the wheel and keys are unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_shell_window.py`, a pointer crossing into the window without moving (the old engine: `'r3' != 'r0'`; fixed: 8 of 8 in three runs); its Xvfb now picks a free display (a fixed number per process met the previous test's Xvfb still going). Windows: the package job's in-game menu check puts the mouse in the middle of the screen before each menu window opens and, once the menu is shown, moves it out of the window and straight back; the screen's own row must keep the focus. On the Windows runner (GitHub-hosted, not a player's PC) the engine before the fix failed it in the 3D and the Classic 2D game (run 319: Host Server, the row under the mouse, took the focus) and engine `6ce2648c` passed it (run 320) (TEST_LEDGER) |
| P15 | Low | Character setup: when the enrollment game crashed while a request was in flight, the menu could say "The connection to the server was lost" instead of "The game closed unexpectedly" (a race, seen once end to end). | **Fixed** (app `43680a9`) |


## Next update: characters, connections, installation (2026-10-18, app `claude/admiring-bohr-dpxzuy`, engine `claude/nu-engine`; `docs/next-update-report.md`)

| ID | Severity | Issue | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NU-1 | High | A friend's character registration was refused for ever with "This account already started with a different character" after any unfinished attempt (the host kept the first un-checkpointed source; a new New Game always differed); a friend's own `invitation.json` blocked the retry. Owner-reported. | **Fixed**: an unfinished first source is replaced, retries are idempotent, the friend's restart works |
| NU-2 | High | A Solo character could not join multiplayer: characters belonged to one world account, so joining needed another New Game, which then hit NU-1. Owner-reported. | **Fixed**: universal characters (`docs/universal-characters.md`) |
| NU-3 | High | Every connection failure read "The server could not be reached"; server refusals looped to "The host did not respond". Owner-reported. | **Fixed**: `connection_diagnostics` codes with host/friend steps; refusals keep their code |
| NU-4 | High | Friends outside the host's home could connect only with a manual port forward, never behind CGNAT or over Tailscale (single-address bind, 100.64/10 refused, no router setup or relay). | **Fixed in code** (wildcard bind for Internet/VPN worlds, overlay peers, UPnP/NAT-PMP, self-run relay); **not verified across real networks** |
| NU-5 | Medium | "No multiplayer link" on the character-setup run looked like a failed connection. Owner-reported. | **Fixed** (engine `6cd8af0f`: setup-mode text; screenshots under Linux) |
| NU-6 | Medium | Installing needed the player's own game.gba, hashes and PowerShell; no installer. Owner-reported. | **Fixed**: game facts (no game.gba), Inno Setup installer, CI install/repair/uninstall check (U31-13 no longer applies to native packages) |
| NU-7 | Medium (**fixed** 2026-11-05: option (a), not gameplay; app `430acd8`; L, and W: run 291's world-loading job, 9 tests passed) | An invite code cannot be revoked or replaced for one friend; a leaked code works whenever the world is hosted. | **Fixed (option (a), the recommended one)**: Host World > the world > Invite codes for friends > **Replace a friend's code** gives that one friend a new code; the old one is refused at once and the account, its character and saves stay. Only while the world is not being hosted (its owner lock free: a hosted world's sessions are never changed under it; the menu says to stop hosting first). Crash-safe (`HostWorld.replace_credential`): the new credential goes first into a record (`credential-replacement.json`), then into the world database (`players.credential_hash`) and `host.json` (replaced whole), and the record goes last; the next open finishes one left half done (a record cut short while written changed nothing and is removed). The friend pastes the new code into Join Friend: their PC offers to replace its stored code only when the new one is for the same account on the same host (the same certificate pin), and replaces it only after that host accepted the new credential (`connection_diagnostics.check_sign_in`, a real sign-in closed at once), so a code someone made up cannot take a working one's place. Tests: the host side (a stop after each step finished by the next open; slots without a friend, the host's own account and a hosted world refused, nothing changed), the sign-in check against a real server, the stored invite's guards, the host's page, and the friend's whole Join Friend against a real host (the new code taken once accepted, a made-up one refused with the stored code kept, the old code refused by the host). |
| NU-8 | Info (open) | Not verified by a person: the real New Game + CONTINUE registration on a PC, two PCs on one network, two Internet connections, a real router's UPnP/NAT-PMP, Tailscale, a relay on a real machine, Windows Firewall's first prompt. | Open: procedure in `docs/next-update-report.md` section G |
| NU-9 | Info | No public relay or rendezvous service exists; a relay must be run by the players (`EmeraldTogether.exe --relay-server`, `relay.py`). NAT hole punching is not implemented (needs such a service; fails on symmetric NAT/CGNAT). | By design; documented in HOSTING.txt and `docs/networking.md` |
| NU-10 | Info | `--relay-server` from the packaged (windowed) EmeraldTogether.exe opens its own console; checked only by unit tests, not on Windows. | Open: try on a PC |
| NU-11 | Info (test environment) | The app suite skips 119 subtests here: the mGBA bridge's Lua subtests (no Lua 5.3 in this container) and one private-save-path test. Pure Python bridge tests still run. | Environment, not a failure |

## A player in a battle disappeared, PLAYERS showed nobody (2026-10-19, app `claude/admiring-bohr-dpxzuy`, engine `claude/mp-session-engine` `16d0aa55`; `docs/multiplayer-session-fix-20261019.md`)

| MS-1 | Critical (release-blocking) | The game's bridge refused the rival checkpoint record (Update 31) that every shared-world frame carries at bytes 200-207: `ValidWorldInbound` still required 192-207 to be zero, so the first record the companion relayed stopped the game's link for good (`runtime.log`: "Multiplayer bridge disconnected: invalid shared-world update"). The game then drew nobody and START > PLAYER > PLAYERS closed silently; the other games kept the player listed but hidden ("unavailable"). Owner-reported on two PCs; reproduced through the production launcher. | **Fixed** (engine `16d0aa55`: the bridge accepts a well-formed record by the rules of `multiplayer_rival.c`; `test_mp_bridge` `world_rival_record_accepted` fails on the old validator) |

| MS-3 | Medium | A roster player off the map's grid (x or y -1 after a step over a map edge) made the game refuse the whole snapshot, which hid every other player. | **Fixed** (app `14b8ad1`: listed and hidden at the edge until the next pose) |

Here the host was in the battle, so the whole world was locked. The characters' saves are untouched. This is the documented policy (`docs/native-battle-recovery.md`: process termination is outside the recovery mechanism); its consequence was not tested before. | **Fixed** (the owner's decision of 2026-10-19: an interrupted battle can be abandoned with no result; app `cddfcda`). Reproduced first on the unchanged code with four Linux games (repro-pairs-1, repro-host-2). On the fixed code, four Linux games through the whole launcher (`native_launcher_abandon_proof.py`): a game killed in one of two 1v1s, a lost connection in a 2v2, a game closed in a free-for-all and the host crashing in a free-for-all all passed (abandon-pairs-2, -multi-2, -exit-2, -ffa-2): the others abandon (or their launchers follow), the player who left rejoins with the same character, no result or wager, parties and saves as before, the same players battle again. Not run on Windows (CI or Wine) or PCs. Field progress made after the last save and before the battle is not kept. `docs/interrupted-battle-abandon-20261019.md` |
| MS-17 | High; **fixed and verified 2026-10-21, no longer release-blocking** (the core's grace; after a recurrence the same day, what held the command up - the host loop's blocking reads - and the companion's late replies) | In abandon-exit-1 (four Linux games, all four launchers in one harness process) a healthy free-for-all was interrupted 15 minutes in: the host's own game reported its held native deadline expired (600 frames, about 10 s, without a command) while the other three cores had used 12-15 frames, so the host launcher's event loop (world server, battle router and the host's own companion share it) stalled for about 10 s, and the host stopped the battle (`execution_interrupted`; every launcher `battle_recovery_held`). Nobody was disconnected or locked out: the battle could be abandoned (MS-16) or recovered. Not seen in the four later runs with a loop-stall watchdog (no stall over 1.5 s); the largest gap between commands in every other recorded battle was 15-67 frames, and 287 frames (4.8 s) in the pre-fix repro-pairs-1. | **Fixed and verified 2026-10-21** (the end of this cell). The history: open - if it recurs, the proof's `loop-stalls.jsonl` names the blocking code. On PCs each launcher runs in its own process. `docs/interrupted-battle-abandon-20261019.md` section 3. **Recurred 2026-10-20** (win-ffa-exit-1, engine `c76651ea`, app `4c96fe2`, the same scenario: the free-for-all rematch after a player closed their game, about 17 minutes in): between two consecutive QUERY rounds the three friends' cores used 9-18 of their 600 frames, the host's own core all 577 left (its next QUERY answered `expired`, `recovery_required`), the route stopped the battle (`execution_interrupted`; the host's launcher: "Battle recovery could not finish..."); this time **no launcher loop stalled over 1.5 s** (no `loop-stalls.jsonl`), so the delay was on the host core's own delivery path (the host's route to its own companion, the companion's bridge outbox, the game's bridge), not a blocked loop. The route records no exception detail (`battle_execution._drive` turns any exception into `execution_interrupted`). Next: per-hop timestamps on that path and the route's exception in the room record. Free-for-all rematches to the end: abandon-exit-2 passed (1031 s), abandon-exit-1 and win-ffa-exit-1 interrupted. **2026-10-20, the owner: release-blocking until the mandatory release tests pass**; the 1v1v1 runs of Update 32 (ffa3-1, lobby-1) did not hit it **Fixed 2026-10-21** (engine `b36b9462`): the core gave up after 600 frames (10 s) without a command, shorter than the route's own 20 s wait for each answer, so one command held up for 10-20 s ended a healthy battle. On the PC (and the test ROM) a held core now gives up 1200 frames after its deadline (`MULTIPLAYER_BATTLE_GRACE`, 30 s); the deadline that QUERY reports, so the route's KEEP pace, is unchanged (routes of every version read the same values). A command held up longer than the route's 20 s still stops the battle, through the route, which writes its diagnosis. Evidence: L, the two-game Players-menu 1v1 with one QUERY to the host's own game held 12 s (`native_launcher_challenge_proof.py --delay-host-query 12`): old engine `a336593b` interrupted (ms17-delay-old-2: the held QUERY answered 12.05 s later with the core expired, `battle_recovery_required`), fixed engine passed (ms17-delay-new-1: answered with 0 frames left, the core still held, a KEEP, the battle to its end, both saved, a fresh Continue). App `tests/test_battle_execution.py`: a 720-frame delay on one core's path stops a battle without the grace and not with it; while players choose, no PC core is left with less than the route's wait. Test ROM: the grace test fails on the old line. The free-for-all rematch (the scenario of both recorded failures) on the fixed build (engine `d466f500`): 3 of 3 runs passed without an interruption (ms17-ffa-exit-5 to -7, the rematch 930-1,276 s; L). Still release-blocking until the mandatory release tests pass (the owner, 2026-10-20). **Recurred 2026-10-21** (newcomers-mixed4-1, L, app working tree before this fix, the machine loaded by low-priority checks beside the four games): a 1v1v1 interrupted 12 minutes in (`execution_interrupted`, the route's `TimeoutError`). This time the watchdog named the code: the host's event loop (world server, battle router and the host's own companion) stalled 1.7-4.8 s four times in `team_desk.refresh` < `_team_roster` < `store.read_checkpoint` - every world broadcast checked each connected player's lease by reading and re-hashing that player's whole checkpoint under `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, which waits up to the connection's 5 s busy timeout while another connection writes; each presence message triggers a broadcast, so four players cost 16 whole reads per message (L, four 128 KB checkpoints: 9.3 ms of the loop per broadcast, 0.44 ms with the fix). During one stall the host's own companion's `execute` timed out (5 s) after the host had handed it the next command (1209); the companion's battle loop ended on that `TimeoutError` and never polled again, so the route's 20 s wait for the answer ran out. newcomers-lobby4-1 (the same old app, started before the fix) was interrupted the same way in its 1v1v1v1 (44 stalls, most in the checkpoint hash, one of 2.7 s in a secret-bases read on a loaded disk). **Fixed (app)**: the roster checks the lease alone in a read-only snapshot (`CheckpointStore.lease_is_current`, `BEGIN DEFERRED`: in WAL mode it never waits for a writer; no checkpoint bytes read or hashed; the rival status every player polls every 1.5 s, Championships and battle waiting and history check the lease the same way), and a late `execute` reply is asked again with the same response for up to 60 s (`battle_session.EXECUTE_LATE_SECONDS`; the host takes the byte-identical repeat as inert and hands the outstanding command again). Tests: `tests/test_storage.py` (the lease check returns while another connection holds a write; `read_checkpoint` waits and fails), `tests/test_battle_execution.py` (four games, replies lost at three commands each: the battle goes on - it ended with the `TimeoutError` before the fix; replies that never come still end the loop; the roster reads no checkpoint - it did before). Still release-blocking until the mandatory release tests pass. **Verified 2026-10-21 (the release tests)**: on the fixed app MS-17's scenario passed runs 8 (app `a90c191`) and 9 (`dafc493`, MS-46's fix, the last change to `src/`; beside the gate 3 queue, eight games on the four cores, no host loop stall over 1.5 s), newcomers-mixed4-2 and newcomers-busy4-3 passed (`a90c191`), the gate 3 proofs in real games passed with no healthy battle interrupted (TEST_LEDGER: 1v1, 2v2 with small and with full parties, 1v1v1, 1v1v1v1 and co-op battles, trades, lobbies, three and four players, abandon and recovery; the battles a proof interrupts on purpose were abandoned and played again), and the four multiplayer-check jobs of windows-package 39610329221 passed on the Windows runner (one machine, loopback): no healthy battle was interrupted. **Closed** as a release blocker; if it recurs, the loop-stall watchdog and the route's diagnosis name the code (TEST_LEDGER, the final master update) |
| MS-18 | Info | After an abandoned battle every restarted game comes back on a new world session, and a team ends when a member's connection changes (`team_desk.py`, unchanged), so 2v2 partners form their team again (seen in abandon-multi-1/-2). | By design; documented |

## Battle, trade and lag repair (2026-10-20, app `claude/admiring-bohr-dpxzuy`, engine `claude/mp-battle-trade-engine`; `docs/multiplayer-battle-trade-repair-20261020.md`)

The owner's report from two Windows PCs: both games stayed on "Waiting for server..." after both chose Enter battle; a trade stalled; lag spikes; the 3D battle background froze. Environments: **L** cloud Linux (real launcher, companions, host and native games), **CI** the Windows runner, **PC** the owner's PCs. Nothing here ran on PC hardware.

| ID | Severity | Issue | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MS-19 | High | Enter battle latches the game's PLAYERS window (no input, no B, "Waiting for server...") until the companion's ADMIT. Any check the launcher or companion refused between the Enter and ADMIT (a save failure that stays set, a leftover trade or export record, a changed binding or revision) sent nothing to the game, so the window waited for good, with no message and no log line. Both PCs show it when both companions refuse. Reproduced on the deployed code (L, repro-ch-failed-1). | **Fixed** (engine `1afd495c`: PC-only release screen, flags 3, accepted only for the very Enter while latched and no battle hold exists; app `af77426`: every check runs before anything is written, a refusal releases the window with the reason). L: fixed-ch-failed-2 passed (the friend's window released with "Saving stopped: restart game", the friend cancelled, the host's launcher cancelled its own entry, both free). Regression test: `tests/test_menu_session.py` `BothPlayersEnterTests` (both players waiting at once: both battle, both refused and released, or one refused and the other ended unstarted). Not run on PC |
| MS-20 | High | The same refusals were turned into a session-wide save failure (`_save_failed`), so saving stopped and every later Enter and trade of that player was refused the same silent way until the game restarted. | **Fixed** (app `af77426`: a refused Enter is `BattleAdmissionRefused`, never a save failure) |
| MS-21 | High | A challenge that ended before its battle bound (its 180 s limit after the last acceptance, once one player had entered) left the entered player frozen with no window: the host's execution room answered "waiting" forever, and the companion sent ADMIT even after the host definitely refused the claim. Reproduced (L, repro-ch-late-1: both games ended frozen). | **Fixed** (app `9b14cb3`: the waiting core is told `recovery_required`, the launcher cancels its never-started entry neutrally; `af77426`: a definite claim refusal sends no ADMIT on games with the release). L: win-late-1 passed (the friend entered 200 s after the host: the friend's window released with "Challenge expired: try again", the host's launcher cancelled its own entry, both free on the field) |
| MS-22 | Medium | A save by either player after accepting changes the checkpoint the challenge pinned; the host ends it (`canonical_changed`) and it vanished from both menus without a word (L, repro-ch-save-5). Autosave (every 5 minutes on the field) can do this to a player who leaves PLAYERS after accepting. | **Fixed (message)** (app `af77426`: "Cancelled: a party was saved"; the challenge still ends, by design: its pinned parties changed) |
| MS-23 | High | Trade failure exits: a refused open or decline closed the menu without a reason; a failure after the trade window opened (a stale offer at Confirm, a lost reply, the window's time limit) left the field frozen and saving disabled until a restart; a failure before the game's hold left the proposal open for the other player. The happy path worked (L, repro-trade-1). The host's commit is one SQLite transaction and was not changed. | **Fixed** (app `91760bd`: a failed trade is settled in the same session by the host's answer, applied or aborted, so both players end with the same outcome; one that never held cancels its proposal; refusals are shown). Simulated tests (`tests/test_trade_settlement.py`); the failure exits were **not** driven with real games |
| MS-24 | Medium | After both players confirmed a trade, both fields stayed frozen 35-40 s while the received save went to the game as 1024 WRITE commands of 128 bytes (one round trip each). | **Fixed** (engine `c76651ea`, app `4c96fe2`: the PC game takes a 4 KiB sector per command, WRITE_WINDOW). Same instrumentation, two real games (L): old game 1024 WRITEs, p50 32 ms, applied 38 s after the second confirmation (base-trade-perf-1); new game 32 sectors, p50 32 ms, about 5 s (win-trade-perf-1). The GBA ROM is unchanged |
| MS-25 | Medium | 3D (Diorama) battles: the water, flowers and sea air stood still for the rest of a battle after a few slow frames (a save, a hitch, a window drag): the redraw step went past its limit, became 0 and was never measured again. Classic 2D has no ambient battle background animation (`battle_bg.c` creates no task), so nothing there can freeze. | **Fixed** (engine `be8b28ab`: standing water is tried again, 2 s doubling to 64 s; `tests/diorama/test_battle_tile_pace.c`). L, the same hitches on both builds: the old game stood still for the rest of the battle (8-9 tile redraws, tiles-old-4), the fixed game came back to every change (405, tiles-new-1). Software OpenGL, not a GPU |
| MS-26 | Info (measured) | Save checks run on the launcher's and host's event loops: `inspect_flash` about 12 ms and ownership about 5 ms per call on the cloud computer, several per trade (confirm, prepare, apply). In the proof harness, where all launchers share one Python process, they showed as 100-300 ms loop blocks (GIL contention); on PCs each launcher is its own process. A battle step journals with fsync (about 150 a second per launcher in a 1v1); the Windows runner measured these writes at 0.47 ms and 0.13 ms (`m25-bench`), so they are not a demonstrated Windows lag cause. | Open, not changed: measured, no evidence of a player-visible cost on PCs; a slow disk or heavy antivirus PC was not measured |
| MS-27 | Low (proof only) | The challenge proof decided "battle running" from the hold flag, which reads ADMITTING (2) only until BIND; a battle that bound before the proof's first look read hold 0 and the proof reported a stuck challenge although both games were in the battle (win-enter-perf-1). | **Fixed in the proof** (it also reads the battle mailbox's state); win-enter-perf-2 passed |

## Update 32 features (2026-10-20, app `claude/admiring-bohr-dpxzuy`, engine `claude/feature-update-engine`; `docs/update-32-features-20261020.md`)

Environments as above: **L** cloud Linux. Nothing of Update 32 ran on Windows or a PC yet. MS-30 and MS-31 are older
bugs that Update 32's tests found.

| ID | Severity | Issue | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MS-28 | Medium (**fixed** 2026-11-04, app `9eb0360`; mixed versions only) | The world's build id is the game data (the unchanged GBA ROM hash), so launchers of different versions can meet in one world, and a host ends the connection of a player who sends a request type it does not know. A newer player with an older host is disconnected when PLAYERS asks for the battle lobbies (Update 32), as it already was for every earlier new request type (friendship, teams); an older player cannot read a 1v1v1 challenge sent to them. | **Fixed** 2026-11-04 (app `9eb0360`; the owner's choice "Same version required"): protocol 4. Every hello says the launcher's own version ("launcher": the package's release label, e.g. "Native 3D CI 265", the one at the top of the main menu; "development" from source), and a world admits only its host's exact version. Another version is refused at the hello ("launcher_mismatch"; the troubleshooting line names both versions), an older launcher's protocol-3 hello is refused with "unsupported_protocol" (every launcher since 2026-10-18 shows that as "Different game versions"), and an older host refuses the new hello's extra field ("invalid_fields", which the new launcher also shows as different versions). The invite-code check finds it before anything starts, the join stops at once instead of retrying, and nobody is disconnected later by a request the other version does not know. The texts and the guides (HOSTING, QUICK-START, RELEASE-LIMITS) say the host and every player need exactly the same EmeraldTogether version. So every package is its own version: when the host installs a new one, every player installs it too. Tested on this Linux machine only (unit tests; real processes for old host + new player, new host + old player with app `ebecf82`, and same versions); not between two PCs, not on Windows. |
| MS-29 | Low (open; the player is told to try again) | In soft-reset-5 the host's game did not open PLAYERS for START > PLAYER > RESET (the cursor was on RESET; the game logged nothing), so the proof timed out. It did not happen in runs 6 and 7; the reason was not found. **Recurred 2026-10-21** in qm-softreset-1 (L, the gate 3 queue, six games on the four cores): the game logged `soft reset: Players refused (connected 1 field 0 enrolled 1 idle 1 menu 0 locked 0 script 0 fade 0 step 0)` - the field check refused while the menu, controls, script, fade and step were clear - and the player saw "PLAYERS is not ready yet. Try again in a moment." **Cause found**: the proof had tapped DOWN to turn the player away from the table and then pressed START. A turn in place sets `gPlayerAvatar.runningState` to TURN_DIRECTION until the next field step with no direction; START within the turn's frames opens the START menu before that step, so the state stays TURN_DIRECTION while the menu is open, and the multiplayer field check (`FieldReadyWithMenu`, `src/multiplayer_save.c`) requires it to be 0. Shown in a real game process (the Classic 2D test build from a Route 101 save, frame-exact keys, gdb on the running game): DOWN held for frames 90-92 and START at frame 93 or 97: the START menu open with `runningState` 1; START at frame 101 or 105, or 60 frames later: 0. So START within about 8-10 frames (0.15 s) of a turn; under load the proof's 20 frames of wall-clock wait were fewer game frames. START > PLAYER > PLAYERS refuses the same way (`MultiplayerMenu_Open`, the same check); the START menu has already closed and the next field step clears the turn, so opening the menu again works; nothing is lost or held. | Open (a later update; it changes the shared field check that the saves, battles and menus use, so it needs the release tests again): accept a finished turn in place (no step in progress) as ready, or clear the turn when the START menu opens (PC only). The refusal names its conditions (engine `91d856fb`); the game's message tells the player to try again. |
| MS-30 | High (older than Update 32: the trade's post-image and the battle's counter check are both older) | A battle right after a trade, before either player saved again, never started: both launchers held it as an interrupted battle (`battle_recovery_held`, saving stopped) and the games stayed on the battle's waiting screen. The trade's canonical post-image keeps the save counter of the frozen export (it is byte-preserving), the game writes it one counter higher, and the game's PREPARE refuses a preparation naming another counter (ORDER; the party CRC matched). Found by the three-player proof (three-3), reproduced with two games (trade-battle-1). | **Fixed** (engine `62c1711a`, PC only: after it imports a canonical image the game accepts that image's counter for a battle preparation until it saves again; the trainer id and party CRC checks are unchanged). L: trade-battle-2 passed (the trade, the 1v1 right after it, both saved, a fresh Continue; 30 checks). The GBA ROM is unchanged |
| MS-31 | Medium (older than Update 32; the release GBA ROM keeps it) | Breeding: a level-up move both parents know was inherited only if it was the baby's first learnset move; the loop advanced the wrong counter and read past its four-move array. | **Fixed on the PC** (engine `af9b33dd`; the GBA ROM keeps the upstream line, so its hash is unchanged). `--selftest-breeding`: two level-30 Bulbasaur, every Egg knows Razor Leaf, Sweet Scent, Growth and Double-Edge (357/357; with the old loop 0/357) |

## Final master update (2026-10-21, app `claude/admiring-bohr-dpxzuy`, engine `claude/feature-update-engine`; `docs/final-master-update-20261021.md`)

Environments as above: **L** cloud Linux, **CI** GitHub's Linux runners (the `gameplay-check` workflow: the Classic 2D
test build, the plan runner of the battle test driver, test builds only). Nothing here ran on Windows or a PC.

| ID | Severity | Issue | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MS-43 | High (PC only; older than this update) | Entering the Battle Dome lobby (Battle Frontier) stopped the PC game (SIGSEGV) in about 7 entries of 10. The lobby's resume script simulates the previous tourney (`InitRandomTourneyTreeResults`); its matches' type matchups (`CalcPartyMonTypeEffectivenessMultiplier`) reach a battler's gimmick (`GetActiveGimmick`) through the Ground-against-Flying check, and that reads the battle's state, which is freed outside a battle. Whether a match puts a Ground move against a Flying Pokemon is random, so not every entry stopped. A Dome challenge simulates the other matches between its rounds the same way (`ResolveDomeRoundWinners`, read from the code; not run). The GBA ROM reads open bus there and does not stop. Found by the gameplay check (CI run 39541530570: five targets of the lobby stopped the game; L, gdb: `GetActiveGimmick` < `GetBattlerTypes` < `IsBattlerGroundedInverseCheck` < `MulByTypeEffectiveness` < `CalcPartyMonTypeEffectivenessMultiplier` < `GetWinningMove` < `DecideRoundWinners` < `InitRandomTourneyTreeResults` < `RunOnResumeMapScript`). | **Fixed** (engine, PC and test ROM: outside a battle no Pokemon has a gimmick; the GBA ROM is unchanged, sha256 `f214735b...3387`). L, the lobby's 10 targets: 3 passed and 7 stopped the game before (dome-2), 10 passed after (dome-3) |
| MS-44 | Low (PC only; older than this update) | The end credits stopped the PC game (SIGFPE) when the save had no Pokemon registered as caught: the credits pick their Pokemon slides at random among the caught ones (`DeterminePokemonToShow`, `Random() % numCaughtMon`), a division by zero that traps on PC (the GBA ROM does not trap). Every way the game gives a Pokemon registers it as caught (`GiveMonToPlayer`, a catch, a hatch, a trade, the multiplayer trade effects), so a player reaches it only with a save whose caught record is empty. Found by the gameplay check, whose test party skips that registration (L, gdb: `DeterminePokemonToShow` < `CB2_StartCreditsSequence`, after the Champion's battle and the Hall of Fame). | **Fixed** (engine `512bed8e`, PC and test ROM: with no Pokemon caught the slides show the starter, as with one; the GBA ROM is unchanged, sha256 `f214735b...3387`). L: the Champion's room target now runs through the battle, the Hall of Fame, the credits and the reset to the title (failed-8; it stopped the game in failed-7) |
| MS-45 | Medium (app; from QF-4's soft reset) | After PLAYER > RESET was opened and not carried out (refused, for example "Finish the trade first", or left), the next START > PLAYER > PLAYERS opened on the soft reset page again instead of the Players home: a plain open asked the menu to redraw its current page, and RESET's page (`PlayerMenu.open_page`) stayed current. That page has no row back to the home, so the roster, challenges and trades were out of reach until the menu's binding changed (a reconnect, a restart). Found by the newcomers proof (`native_launcher_newcomers_proof.py --scenario busy`, newcomers-busy4-1, L, 2026-10-21): friend 3's RESET was refused during a trade offer, then PLAYERS showed the reset page. | **Fixed** (app): a plain open (`MenuRequest` kind 1) asks `PlayerMenu.open()`, which leaves the reset pages for the Players home; RESET still opens its own page. Tests: `tests/test_soft_reset.py` (`test_a_plain_open_asks_the_menu_for_its_home`, `test_players_opens_on_its_home_after_reset_was_used`). L: see TEST_LEDGER (newcomers-busy4-2 on the fixed app) |
| MS-46 | Low (app; latency, no failure seen) | Each game launch or relaunch hashed the game and the ROM on the launcher's event loop, twice (on opening, and again just before the spawn): about 220 MB in the Linux proofs (the Classic game and the ROM, each twice; a Windows package also lists the 3D game), 2-3 s on a loaded PC. Everything else that launcher does waited meanwhile - its world connection, and on the host the world server and the battle router for everyone else. Found by the loop-stall watchdog in newcomers-busy4-3 (L, 2026-10-21): friend 3's launcher held 3.05 s and 2.18 s while its game restarted after a soft reset (`PlayerRuntime.open` > `RuntimeBundle.verify`). Nothing failed: since MS-17's fix a late reply is asked again, and the player relaunching is not in a battle. | **Fixed** (app): the four hashes run in a worker thread (`asyncio.to_thread`), the same checks in the same order. Test: `tests/test_player_runtime.py` (`test_the_game_and_its_rom_are_hashed_off_the_event_loop`, fails on the old code). L: the gate 3 proofs run on it (TEST_LEDGER) |
