Reproduced on Linux (ffa-exit-diag-1) and then deterministically (double-2: a level-30 Earthquake knocks out the host's and Friend 2's level-2 Pokemon on turn 1). **Root cause**: the battle's master (the host's game) sends every battler's action menu (`CONTROLLER_CHOOSEACTION`) with battler 0's action of the turn (`battle_main.c`: `gChosenActionByBattler[0]`, as the original link battles do). Once battler 0 - the host's - is out of Pokemon while the battle goes on, that action is `B_ACTION_NOTHING_FAINTED` (13), and the native core's check of a master's command (`multiplayer_battle_controller.c` `MasterDataValid`) accepted only actions up to Run and `B_ACTION_NONE`: the host's game refused its own next block (`MultiplayerBattle_SendBlock`, gdb backtrace in double-2: `Task_HandleSendLinkBuffersData` > `SendBlock` > `MultiplayerBattle_SendBlock` > `FailNative`, the block `00 01 03 03 08 00 09 00 11 0d 00 00`), went to `recovery required`, and the host's route stopped the battle (`native query differs from the held execution`) for everyone. It comes on about half the turns after the host is out while the battle goes on (each turn of a double battle the game decides at random, `AI_REVERSE_BATTLER_LOGIC_ORDER_CHANCE` 50%, whether the other battlers' menus are settled before battler 0 is marked out; otherwise they carry `B_ACTION_NONE`, which was always accepted), which is why it came and went. It comes whenever the host is knocked out before the end and the battle goes on: a free-for-all (where it was found), and the same path in a 2v2 or co-op battle. The same signature as MS-17's two free-for-all recurrences (abandon-exit-1, win-ffa-exit-1), which the grace could not prevent. | **Fixed** (engine, PC and test builds only: `#if defined(PORTABLE) \|\| TESTING`, the GBA line kept at its number, the ROM unchanged): the check accepts `B_ACTION_NOTHING_FAINTED` in an action menu (still no item with it); every core runs the same check, so the master sends it and the others take it. Test ROM: `test/multiplayer_battle_controller.c` (an action menu with each action: the six it can carry pass, the others and an item without USE_ITEM refuse, the master's alone). The engine fix alone was not enough (L knockout-fixed-1, the host knocked out on turn 1): the host's game now sent the block (`00 01 02 03 08 00 05 02 11 0d 00 00`) and the host app's own copy of the check (`battle_controller.py` `_master_valid`, which mirrors `MasterDataValid` for every block it routes) refused it (`native controller violates its source, payload or pending-request role`), so the route stopped the battle the same way; **fixed the same** (app): `tests/test_battle_controller.py` (that block accepted in a free-for-all and its owner's reply after it; an item with it and the actions it cannot carry refused). The pre-fix build hit it again on its own in L ffa-exit-gdb-5's fourth rematch (the host and Friend 2 out: `00 01 03 03 08 00 05 02 11 0d`). **With both halves (L knockout-fixed-2)** the host's game sent two action menus with battler 0 out (`00 02 01 03 08 00 03 02 11 0d`, `00 03 01 03 ...`), the host app took them, and the free-for-all went on to its result (293 s, Friend 1's level-30 Mudkip won); everyone back with their parties, the saves, the store's integrity check and a fresh Continue passed. The proof's knockout scenarios now also require the host's route journal to show such menus (`ffa-knockout`, and `multi-knockout` for the 2v2). **The 2v2 (L knockout-2v2-1)**: Friend 1's Earthquake knocked out its own partner's - the host's - Pokemon on turn 1, the host's game sent 4 action menus with battler 0 out, and the 2v2 went on to its result (253 s); everything after it passed. The proof's diagnosis (app `fa6e601`) and the route journal decoded the cause. The proof's 2v2 check failed three times on Windows (runs 145 and 147) with no fault in the game: twice the host was battler 2, where its game is not the master (a 2v2 orders each team by player id), and once a short battle drew the turn order that keeps the value out of the menus; the proof now pairs the host so that it is battler 0, and every other trainer lasts several turns (TEST_LEDGER). |
| MS-58 | Low (cosmetic; the rival checkpoint page, four players) | A 2v2 rival match's row cut its two teams as single strings and kept one trainer a side ("Battle: Rival T v Rival H"; L rival-4p-1, the first real-game run of the four-player format). | **Fixed** (app `a16fdcf`): `rival_menu._versus` takes the teams and cuts each name within its team ("Kev&Sam v Alex&Jo"); `tests/test_rival_menu.py`. |
| MS-59 | None (**closed: not a defect** - the gdb instrument, not the game) | Seen with gdb on every game of the free-for-all rematches (ffa-exit-gdb-1 to -5, L): a breakpoint on `MultiplayerBattle_FailNative` stopped twice in `FinishNativeReturn` at the end of every free-for-all on all four games, which read as the field-return check failing. It does not fail: `MultiplayerBattle_FailNative` is inlined, so the breakpoint has seven locations, and the one in `MultiplayerBattle_Tick` (`Tick+494`) is the first address of the inlined copy's code, which the compiler shares with the normal "field not back yet" path (every `FieldReturned()` test after the fade jumps there). The real failure store is at `Tick+1494`, not a breakpoint location. The host's route journals of all eight free-for-alls that ended under gdb show each game's QUERY with `restored` (flag 32, set only on `FinishNativeReturn`'s success path, after the party count and CRC match the preparation) and then RELEASE; the party dumps at the stops matched the backup byte for byte. The breakpoint behind MS-57 (`MultiplayerBattle_SendBlock+664`) is a block reached only from the refusal, and that route journal showed the failure on its own. | Closed, nothing to change in the game. The gdb script for later runs watches writes of `recovery required` to the battle state (a hardware watchpoint) instead of breaking on the inlined function. |
| MS-60 | High (multiplayer: a native battle entered by a game with progress its host had not saved never started; both trainers were held with saving stopped, and the abandonment that freed them lost the unsaved progress); **fixed (engine and app), verified on Linux and on Windows in real game processes** | Found by L tower-join-2 (2026-10-22, the Tower's co-op progression): after a Duo floor whose co-op experience award levelled player 1's three Mewtwo (10 -> 11, in memory, not yet saved), the next Duo floor never started. The host prepares every native battle from each player's last host save (its save counter, trainer id and party CRC), and the engine's `PREPARE` (`multiplayer_battle.c`) refuses a preparation that is not the game's own state (`MP_BATTLE_ORDER`): the route reported `native_refused` and the room went to `recovery_required`. The general case was confirmed in real games by L ms60-pvp-2 (2026-10-22, a scratch experiment): player 2's lead lost 1 HP in its game's memory (what a wild battle leaves) and an ordinary 1v1 was challenged, accepted and entered: both launchers went to `battle_recovery_held` (saving stopped) and offered Abandon battle; the abandonment started player 2's game again from its host save and the change was gone (lead HP 20 in memory, 21 after). Any progress the host had not saved led there: a wild battle, an item used, a Pokemon caught, a co-op award, or the game's own START > SAVE (it saves only in the game). High, as a loss of the player's progress (like MS-30). | **Fixed** (engine `547d0789`, app `cff44fe` and `4328509`): the game's save mailbox answers `QUERY_PARTY` (PC only: its save counter, the counter `PREPARE` also takes right after a trade's import, its trainer id, party count and party CRC - `PREPARE`'s own inputs; manifest `save_party_query_abi_version` 1). The launcher names the answer in a trainer's first Enter claim when the host says it checks it (`pending` with `live_check_version`), and the host turns a game that is not the save the challenge names away before anything is recorded or held (`save_needed`): the challenge ends for everyone with no result, the refused game keeps its progress, its Players menu says "Not saved: close menu to save" and its launcher saves it as soon as the menu is closed; the other trainer's menu says "Cancelled: a trainer must save" (also when its own Enter comes after the challenge ended), and a trainer who had already entered has its entry cancelled neutrally by its launcher (also when the challenge ends between its two claims or before its first poll of the battle: `4328509`). A game with QUERY_PARTY that could not answer is refused before any claim ("Your game is busy: try again"), never claimed unchecked; an older game or host goes as before. A Tower Duo battle refused this way is void for both (nothing paid or lost). The Tower Duo's save gate (app `5034e60`) stays: its page says "Save first: launcher Save now" (it said "START > SAVE", which saves only in the game and never lets the Duo go on). Tests: `tests/test_party_identity_check.py` (the answer, the protocol, the host's check and cancel, the launcher's claim, its save after the menu closes, the manifest marker), `test_battle_recovery_session.py`, `test_battle_execution.py`, `test_player_menu.py`, `test_tower_menu.py`, `test_windows_workflow.py`; the engine's `tools/pc_port/tests/test_mp_bridge.c` (4,479 checks, 0 failed). **Verified on Linux in real game processes** (L stale-save-6, `scripts/multiplayer/native_launcher_stale_save_proof.py`, key presses only): the friend saved in the game only (save counter 3 -> 4, its host save at 3), entered first and was refused; the host's Enter after it was refused with the reason; nobody held anything, both launchers could save, the friend's game kept its progress and its launcher saved it 1.0 s after the menus closed (host save counter 5 = the game's); again with the host entering first (its entry cancelled neutrally); then the same two battled 1v1 to the end, saved, Continued, and the store's integrity check passed. stale-save-4 and -5 failed in their second round on `cff44fe` (its own refusal of the battle session's claim after ADMIT), fixed in `4328509`. **Verified on Windows** (GitHub's hosted runner, Windows game processes; not a player's PC): the interrupted-battle check's `stale-save` entry passed both attempts in run 158 (app `46e46ee`, engine `547d0789`; the launcher's save 1.5 s after the menus closed). |
| MS-62 | High (multiplayer: battles between two PCs over the internet paused 30-60 s between actions, unplayable); **improved (engine and app), measured on Linux with simulated internet delay; not yet tried on two real PCs** | The owner's reports (2026-10-22 and -23, two real PCs, installer run 181: "still tremendously slow", "30 to a minute pause between every thing"). Every link block of a native battle (a short starter battle has about 375) cost the remote player several round trips over the network, one after another: the host asked its core with QUERY until a block appeared, read it with READ_OUT, delivered it with RECEIVE and acknowledged it with ACK_OUT, and every core waited for the slowest player's round trip on every step. Each command also wrote and flushed several files on both PCs (the host's route journal and room record, the launcher's own record), which costs little on Linux and the CI runners and more on a player's Windows PC. Measured on Linux (`native_launcher_newcomers_proof.py --scenario lobby --no-save --latency-ms 50 --remote-only --disk-ms 8`: the friend 50 ms away each way, 8 ms on every fsync): base-real-3, the router of installer run 181 (app `138029a`): the Rival 1v1 took 365 s (2,514 host commands, 145 ms each). Without delay the same battle takes about 72 s. | **Improved** (engine `09dd6ac6` and `42c2f4a7`, app `2237839`): the PC game's battle PEEK (opcode 25: QUERY plus the outbound block's bytes, changing nothing; ACK_OUT then needs no READ_OUT; manifest `battle_peek_abi_version` 1, bridge.txt `battle_peek=1`). Each launcher looks at its own core every few milliseconds while it holds no host command and reports what changed at once (`battle_observer.py`, the `observe` battle operation); the host's observed router (`battle_execution._NativeRoute.run_observed`, route journal v4) answers every response and observation the moment it arrives, in journal order (recovery replays the same decisions), delivers each block as soon as it exists and lets two cores take a new block while another is in flight (three or four keep the batch rule). Fewer disk syncs: transport replies and observations are made durable by the next command's fsync, the room record is not rewritten for transport commands, the launcher does not record a transport answer again; a held execute poll is woken when its command is published (it polled every 2 ms, about 16 ms on Windows). Tests: `tests/test_battle_execution.py` (`ObservedExecutionTests`: every network test on the observed route), `test_battle_recovery.py` (`ObservedRecoveryTests`: every interrupted battle recovered from a v4 journal), `test_battle_throughput.py`, `test_battle_protocol.py`; the engine's `test_mp_bridge.c` (4,488 checks, 0 failed: PEEK needs `battle_peek=1`; a first real-game run, peek-real-1, found the bridge refusing PEEK as an admission cleanup command, fixed); the GBA ROM unchanged (f214735b...3387). **Measured on Linux in real game processes** (peek-real-2, same conditions as base-real-3): the Rival 1v1 took **118 s** (796 host commands and 664 observations), about three times faster, and the whole proof passed (back on the field, closing saves, a fresh Continue, the store's integrity check). Still open: the remaining overhead (about 46 s a battle here), real-PC confirmation by the owner. **2v2: RECEIVE_MANY** (app `10082d6`, engine `c67a1a67`: a core's waiting small blocks in one command) measured on Linux in real games (2026-10-23, a starter 2v2; many-lobby2v2-1 and -lag-1 against lobby2v2-1 and -lag-1): fewer host commands (5,736 to 4,691 without delay, 4,752 to 4,475 over a 50 ms connection) but **no shorter battle** (318.8 s to 325.4 s; 482.5 s to 501.0 s, one run each): a core seldom has more than one small block waiting, and a command is answered in about 35 ms even without delay (the games' own pace), so a 2v2 takes the games' pace plus a round trip per exchange. Kept (harmless; journal v5). Windows: the launcher asks for a 1 ms timer (app `0188d23`; Windows otherwise rounds the battle loops' few-millisecond waits up to 15.6 ms), not yet measured on a player's PC. Still open for both formats: the games' per-command pace. **Battle speed: DELIVER** (2026-10-24/25; engine `58123840` on `claude/battle-speed-engine`, app `fa80016` on `claude/battle-speed`; manifest `battle_deliver_abi_version` 1, bridge.txt `battle_deliver=1`, route journal v6; every run at normal battle speed, PKMN_BATTLE_SPEED=1). Where the time still went on the observed router (50 ms each way, 8 ms fsyncs, the games at 1x): a block's core could send its next block only after every other core had received it (its ACK_OUT followed the last RECEIVE), so the master waited a round trip to the others for each of its two blocks per controller command (the command and its own DONE); each core took one command a round trip, about 3.5 per controller command in a 1v1 (a RECEIVE of each of the other core's blocks, then its own block's ACK_OUT) and 4.4 in a 2v2, each 150-250 ms to a remote game on a quiet machine (100 ms of network, the launcher's record with its two fsyncs, the host journal's fsync, the game's next frame); and 180-800 launcher reports a battle crossed a command, were refused and were sent again. A 2v2 controller command took 1.6 s (median, quiet machine), 3.6 s on a loaded one. The delivering router (`battle_execution._NativeRoute`, `_next_delivered`): acknowledges a block as soon as the host holds it (its bytes are in the journal before the acknowledgement's command is fsynced and published), so the core goes on at once; gives each core one DELIVER a round trip with its own block's acknowledgement and every block it is owed that fits, in one global order every core receives the blocks in; holds the other cores' replies and DONEs for a slave until the master's next command (the first thing that needs them there) and acknowledges a slave's DONE with that delivery while it owes nothing else; holds nothing back after the master's end command or for a core whose deadline runs low (it gets everything instead of a KEEP); and takes a launcher's report while a command to it is outstanding (one made after the command ran is applied right after the command's answer). Every decision uses only journaled events in journal order, so recovery replays the same commands (`battle_replay.py`; `test_battle_recovery.py` DeliverRecoveryTests: every interrupted battle recovered from a v6 journal, and a DELIVER cut off before or after its core ran it, 1v1 and 2v2). The PC game: DELIVER (opcode 27: ACK_OUT's block fields and payload head, then RECEIVE_MANY-style whole small blocks, several from one core in its order; consumed only when its acknowledgement or a block was applied; controller blocks drained into the link buffer one at a time to keep the order; answer: acknowledged, applied, refusal), and the link send and receive tasks lose no frame between native blocks (PC only, `#line` gba-lines; the GBA ROM unchanged, f214735b...3387). Mismatched versions refuse cleanly: a game without `gMultiplayerBattleDeliverAbiVersion` gets no `battle_deliver=1` and the host keeps the v4/v5 routers; a bridge without `battle_deliver=1` refuses opcode 27; a battle recovered from its journal routes as its journal did. **Measured on Linux in real games with simulated delay only** (TEST_LEDGER "Battle speed: DELIVER": old and new interleaved, all ten runs passed end to end): 2v2 on a quiet machine **388 s to 237 s and 246 s** (a controller command 1,567 ms to 796-929 ms, median; 893 s for the old code on a loaded machine); 1v1 on a quiet machine **118 s to 76 s** (864 ms to 564 ms). Load-independent: host commands per controller command 17.2 to 8.2 (2v2) and 7.0 to 5.0 (1v1); to each slave core 4.4 to 1.5 and 3.5 to 1.6; refused reports to 0; launcher-side fsyncs halved in the 2v2. The interrupted-battle proofs passed on v6 (`native_launcher_abandon_proof.py` multi-network: a 2v2 with a lost connection, abandoned, then played again to its result; pairs-crash: a game killed during a 1v1 while another 1v1 finished); the app's 30 battle test files 513 passed, 20 skipped; the engine's test_mp_bridge 4,538 checks. Still open: the master core still takes about 3.8 commands per controller command (its command's acknowledgement, its DONE's, the others' DONEs), each a round trip when the master is a remote player; the launcher writes its record (two fsyncs) before every command; the game answers a command at its next frame; not yet tried on two real PCs. **Step 10 (2026-10-29, the owner's decision: flush about every other turn, in the background, players never waiting):** the host's route journal and each launcher's record no longer fsync before every transport command (nearly all of a battle's commands); they are written first (a crash of a game, a launcher or the host's process loses nothing) and made durable in a background thread at most every 10 s, and by every lifecycle command and state change, still fsynced before they go out (`battle_execution_journal.py`, `battle_session.py`). Only a power cut or a system crash can lose the newest records; recovery then refuses (`battle_order`) and the battle is abandoned with no result. L, the newcomers proof's Rival Battle 1v1 with every fsync 20 ms slower (`--disk-ms 20`, as on a player's PC), one run each: 128.8 ms per command before, 96.8 ms after (the host's take 22.2 to 3.1 ms, the launchers' answer 57.1 to 26.0 ms); the battle's fsyncs about 3,190 (1,058 of the journal's, 2,132 of the launchers' records) to about 218, 50 of them in the background threads. Not tried on Windows or between two PCs. |
| MS-64 | High (multiplayer: a trade ended as soon as it began, the player back on the field with nothing said, and the launcher stopped saving); **fixed (app); reproduced and verified on Linux in real game processes (Classic 2D and 3D builds, simulated internet delay); not yet tried on two real PCs** | The owner's report (2026-10-23, two real PCs, 3D, installer run 181: "trading is also broken", "for trading it literally just kicks you out of the screen"). After "Trading" (or "Choose your Pokemon") the Players menu closes while the launcher prepares the party: it asks the host (three round trips), then the game for its ownership state and a fresh ID grant (QUERY_OWNERSHIP, GRANT_OWNERSHIP) and to freeze the party (BEGIN_TRADE_EXPORT). The game answers those only on a calm field (`multiplayer_save.c` FieldReady: not walking, no menu or fade), and otherwise refuses them as unsafe before changing anything. A player who walked on during those seconds got that refusal, and the launcher ended the trade at the first one (an ordinary save retries it later); its cancellation prepares the party too, was refused the same way, and the failure then stopped saving (save recovery required). The only message ("Trade did not start") waited for the next opening of the Players menu. Reproduced (`native_launcher_newcomers_proof.py --scenario trade --no-save --latency-ms 50 --remote-only --latency-all --disk-ms 8 --walk-while-preparing 6`: the trading player walks left and right once its menu closed): trade-walk-old-1, on the app as committed (`d5bf20e`, installer run 185): the friend's ownership query refused as unsafe in the preparation and again in the cancellation, the trade window never came back, the launcher stopped saving. Without the walk the same trades passed (trade-lag-1, battle-trade-1 after a rival 1v1, trade3d-lag-2 on the 3D build). | **Fixed** (app, this commit): a trade asks the game again every 0.1 s, for up to 15 s a command, while it answers QUERY_OWNERSHIP, GRANT_OWNERSHIP or BEGIN_TRADE_EXPORT busy or unsafe (`live_trade.CALM_OPCODES`, `LiveTradeCoordinator._command`), in the preparation and in a cancellation; a trade that does not start or is cancelled says so at once in the game's banner ("The trade did not start." / "The trade was cancelled."). Saves go faster over the internet: a checkpoint upload or download sends four 4 KiB chunks at a time instead of one per round trip (`Companion.checkpoint_pipeline`, `checkpoint_client.TRANSFER_WINDOW`; the host takes a connection's messages in order; every save, trade and battle checkpoint uses it), so the trade window comes back sooner. Tests: `tests/test_live_trade.py` (an unsafe field delays the preparation and the cancellation instead of ending them; one that never calms still ends with nothing held), `test_checkpoint_client.py` (`PipelineTests`: four chunks in flight, in order, from the live-save owner's task; a refused chunk or wrong acknowledgement ends the transfer without a commit), `test_trade_settlement.py` (the banners). **Verified on Linux in real game processes**: trade-walk-new-2, same run with the fix: the friend's game refused GRANT_OWNERSHIP as unsafe 50 times while it walked (6.4 s), the launcher waited, both trades applied (traded and traded back), the closing saves, a fresh Continue and the store's integrity check passed; trade3d-lag-2 (3D build, light settings, no walk): traded and traded back, the trade window back 3.5 s (host) and 4.1 s (friend) after the choice. Still open: confirmation on two real PCs. |


## 3D view, trades and Windows runs (2026-10-23, app `claude/admiring-bohr-dpxzuy`, engine `claude/feature-update-engine`)

Environments as above. The 3D pictures were taken on **L** in the 3D test build under Xvfb, drawn by Mesa's software
renderer: not a GPU and not a player's PC.

| ID | Severity | Issue | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MS-65 | Medium (3D view: the picture misled - rock read as floor, paths as spikes) | The owner's report (2026-10-23, screenshots of the 3D build): "mountain textures are all fucked up" (Route 112: rows of little stepped pyramids), a house beside two signposts with its roof ridge floating and its front wall twice (Route 116's rest house), and in Rusturf Tunnel "it looks like u can walk through some places when u cant" with the exit drawn like the rock round it. Causes: (1) the game tags its mountain paths MB_MOUNTAIN_TOP as well as its rock (the battle background's tag: on Route 112, 174 walkable cells against 919 blocked), and the 3D rules stood every such cell up as a one-cell cliff whose drawing was eroded into a pyramid of its own; (2) where the terrace solver puts a walk a level below the top, blocked cells kept the top level, so the rest house stood a tile up on its own cells (their sides drew its facade again, its sign twice) and trees stood on plinths; every hipped roof carried a box along its ridge; (3) the cave rock picker (`tools/battle_env/cave_pins.py`) read tile behaviours one value off from MB_INTERIOR_DEEP_WATER on and took only MB_NORMAL rock, so the rock carrying the cave's own MB_CAVE (Rusturf's rock tops, about 330 cells) lay flat, as did a cave's rock drawn with a metatile it also uses for floor (Granite Cave's sand), the map's border block past a cave's edge, and Rusturf's blocked ledges between its rock tiers (trenches through the rock); the exit drawn below an arrow warp stood as a block. | **Fixed** (engine `5285d3ed`, the PC 3D build only; the GBA ROM unchanged): a walkable MB_MOUNTAIN_TOP cell is ground and a blocked one a cell of solid rock with its drawing on its top and sides (a mountain tree a rounded crown); outdoors a blocked cell stands on the level of the ground nearest it (cliffs, mounds and walls keep theirs); no ridge box; the cave rock picker reads the behaviours right, takes rock with no behaviour, the cave's own or a one-way edge, and never the cell beyond an arrow warp (the exit is a gap in the wall); a cave stands up its blocked rock masses cell by cell, blocked ledges no player can jump (35 in Rusturf Tunnel, 10 in Victory Road 1F, 2 in Granite Cave 1F; jumpable ledges stay ledges) and its border block as the same rock. Tests: the engine's `tools/diorama_rules` (test_compile_rules, test_mountain_art, test_terrace_topology; new checks for walkable mountain ground, cave rock, the exit and the ledges) and `make check-diorama`. **Seen on L** in pictures of the 3D test build before and after (Routes 112, 116 and 117, Petalburg, Lavaridge, Oldale, Littleroot, Rusturf Tunnel, Granite Cave 1F and B1F, Meteor Falls, Victory Road 1F; Mt Chimney, Jagged Pass, Ever Grande, Mossdeep, Routes 104 and 110 and Rustboro looked over for changes); **not yet seen on a player's PC or a GPU**. |

Compared room by room, the 60 rooms the tools read with it: only the Sootopolis Gym 1F's reading would have changed - its floor is ice, which the right names count with the water that is no room's ground, so the dark tiles past its walls would have become its floor - so an ice floor counts as the room's floor, as it did by accident. In the passing runs the same closing save made revision 7, so no other save comes between. **Fixed** in the proof (app `f830795`): it clicks Save again while busy, for up to 30 s, and any other refusal still fails (`tests/test_launcher_proof_save.py`). For a player: a Save pressed in the last moment of a trade says it is not ready, and the next press saves. Diagnostics from app `6d9cc01` stay |

Not reproduced: trades over a slow connection passed on Linux (trade-lag-L1, trade3d-full-lag-2 between two 3D games with six Pokemon each, trade-rival-lag-2 between the rival saves) and on the Windows runner (run 196's `trade-lag`; run 194's traded too, MS-67). | Changed (app `809f3f9`, `0188d23`): the game's banner says at once how every trade ended ("Trade complete! / You received ...", "Trade cancelled.", "Trade ended by friend or host.") and, for a trade that stopped on an error, its code ("Code: ..."), so the next failure says why; on Windows, replacing or removing a file the trade has just written is tried again every 20 ms for up to 2 s while an antivirus scanner or the indexer holds it (a sharing violation ended the trade before: a cause a player's PC can have and the runner does not show). **Third report and cause** (2026-10-24: "when trade was being done it kicked us out and said trade cancelled"): the trade window's B button and the one row the game's cursor rests on were "Cancel trade" with nothing asked. The game keeps its cursor row when the next screen comes (back to the top when that one is shorter), so the A that confirmed the trade, or a press while the window was changing, landed on Cancel trade; and a proposal expired 180 s after it was made even while both players were still in it. **Reproduced in real games** (Linux, 40 ms each way, `native_launcher_newcomers_proof.py --scenario trade --mash-after-confirm`: both players press A and B after confirming): the old code (app `6d5beba`) ended the first trade with `trade_cancelled` on both launchers (trade-mash-old-1). **Fixed** (app, this change): B and every cancel row open "Cancel the trade?" with "No, keep trading" first, and a Yes in the first 0.6 s after that page appears counts for nothing; no row a confirmation leaves under the cursor cancels; the host keeps a proposal alive while its players are at work in it (status, offers, confirmations, preparation); and the host remembers why each player's trade ended, so the banner names it ("Your friend cancelled the trade.", "The trade timed out.", "Your friend lost connection.", "Lost connection to the host."; the trade status's `end_reason_version` 1). The same reproduction with the fix: both trades applied while both players pressed buttons, then the battle, closing saves, a fresh Continue and the store's integrity check (trade-mash-new-1, PASSED). Tests: `tests/test_trade_menu.py`, `test_trade_desk.py`, `test_trade_network.py`, `test_trade_settlement.py`. Not yet tried on two real PCs. **Follow-up (review of 2026-10-24, app branch `claude/fix-menus-tower`)**: a Confirm or offer that raced the friend's change (host `trade_offer_changed` / `trade_preparing`) escaped the window and the launcher cancelled the whole trade, and the friend read "Your friend cancelled the trade" for any cancel the launcher made on its own after an error. Now the window shows the trade again ("Offers changed; review again"); a launcher's own cancel carries the cause `automatic` (to a host that acknowledges the status's `end_reason_version` 2) and the friend reads "Trade stopped on friend's side."; "Cancel the trade?" opened with B starts with No alone and shows Yes below it once the grace has passed (B leaves the cursor on any row); one trade window polling alone no longer keeps an unanswered proposal alive. Tests: `test_trade_menu.py`, `test_trade_desk.py`, `test_trade_network.py`, `test_trade_settlement.py`. |

| MS-72 | Medium (**cause found and fixed, reproduced and verified on Linux in real games**; not yet tried on two real PCs) | The owner's report (2026-10-24): "illusion on zorua isnt working in multiplayer". In a battle between players only the link master (the game with multiplayer id 0, which can be either player's) runs the battle engine; the other game replays its commands. A battler's Illusion is worked out the first time a screen asks for it (battle_util.c GetIllusionMonPtr) and forgotten only by the switch-in script (battle_main.c SwitchInClearSetData), which runs on the master alone. So on the replaying game the battle's first Pokemon fixed that place's answer: a Zorua sent in later showed as itself (its picture, name and messages), there to its owner as well as to the opponent, while a Zorua that led worked (why illusion-1, Zorua first, passed). **Reproduced** (illusion-old: host Voltorb (Self-Destruct) then Zorua Lv25 then Snorlax Lv35, friend Zigzagoon with Growl; the friend's game was the master): after Voltorb fainted the friend saw "Snorlax Lv25" while the host's own screen showed "Zorua Lv25" with Zorua's picture. | Fixed (engine `368e3e0d`, PC only): the replaying game forgets the place's Illusion when a Pokemon is sent in there (battle_controllers.c BtlController_HandleSwitchInAnim), as the master's switch-in script does. The same battle with the fix (illusion-fix): both screens show "Snorlax Lv25" with Snorlax's picture (captured mid-battle; both proofs then stopped at their 600 s battle limit, the Growl keeping Zorua's Attack low). The fixed game also passed a whole proof (Magikarp, Zorua, Snorlax against a Zigzagoon with Pin Missile: the battle, the saves, a fresh Continue); a second such run failed and its log was overwritten by the next run before it was read. |
| MS-73 | High (**cause found and fixed**; unit tests and Linux real games; not yet tried on two real PCs) | The owner's report (2026-10-24, screenshot of Join Friend): "my friend created a character and now its not letting him join back" with "The game could not open. Private character and recovery files have been retained." Then: the host had closed the hosted game while the friend had not saved ("its not a crash he literally logged out"), and the friend's `pending-emergency.json` read `"state": "uncertain"` with no saved bytes (expected_revision 18). **Cause:** on a host loss the friend's launcher asks the game to stop for an emergency save; the game refuses while it is not standing free on the field (a menu, a message, a battle, a transition: multiplayer_save.c BEGIN_EMERGENCY_EXPORT, MP_SAVE_UNSAFE) and freezes, and the journal is left "uncertain" ("Keep this paused game open for recovery"). Nothing can ever adopt such a held game (the reconnect fence lifts only to abandon a battle), and once it was closed every later start stopped at cold_start.py's `emergency_recovery_required`, a code with no text, shown as the catch-all. | **Fixed** (app): a cold start, which holds the save lease and no game (so no core of this launcher still runs on the save), sets an unfinished preservation ("holding", "bytes_durable" or "uncertain", of this character) aside into `closed-recoveries/` with any bytes it wrote, uploads an export it had already made durable like any interrupted save, and starts from the world's save; the game then says "The host left before your latest progress could be kept, so you continue from your last save." A "preserved" journal, or one of another character, is never set aside. The held game's text now says to close it and continue from the last save. Also: the menu names any start failure's class and code ("Reason: ..."), shell/last-error.txt keeps it with the launch's crash.log and runtime.log, and the play check covers a host leaving while the friend is unsaved (`--host-leaves --friend-menu`) and joining back (`--rejoin`, `--second-character`; the Windows installed check runs the latter). Workaround for an installed build: rename `pending-emergency.json` in the account's folder under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\PokemonPrivate\launcher\players` and join again (the world's last save is kept). |

## Multiplayer and 3D repair update (2026-10-26, app `claude/admiring-bohr-dpxzuy`, engine `claude/feature-update-engine`; `docs/mp-3d-update-20261026.md`)

Scope: multiplayer stability, responsiveness and synchronization; the 3D world's rendering and performance, including the
overworld getting laggier the longer one plays. No casino work and no new mechanics in it
(the Game Corner changes of the same day: `docs/casino-economy.md`, `docs/ui-overhaul-and-fixes-20261026.md`). Where each result comes from: **L** this Linux machine (real game processes, the real launcher and world
server; the 3D view drawn by Mesa's software renderer, not a GPU); **W** GitHub's Windows runners (`windows-package`
full runs: real Windows game processes, one machine, no GPU). A simulated connection is the proofs' own
(`scripts/multiplayer/proof_network.py`: a delay each way, jitter, stalls, slow disk syncs). Nothing here was tried on the
owner's PCs or between two real PCs.

| ID | Severity | Issue | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MS-88 | High (multiplayer battles; **improved**, engine `bee47749`; L, real games over a simulated connection; not tried on two real PCs) | The owner (2026-10-26): a pause after almost every choice in a battle between players. Measured (L, the 3D game, 120 ms each way plus up to 80 ms of jitter, 20 ms per disk sync; the games' breadcrumbs timed with `PKMN_LINK_TRACE=1`, `tools/pc_port/link_latency.py`): a choice waited a median 2.9 s (FIGHT to the move menu, 2,924 ms) to 3.1 s (a move to what follows, 3,060 ms) for the host's answer, with nothing on screen meanwhile - an A press to the first thing on screen answering it took a median 2,983 ms. Each link block costs about 1.4 s: four one-way trips through the host (the network, the host's handling and its disk syncs: 4,613 syncs in one battle, 22.6 ms each on the simulated disk) and the master game's own pauses between blocks (messages and animations at normal speed, MS-80); an answer is two or more blocks away. | **Improved** (the 3D game): FIGHT shows the move page at once from what the game knows (the last move menu of that Pokemon, else its party data); a move picked there, START or B become a goal that the engine's own move menu carries out when it comes (the choice stays the engine's, made on its menu; a message or another menu drops it). After any other choice the message plate says "Choice sent. Waiting for the other player(s)..." from the next frame. In a battle between players A or B goes on from a message at once (each game for itself; the 49-frame wait stays for a player who does not press). After (same set-up): an A press to the first thing on screen a median **32 ms** (two frames), from 2,983 ms; with the move page all 22 move menus took the move chosen in advance, 4 frames (82 ms) after the menu came, against 16 frames (283 ms) for the proof's player pressing as soon as it could. **Not changed:** the round trip itself, a median 2.98 s before and after (the link protocol's trips per block and the host journal's disk syncs; shortening them changes the battle's durability and was not attempted). The Classic 2D game draws its battles with the GBA's windows: only the A/B skip applies there. |

Audits of the engine's per-frame overworld path (spawns, kept shinies, sparkles, shared spawns, other players, the bridge, the 3D caches) and of the launcher, companion and world host: every list on the per-frame path is fixed or bounded. Found and fixed: the game queued its sound every frame and never looked at the queue, so a game clock a little fast or a sound device that stopped for a moment left sound queued that never drained - the sound fell further behind for the rest of the session. Also found, not on the per-frame path: the character's profile folder grows by about 3 MB an hour of play (every save's copy is kept) and is listed every 1.5 s. | Sound: **fixed** (at most 8 frames queued; a stalled device's backlog dropped; `PKMN_PERF_LOG` reports the queue). ## Movement lag, multiplayer stability, trades and battles (2026-10-26, app `claude/admiring-bohr-dpxzuy`, engine `claude/feature-update-engine`; `docs/movement-mp-stability-20261026.md`)

The owner's request "8. Player Movement Lag, Multiplayer Stability, Trading & Battle Fixes" (no packaging). **L** this
Linux machine (the 3D view through Mesa's software OpenGL: a CPU's frame times, not a GPU's); nothing here ran on the
owner's PCs or between two real PCs.

Found 2026-11-04 (every input given to the mesher written out, `PKMN_DIORAMA_DUMP_CHUNKS`): walking to and fro over a map's seam turns the atlas pages round (page 0 is the map the player is on) and the inputs of the chunks by the seam with them, so a chunk was built again from an input it had had a version or two before: 159 of 557 builds on the Route 117 walk by Mauville (338 of its 940 ms). | **Improved** (engine `9f41e9f3`): a chunk keeps up to two earlier versions in the cache (never drawn, in slots nothing seen since needs) and takes one back, unbuilt, when its input returns to it (the same signature, its atlas pages holding what they held); the signature now covers the two inputs the mesher read that it left out (a cell's claim, the rock wall's material). The same binary with and without (`PKMN_DIORAMA_SUPERSEDED=0`), alternating, on this machine (software OpenGL): the walk (four crossings) built 296-298 chunks instead of 424-431, syncs over 16 ms 15-19 instead of 29-33, p95 15-17 ms instead of 18-21; 20 crossings: 237 built instead of 2,109, syncs over 16 ms 42 instead of 156, p95 11.9 ms instead of 20.7; the smallest cache (the grid's 144 chunks): 372-373 built instead of 430-436, syncs the same. Every game then answers its own open choice at once with an ordinary reply that changes nothing (the action menu a pass, the move menu a cancel, the party menu its first Pokemon that may go in, or none) and the master plays the forfeit as the giver's Run, so the giver loses (a forfeit decides the result even if the battle ended another way after it). A launcher without this change refuses the notice: the game and the launcher go together. **Update 2026-11-05 (the notice during a turn, L; Windows entries added):** the newcomers proof's `give-up` and `give-up-mid-turn` scenarios (app, this update), each two new characters' Rival Battle - 1v1 in real Linux game processes: in `give-up` the master's player has walked away and the other game gives up from its waiting screen (passed twice); in `give-up-mid-turn` both choose, and the giver gives up after the master has begun the turn (its first command of the turn was with the host) and before the giver's own game had heard of it (the host's messages to it held 4.5 s from its move on): the notice reached the master during the turn, which played it as the turn's next action, and the giver lost (passed; TEST_LEDGER has the two earlier attempts that did not reach that state). On the Windows runner (run 317, engine `6ce2648c`) both passed in Windows game processes, the mid-turn one with the master's command 48 with the host before the notice and Yes 2.1 s after the turn began (TEST_LEDGER). With the POWDER JAR and a Berry (the Direct Corner's own conditions), the Direct Corner's attendant (the right-hand counter on every Pokemon Center's 2F) first asks "Play BERRY CRUSH with computer partners?" and how many players in all (2 to 5); "no" goes on to the cable club as before. The player leads; the partners (LADDIE, LASSIE, DUDE, MISS, names in the style of the Berry Blender's) each crush a common Berry (Cheri to Aspear), press A at a person's pace (5 to 8 a second, with short rests) and answer "yes" to playing again; the player's "no" ends the session and they walk out of the link room, as after a wireless game. The rest is the original game: the player's game counts every press and works out the results, the BERRY POWDER (the original formula: every player's Berry's powder, times the group's size, times the silkiness; so a group of 5 gives what 5 friends would) and the pressing-speed record for that size, and saves after each round as the original does. The partners' names are not written to the trainer records. How: the link's exchange of each frame runs in memory (`src/link_rfu_2.c`): the player's own command returns to it as player 0's, as the adapter returns it, each partner puts its command in its own slot, and its link layer answers standbys and the closing of the link by itself. Found and fixed on the way (both reached only by this new path): the frame wait spun forever once the wireless link was set (the GBA waits for the adapter's VBlank in a loop; on PC the VBlank only comes in the normal wait), and Berry Crush read its freed game at the end of every session (harmless on the GBA, a crash natively). **Built next: Pokemon Jump and Dodrio Berry Picking with computer partners** (2026-10-28). In Mossdeep City's minigame building, after the old man's welcome, the PC game offers "You can play with computer partners. Which game will it be?"; then, as the lobby asks, a Pokemon that can play it and how many players in all (Pokemon Jump 2 to 5, Dodrio Berry Picking 3 to 5), and into the minigame room as after the wireless lobby. Pokemon Jump: each partner has a Pokemon that can play (Pikachu, Marill, Skitty, Spinda, Plusle, Minun, Azurill or Wooper) and jumps once a swing, timed from the vine's speed so that it is in the air as the vine passes, give or take 3 frames; now and then one jumps too late (never in a game's first 10 jumps; 1 swing in 300 at the vine's slowest stage, up to 1 in 75 at its fastest) and the game ends, as when a friend misses; partners whose Pokemon jump slowly can also be caught when the vine is at its fastest. Dodrio Berry Picking: each partner picks a Berry in reach of one of its Dodrio's heads after a person's reaction and leaves the columns it shares with the player's Dodrio to the player. Both answer "yes" to playing again; the player's game decides every jump, pick, score, bonus and prize and saves as the original does. Found and fixed on the way (reached only by this new path): Dodrio Berry Picking never started (the original takes a member's "ready" only when the leader's own last command was a packet, and the leader sends none while it waits for everyone), and it closed at every game's start natively (it set up its screen before allocating it: harmless on the GBA). **Built: all three between the world's players (MS-141c, 2026-11-04; `docs/wireless-minigames-world.md`).** In a world the attendant (and Mossdeep's old man) first asks "Play BERRY CRUSH with players in this world, or with computers?" (WORLD PLAYERS, COMPUTERS, NOT NOW); WORLD PLAYERS saves, as the wireless lobby does, and the Players menu opens on the game's lobby: lead a group, or join one ("Join NAME n/4"); the leader starts it once the game's minimum is in ("Start with N players"; Berry Crush and Pokemon Jump 2, Dodrio Berry Picking 3); every game walks into the link room and the original game runs over the world server - the leader's game the group's parent, the others its children, every frame of the link through the host. A group lost during a game (a player's connection or game gone, or the leader's frames stopping for 10 s) shows the game's own communication error, and A returns to the last save, as on the GBA. Up to four players (a world's). Proven: Berry Crush between two native games through real launchers and a hosted world (Linux): the lobby, the Berries, the crush, the results, "play again?" (one YES, one NO: "...dropped out"), both games back on the field with no communication error, the launcher's saves and a fresh Continue; and Pokemon Jump between two native games the same way (Mossdeep's old man, a Pikachu and a Marill): both jumping the vine six swings running, each game showing the other's jumps, the same score in both (the leader's), "play again?" YES and NO, both out of the room, 2,241 link frames, none dropped; and Dodrio Berry Picking between three native games (a Dodrio each): all three picking, the same results table in all three (the leader's, sent as a link block, every friend's Berries in it), "play again?" YES, NO, NO, all three out of the room, 7,182 link frames, none dropped. Found and fixed on the way by the Berry Crush proof (all in this new code): the bridge refused the lobby pages; the lobby could not open while the attendant's script held the field, nor while the player was still "moving" after a press toward the counter; a session's first menu refresh replaced the lobby with the Players home; a screen right behind the start's window close replaced it (the game's menu mailbox holds one), so the window stayed open; the leader's last frame went out after its leaving; a member's link never ended at the game's close (a GBA child leaves that to its adapter); the communication error's screen crashed in the adapter's VBlank work. **Still open**: four players; Windows. `docs/gameplay-visual-updates-20261027.md` section 3 |

here ran on Windows, a player's PC or between two real PCs.

## Found by the item audit after the first candidate (2026-10-27, engine `claude/feature-update-engine`; `docs/item-audit-20261027.md`)

**L** this Linux machine (the Classic 2D test build, real game processes). All three were present in the first
candidate (engine `6320c77d`): MS-156 in every engine since 2026-10-14, MS-157 since Update 29 (2026-10-16), MS-158 in
every PC build (the GBA's own code; its stack happened to keep what the PC's does not). The owner made the item audit
the package's gate, so all three fixes are in the 2026-10-27 package (engine `a5013203`).

| ID | Severity | Issue | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MS-156 | Medium (single-player play outside a world; **fixed in the 2026-10-27 package**, engine `223d15e1`; found 2026-10-27; L real game for two sources, the rest code read) | Offline (not connected to a world), the optional static encounters never start their battle: a Route 120 Kecleon revealed with the Devon Scope ("An invisible POKeMON became completely visible!", "The startled POKeMON attacked!") turned invisible again and no battle came, with and without `FLAG_RECEIVED_DEVON_SCOPE` set (scratchpad `devonscope/`). Each such script asks the Local Soft Reset's durable admission first (`MultiplayerStaticReset_BeginCurrent`), which answers FALSE when the game is not connected (`MultiplayerResetAdmission_Begin`), and FALSE takes the script's neutral return with no battle. The same branch is in every source: the seven Kecleons of Routes 119 and 120, New Mauville's Voltorbs, Aqua Hideout's Electrodes, Regirock, Regice, Registeel, Sudowoodo, Rayquaza, Kyogre, Groudon, Latias or Latios, Lugia, Ho-Oh, Mew and Deoxys (code read). The story's own battles (the bridge Kecleon with Steven) do not ask and are not affected. In a connected world the admission is the designed path. | **Fixed** (engine `223d15e1`, PC only; in the 2026-10-27 package): a save no world has enrolled meets them as ordinary battles (no admission, no reset in them); an enrolled save keeps the admission. Offline in the Classic 2D test build the Kecleon's battle starts, and Sudowoodo's after the Wailmer Pail. Not tried: the other sources one by one, an enrolled save offline (it still asks the admission, by design) |
| MS-157 | Low (the Bag's text; **fixed in the 2026-10-27 package**, engine `4f841321`; found 2026-10-27; L real game) | The Coin Case's description said "A case that holds up to 9,999 Coins.", but the PC game's wallet holds up to 999,999 coins since Update 29 (`MAX_COINS_WALLET`; the Game Corner's own screen shows "/ 999,999"): the Coin Case used from the Bag with 123,456 coins showed "Your COINS: 123456" under that description (scratchpad `keyitems/coincase-sheet.png`). | Fixed (engine `4f841321`, PC only; in the 2026-10-27 package): the description reads "A case that holds up to 999,999 Coins."; the GBA ROM keeps its own text (unchanged). |
| MS-158 | Low (the Bag's Mail; **fixed in the 2026-10-27 package**, engine `812d7bf3`; found 2026-10-27; L real game) | CHECK on a Mail in the Bag showed it through a Mail that no longer existed: the Bag's `CB2_CheckMail` handed `ReadMail` a local Mail, and the Mail screen reads it again as it draws and closes, after that callback has returned (`mail.c` keeps the pointer). On the GBA the stack happened to keep it; on PC it does not: CHECK on an Orange Mail asserted "invalid species: 12917" (a stale icon species). A player's PC game logs that error to `errors.log` and goes on (MS-71); the test build stops (scratchpad `field14/mail/test.log`). | Fixed (engine `812d7bf3`, PC only; in the 2026-10-27 package): the Mail is kept for the screen's whole life and cleared before each CHECK; the Orange Mail's design shows, B returns to the Bag, no assert (scratchpad `ms158/mail-after-sheet.png`). The GBA ROM is unchanged. |

## Found by the package runs (2026-10-27, windows-package runs 233 and 234, engine `claude/feature-update-engine`)

**W** the packaging runs' jobs (the Linux cross-compile job with MinGW; the Windows game processes on GitHub-hosted
windows-2022 runners); **L** this Linux machine (the same MinGW cross-compile of the four Windows builds; the same
checks in Linux game processes).

| ID | Severity | Issue | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MS-159 | High (the Windows build; **fixed**, engine `06ba39c2`; found 2026-10-27 by run 233) | The Windows 3D game did not compile since engine `dd416dd0` (2026-10-26, the native UI's location pop-up): `native_ui.c` includes the game's `window.h` after `SDL_opengl.h`, which on Windows includes `windows.h`, and `winuser.h` declares its own `ScrollWindow` ("conflicting types for 'ScrollWindow'"). The first 2026-10-27 package run (233, engine `a5013203`) stopped in its build job and packaged nothing; the 4 PM candidate `6320c77d` had the same file. The Linux builds never include `windows.h`, so every check on this machine passed, and no Windows build had run since `dd416dd0`. | Fixed (engine `06ba39c2`, PC only): `NOUSER` keeps `winuser.h` out of that file (nothing in it uses the Windows USER API) and wingdi.h's `TRANSPARENT` is undefined as `bfx_test.c` does; the four Windows builds are made on this machine with MinGW before the next package run. The GBA ROM is unchanged. |
| MS-160 | Low (a test script; **fixed** in the script, engine `a233be2a`; found 2026-10-27 by run 234; not the game) | The package run's feature-checks job (W) failed `shiny_check.py` in four cases (roamer, roamer-plain, outbreak, keep-won), each "its battle shiny 14". The script took the battle's shiny flag as the last number of the game's `owe battle:` log line; engine `65940085` (the wild item audit, 2026-10-27) added the Synchronize check's natures after it ("species S shiny X nature N lead ability A nature L"), so the script read the lead Mudkip's nature, 14, in every case. The game drew and fought each Pokemon alike: the same cases on the package's engine in Linux game processes (L) log "owe bumped: ... field shiny 1", then "owe battle: species 380 shiny 1 nature 22 lead ability 67 nature 14". The script is not in the Linux check battery, so only the Windows job ran it after `65940085`. | Fixed: the script reads the shiny flag by its name (`shiny_of`); on the package's engine in Linux game processes the old script fails the same four cases as the Windows job did ("its battle shiny 14"), and their logs read with the fixed one give each battle as drawn (roamer 1 and 1, roamer-plain 0 and 0, outbreak 1 and 1, keep-won 1 and 1), and the fixed script with the test harness of `ca0c588a` passes the four cases in Linux game processes; and the Windows job **passed** in run [235](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/40602685045) (that job alone, engine `a233be2a`; W: move switching, the shiny check and the Game Corner, each "passed", 00:20 UTC on 2026-10-28). |
| MS-93 | (update 2026-10-28; **open**, the cause named by run 234; app change, not yet run on Windows) | Run 234's climb (W, engine `eb83e633`, app `245011f`): both players climbed all 100 floors in 179 minutes, were paid (990,000 money, 45 Rare Candies and 751 BP each), saved (revision 72) and closed normally; 7 s later the fresh Continue's host start failed with the cause the 2026-10-27 change makes the log name: `checkpoint transaction failed (SQLITE_BUSY)`, another connection still holding the world database's write lock after the store's 5 s wait. The closing session had let the database go (its owner lock was free, or the error would have been `host_active`), and no other part of the launcher opens that file; which connection held the lock is not known (the run's records cannot be downloaded here: this environment's network policy refuses the artifact host). | Changed (app, after run 234): a host's start waits up to 60 s for the lock (`STARTUP_BUSY_TIMEOUT`; play keeps 5 s, MS-17); a wait of a second or more is written to the launcher's stderr with the write-ahead log's size, and a failure says how long it waited and how big the log was. L: the store's tests (113 pass). W: a climb-only run is next. |
| MS-93 | (update 2026-10-28, later; **fixed in the app**: the cause found and reproduced here; not yet run on Windows) | Run 237 (W, the climb alone, engine `eb83e633`, app `6d2d6fe` with the 60 s start-up wait): the climb passed again (both players won all 100 floors, in 179.7 and 180.8 minutes; paid 990,000 money, 45 Rare Candies and 751 BP each; saved at revision 72; closed normally). 5 s after the close the log said `checkpoint store: waited 4.3 s for the database lock (wal 0 bytes, shm 32768 bytes)`, and 7 s after it the fresh Continue's host start failed: `checkpoint transaction failed (SQLITE_BUSY after 5.5 s; wal 0 bytes, shm 32768 bytes)`. That transaction gave up after the play's 5 s, so it was not the store's open, which now waits 60 s; the timing fits the host start's first open of the world (`HostWorld.open`, which still waits 5 s). The write-ahead log was empty: whoever held the lock was not writing. The cause, found in the code and reproduced here: every open of a world runs its integrity check, which reads every checkpoint again and rebuilds the ownership history from each save, and it held the database's write lock while it ran; the launcher window checks every world in the background when it opens and when a game ends (`HostWorld.open`, which ran the check twice), so as the host started, those checks held the lock for seconds. The climb leaves 144 checkpoints (72 per player), and one check of them takes 4.1 to 5.2 s here; the shorter runs' worlds keep under 20, which is why only the climb met it. In the proof three windows checked at once (the climb's two, still open in its process, and the fresh Continue's own); on a player's PC there is one. | Fixed (app): the integrity check reads a snapshot (in WAL mode a reader holds nothing a writer waits for), and a check repeated on one connection with nothing committed since its last pass (through it or by another connection) gives the same answer without reading every checkpoint again: a host's start checked its world four times, now twice. The launcher proofs wait for the launcher windows' world lists before a phase starts a game, as a player starts once the list names the world (and a fresh Continue starts the launcher again, without the last phase's windows). L: on a test world grown to the climb's 144 checkpoints (19.5 MB), with two background checks running, the host start's open failed as in run 237 (`SQLITE_BUSY after 5.0 s; wal 0 bytes, shm 32768 bytes`); with the fix it opened, and the host start's opens took 10.2 s alone, 14.4 s beside one background check and 24.3 s beside three. The store's tests and its callers' (188) pass. W: a climb-only run is next. |
| MS-93 | (update 2026-10-28, run 238; **fixed**: passed on the Windows runner) | Run 238 (W, the GitHub-hosted windows-2022 runner, the climb alone, engine `eb83e633`, app `05de0b0` with the fix): the climb passed (both players won all 100 floors, in 179.3 and 179.8 minutes; paid 990,000 money, 45 Rare Candies and 751 BP each; saved at revision 72; closed normally), then the fresh Continue passed: both games started in Littleroot (60 s and 106 s after the climb's close), the launcher workers were ready and both games closed normally (exit codes 0 and 0); the store's integrity check passed with the source saves unchanged. The job's log, read in full, has no "waited ... for the database lock" line and no `SQLITE_BUSY`. | Fixed (app `05de0b0`), passed in Windows run 238 on GitHub's runner, not on the owner's PCs. The 2026-10-27 package does not include it (its app is older); MS-161's faster check (app `78c373a`) came after this run. How the fresh Continue's 60 s divides between the launcher's start, its world list and the host start is not known here (the run's records cannot be downloaded: this environment's network policy refuses the artifact host). |
| MS-161 | Medium (performance, growing with play; **partly fixed**, app, about 5 times faster; found 2026-10-28 with MS-93) | Opening a world takes longer with every save it keeps. Its integrity check reads every checkpoint again and rebuilds the ownership history from each save: about 32 ms per checkpoint on this machine (the climb's 144: 4.1 to 5.2 s). Every revision is kept, and the launcher saves every 5 minutes of play besides manual saves and the saves the host makes itself (a won battle's experience, a trade), so a long game keeps hundreds or thousands (1,000 would take about 30 s per check here). After MS-93's fix a host's start runs the check twice, and the launcher window once per world when it opens and when a game ends. | **Partly fixed** (app, 2026-10-28): the check reads each save once. Its two banks and inspect_ownership's second reading are answered from a small cache kept by content (the 32 most recent bank extractions and inventory readings; a save's older bank is usually the last save's newer one), so nothing it verifies changed: equal bytes give the same answer, changed bytes are read again. L: a full check of a test world of 144 distinct saves 0.77 to 0.86 s (it was 3.9 to 4.7 s), of the 16-checkpoint world 0.18 s (0.48 to 0.56 s); a host start's opens of the 144-save world 1.9 s alone and 3.2 s beside three background checks (8.9 and 22.4 s before). **Still open**: the check still grows with every save (about 6 ms per checkpoint here); checking only what was added since the last checked point, keeping a digest of the history already checked, is the owner's call (the check guards every player's Pokemon ownership). |

| MS-166 | Medium (scripts; **fixed**, engine `d64bae5c`, PC only, not in the 2026-10-27 package; the owner's report of 2026-10-29) | Taking a Pokemon back from the Day Care lady: she named the fee and, with the money in hand, said "You don't have enough money..." (reproduced in the 2D test build: a save with 3,000, a fee of 100). The script engine calls every special through `u16 (*)(void)` and stores what it returns; a special declared to return an 8-bit value (bool8, u8, s8) defines only the low byte of the PC's return register, the rest whatever the function computed last: `IsEnoughForCostInVar0x8005` ends with `setae %al` right after reading the money, so its TRUE read 2,817 with 3,000 in hand, and a FALSE could read non-zero. 99 of the 663 specials return 8 bits (the Day Care's own `GetDaycareState` among them); whether one misread depended on what its last instructions left in the register. The GBA ROM never had this: its compiler widens the byte. | Fixed (engine `d64bae5c`, PC only; the GBA ROM unchanged): `specialvar` narrows each such special's result to its byte (widened by its sign for the one s8), from a table `tools/pc_port/special_results.py` writes from `data/specials.inc` and the specials' definitions (its `--check`, in the new check target `check-special-results`, keeps it current). L: `--selftest-special-results` runs `specialvar` as a script does, 10 checks: with the narrowing switched off 5 fail (100 with 3,000 not enough, 3,001 with 3,000 enough), with it all pass; the Day Care in the 2D test build: "Perfect! Here's your POKeMON", both Pokemon back for 100 each. Not tried on Windows; the other 98 specials are covered by the same rule, not each played. |

(2) a \l (scroll a line) cleared the box like a \p (a new page): the line above vanished, the box showed one line, and after the button nothing until the next letter came (a blank box at a slow text speed). | Fixed (engine `b8efebca`, PC only; the GBA ROM unchanged): the copy and the screens' buffers hold 1,024 bytes (the longest text is about 800), checked against each other when the game compiles; a \l keeps the line before it on top and the text after it comes under it, as the GBA's printer scrolls; the battle box uses the same page logic. ## The owner's crash report and step 16's first fixes (2026-10-29/30, engine `claude/feature-update-engine`; `docs/3d-world-geometry-20261029.md`)

**L** this Linux machine (the 3D test build, real game processes; the 3D view on Mesa's software OpenGL). Nothing here
ran on Windows or a graphics card.

| MS-169 | High if it is what the owner meets (the PC game; **open: not reproduced**; the owner's report of 2026-10-29: "being in game too long / being in safari too long causes game crash") | No cause found yet. Ran clean (L): a 60-minute Safari Zone stay (216,000 frames; the Safari game restarted 384 times, 42 battles, catches; game process 270 -> 278 MB, game heap back to 11.5 KB of 113.2 KB in the field, GPU memory flat), an AddressSanitizer build's 10-minute Safari stay with the sprite palettes forced to run out 35 times and the clock past midnight, and both ends of a Safari game (out of steps, out of balls: the player put at Route 121's entrance, play going on). AddressSanitizer found, and engine `42daa2b6` fixed: three reads past the object table around visible wild Pokemon when the palettes run out (`GetOldestActiveOWESlot`, `SortOWEAges`, `StartWildBattleWithOWE`; upstream bugs, shown on the unfixed build) - the memory there is zero padding in both the Linux and the Windows builds, so this is not shown to be the crash - and a read past the quick-start HUD's palette on the title screen. Left as is: the picture decompressor reads a word past compressed data (upstream; harmless). Needed to go further: after the next crash, `crash.log`, `errors.log` and `runtime.log` from the newest folder in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\PokemonPrivate\launcher\launches\`, and whether it was in a world with other players. | Open |
| MS-169 | (update 2026-11-03: AddressSanitizer soaks, Windows-sized stacks, memory over battles; **still not reproduced**) | Ran (L): AddressSanitizer builds of the 3D and the Classic test builds through the twelve-town loop (3D: 794 visits in one process over two hours, no report once MS-182 was fixed; Classic: 240 visits, no report) and through the gameplay plan's first eighth (474 visits, 64 trainer battles, two processes); the loop with Windows' default 2 MB stacks (`ulimit -s 2048`: the most any thread used was 264 KB). Found and fixed on the way, each a read past the end of some data, none shown to be the crash: MS-182 (the 3D snapshot on a warp's last frame), MS-183 (door palettes), MS-184 (the Mega indicator's palette), MS-185 (the PC screens' string copies). Left as they are (upstream code shared with the ROM; reads of the next few bytes, the values unused or overwritten): the decompressor's lookahead, the end of a double battle's turn reading one past the turn order (`HandleAction_ActionFinished`), the map name pop-up's frame copied 64 bytes long (`map_name_popup.c`), a tall-grass effect reading the next map's tile attributes on a warp's last frame (`UpdateTallGrassFieldEffect`). The plan's first AddressSanitizer process ran out of memory after 55 minutes and 46 battles: AddressSanitizer's own allocator in a 32-bit process (2.3 to 2.8 GB over 12 battles in the second); the normal 3D test build ran the same whole plan in one process (472 targets, 65 battles), its address space 826-866 MB from the 12th battle to the last, growing only as new places loaded (MS-89 measured 81 battles flat before). The Windows 3D test build under Wine with everything it allocated above 2 GB (`PKMN_HIGH_ADDRESS_TEST=1`, as a long session can place it on a 64-bit Windows PC) ran the town loop (120 visits), the same plan (472 targets, 65 battles) and a 30-minute Safari Zone stay (132 Safari games, 25 battles, memory flat at about 450 MB), each in one process, without a crash. Also found on the way: MS-12, a stall or pause of 12 s or more during a checkpoint disconnected the game from the world until it restarted (fixed; not a crash: the game goes on alone). | Open: the owner's `crash.log`, `errors.log` and `runtime.log` are still what can name the cause (as above). Every battle message carries the three battle text buffers and the item effect's battler as the last battle left them - no battle clears them, each message prepares what it names - and in a battle between players the master's intro sent them to the other cores, where the link's message check (MS-71's) refused one. Seen in the Championship's final after each player's computer matches: the host's buffer 2 held a Pokemon's name for party slot 6, one past the party - what `Cmd_switchhandleorder` writes when the party screen of the "switch Pokemon?" prompt is left without a choice, as players do in ordinary trainer battles. The GBA ROM unchanged. Linux only, not on Windows or the owner's PCs. |

| MS-73 | (update 2026-11-03, a personal hunt's encounter) | The fix (a start after a frozen game closed sets its unfinished emergency save aside and continues from the world's save) also covers a host loss right after a personal hunt's encounter was generated, which one test still wanted refused: CI's `python-tests` failed on that test on every push since 2026-10-24 (the ledger said it was older than the fix; a bisect shows the fix broke it). | Kept as the fix made it, the test updated: the encounter lived only in the held core, which a closed game no longer has, so it is lost as an unsaved encounter is, and the player is told; the world's save is untouched and the host's attempt count is never rolled back (the next try is the second, so no free try). Refusing instead would lock the character out for good. Unit tests only (L). |
| MS-181 | Low (multiplayer, under the water; **fixed** 2026-11-04, app `0a42eae`; found 2026-11-03 reading the code) | A player under the water (after Dive) is not drawn in any other game: the companion sends the game's underwater movement (the mailbox's mode 4) as "unavailable" (`mailbox.py` `Pose.network_fields`: "no transport/rendering support yet"), and the world record has no movement mode for it (`protocol.MOVEMENT_MODES`: walk, run, bike, surf). So two players diving on the same underwater map do not see each other. And while a diver stands in a menu or a dialogue the game reports "menu" (`multiplayer_presence.c`, Update 32) with the movement "walk", which every game draws: another diver on that map would see them standing there as on land, gone again when the menu closes. The 2026-10-27 doc said another player under the water "keeps the original's underwater look": they are not shown at all. | Open: needs a movement mode for diving in the world record - since MS-28's fix (2026-11-04) every player of a world has the host's exact launcher version, so a new value needs no capability exchange any more, only a new launcher version - then the companion's mapping (autosave and the emergency save's arming read the same "overworld" and must keep skipping the sea floor until that is decided), and the game drawing them on their carrier as the silhouette (`docs/gameplay-visual-updates-20261027.md` section 4). Not started. | | **Fixed** 2026-11-04 (app `0a42eae`, PC games; the companion only, no engine change): the world record has the movement "dive" (protocol 4, the same version as MS-28's fix), and the companion sends the game's underwater movement as "dive" on the field and in a menu alike, and gives the other games the underwater movement back, which they already draw with the underwater look. Autosave and the emergency save's arming still skip the sea floor, as before. Tested on this Linux machine (`scripts/multiplayer/native_dive_check.py`: two games, a real world server and real companions under the water of Route 124, Classic 2D and 3D test builds; and the same scene with the code before the fix: B not drawn while swimming, drawn standing as on land in its menu). Not yet: another diver on their own carrier's silhouette as one's own screen shows it (they show the original's underwater look); not on Windows, not between two PCs. |
| MS-181 | (update 2026-11-07; **fixed**, engine `18e66680`; PC only, the GBA ROM unchanged; no app change) | Another player under the water was drawn with the original's underwater look (the generic blob drawn in), not on the Pokemon that dove, as each game draws its own diver. | **Fixed**: a game under the water on the Pokemon that dove publishes it (the mount state "carrier", as on the water; the host accepts that state with any movement), and every other game draws that player as its own diver: their character's surfing pose as the silhouette, in a palette of their slot's, on their carrier's silhouette in another; the carrier is made first and hidden until the avatar is drawn as its rider, so the two never disagree; without a free palette the original's look stays (tried again a second later). Checked (L): `--selftest-carriers` 65 checks, 0 failed (15 new; a mutant without the hiding fails one); two games diving from Route 124's surface with a real world server and companions (`scripts/multiplayer/native_dive_check.py --scenario surface`), the Classic 2D and the 3D test builds: A's companion received B's Wailord under the water and A's pictures show B on it as the silhouette, turning as it swims (engine `a4a318ec`, the same scene: no carrier, the original's look); the original scenario passes as before; the line check, the ROM, the builds, check-native (TEST_LEDGER "Another player under the water on their own carrier"). W: run [372](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42369119267)'s new `dive-check` job (Windows game processes of the Classic 2D test build, engine `bf9a91b1`): both scenarios passed, B's carrier received under the water and B given to A's game; its pictures not looked at (not reachable from this machine). Not between two PCs. **The review's fixes** (2026-11-07, engine `50d865c5`): another diver's carrier is made only when every sprite palette it needs at once is free (its species' while its silhouette is made from it, its silhouette's, and its rider's look), its silhouette's from a free slot only: none is ever made by removing anything else (the overworld's palette loader makes room by removing wild Pokemon); a carrier left hidden for two seconds without its rider (no palette for their look) is given up, its palette with it, and tried again a second later; the retry's wait starts afresh when the slot is released or a surface carrier is made; and the diver leaves the rider's look before their carrier is removed (on the surface, or their carrier gone from the relay), keeping the carrier under them while no palette is free for their next look, so a rider is never drawn on nothing (that hand-off by reading only: a dive and a surfacing each change the map, which removes the other player's object first, so no two-game scenario reaches it). Checked (L): `--selftest-carriers` 77 checks, 0 failed (12 new; copies without the give-up, without either reset of the wait, or with the earlier palette test under the water fail them; one whose silhouette's palette comes from the evicting loader passes, that path being unreachable once the palettes are counted first); the dive check from the surface in the 2D and 3D test builds and the warp in 2D passed as before, the pictures as before; every self-test of the Windows job's list in the Linux game; the line check, the ROM, the builds, check-native, check-abi. W of the review's fixes: run [373](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42372104776) (the full run, every one of its 92 jobs passed): `--selftest-carriers` 77 checks, 0 failed, on Windows; the dive-check job from the surface, B's carrier under the water received (mount state 2, Wailord 321) and B given to A (mode 4, drawn), A's 110 pictures not looked at (not reachable from this machine), the warp as before; its installer, the one prepared, carries the change.
