| MS-51 | (update 2026-11-04; **fixed**) | A milestone Pokemon delivered to a game that closed before the player saved was lost when the save it started from had a full party and PC: the replayed word made nothing (0), but the host kept the first receipt and refused "Send it now"; and the host's replayed claims word, OR-ed into the game's record, marked the milestone delivered there too, so even a resend would have answered "already delivered". | Fixed (engine `36fb4589`, app: the commit of this row; PC only): the game takes only the prize floors from a claims word - a milestone's delivered bit is set with the Pokemon itself, in the same save (`TOWER_CLAIM_MON_MASK`) - and the host's claims words carry the prize floors only (`tower_rewards.word_claims`); the host reads a replayed milestone word's new receipt (`TowerService._collect_receipts`, `TowerBook.replayed_mon_receipt`): 0 owes a delivered milestone again ("NO ROOM FOR YOUR POKEMON", "Send it now" sends the very same Pokemon once there is room), 1 or 2 count an owed one delivered. L: `--selftest-tower` 2,103/2,103 (the game code with a full party and PC), app tests (Tower 173). Not run on Windows; no launcher run of the close-before-save sequence (it needs a save with a full PC). |
| MS-190 | Medium (co-op Gym battles: experience farmed, and a pending award lost to a reset; **fixed** 2026-11-04, engines `1b2c0089` and `1552e268`, PC only, the ROM unchanged; the owner's instruction of 2026-11-04, `docs/owner-decisions.md` 4-5) | (1) The co-op Gym offer was blocked only for a player who had fought the Leader, and every co-op Gym battle awarded its experience, lost ones too: a team could knock out some of the Leader's Pokemon, lose, and fight again for the same experience with no whiteout and no cost, and a helper who already had the badge earned it again with each teammate. (2) Each game kept the award (the defeated Pokemon and who faced them) in memory only until its result script ran: a game that closed or reset in between lost it, and so did a Tower Duo floor's experience (its floor stays the host's). | **Fixed**: (1) a co-op Gym battle awards its experience only with the win that earns this player the badge (the victory script's own test); a loss, a draw and a helper's win award none; the helper's prize money is unchanged; ladder battles keep the host's rules. (2) The result waiting for its script is in every save made meanwhile (SaveBlock3's co-op result record, 76 bytes after the TM record, zeros when nothing waits). The launcher saves on the host as soon as a co-op battle has fully ended, and the game runs the result script once that save is made (or after 15 s), then asks for the host save again; it holds the soft reset key while the first save is coming; a save loaded with a result waiting runs it once. The launcher's save checker reads the record (`flash.py`, refused unless sealed). Found by the real two-game runs: the game first asked for the first save itself, opening its Players page while the launcher was still finishing the battle, which broke the battle's handoff in both launchers (run c1: both stopped with "save recovery required"); the launcher now makes that save itself (engine `1552e268`, `launcher_session._save_after_coop_battle`). And a game started again with a result waiting asked for its save after the script while its launcher was still arming its emergency saver: the one refusal ("busy: checkpoint lock") left the result's effects unsaved until a later save (runs c3, c4); the launcher now tries such a request again for up to 30 s (`HOST_SAVE_BUSY_SECONDS`). Left: a crash in the second or two before the first host save still goes back to the save before the battle. Tests: `--selftest-coop-exp` 57/57, `tests/test_coop_result_record.py`, `tests/test_soft_reset.py` (the retry); real two-game run c5 on Linux **passed** (Rustboro Gym, the friend's game closed during its award: restarted from the host save with the result waiting, the same experience as the closed game had in memory, nothing lost or doubled, both games saved before and after their scripts, a fresh Continue and the store's integrity check after). **Windows**: the same proof in Windows game processes, windows-package run 280's new `coop-proof` job, **passed** (the friend's game closed during its award with WM_CLOSE, started again from the host save and given the same experience, 212,427; a GitHub-hosted runner, not a player's PC). Not on two PCs. |
| MS-191 | Low (an engine layout check; **fixed** 2026-11-04, engine `1b2c0089`; found 2026-11-04) | `make check-abi` (`tools/pc_port/abi_layout.py`, the GBA vs PC layout parity, run by the engine's native-pc workflow) failed from the TM record (engine `9e033b5d`, 2026-10-31): SaveBlock3's PC-only tail had three records but the tool still allowed two. | **Fixed**: the tool allows SaveBlock3's four PC-only records (egg queue, Tower, TM, co-op result); `ABI layout parity: OK (172 types checked)`. |
| MS-161 | (update 2026-11-04; **improved again, about 2 times; still grows with every save**) | A host's start and the launcher window still read every save of a world (MS-161 above): measured again on a test world of 600 distinct saves (two players at 300 each, grown from a trade world), a cold open with its integrity check took 2.67 s (about 4.4 ms a save); the profile put half of it in checking each save's 420 PC slots one byte at a time and a third of the rest in three full hashes per save that the check never reads. | **Improved** (app, 2026-11-04): an empty PC slot is answered at once (the full reading's own answer for all zeros) and a full one is decrypted and summed in one step, and a save's three hashes are made only when something reads them; every check is the same (`tests/test_flash_box_reader.py`: the reader it replaced, kept word for word, gives the same answer or the same refusal on 5,603 generated slots). The 600-save world's cold open: 1.13 to 1.39 s (from 2.67 s), about 1.9 ms a save. **Still open, options for the owner**: the check still reads every save, so it still grows (2,000 saves: about 4 s here). (a) Keep it (it guards every player's Pokemon ownership, and now costs about 2 ms a save); (b) remember each save's checked reading by its content hash and parser version in a sidecar file, so a save is parsed once in its life (the order and ownership replay still runs in full; a damaged save is still caught by its hash); (c) check only the saves added since the last full check, keeping the replay's state and a hash chain of the checked rows (fastest; a check then trusts its own earlier result). |
| MS-145 | (update 2026-11-04; **fixed**, engine `9f0809f6`, PC only, the ROM unchanged) | The keep list (MS-79's shinies, MS-144's Legendaries) lived only while the game ran: a game closed and started again forgot a shiny or Legendary the player had walked away from. | **Fixed**: the list is in every save (SaveBlock3's kept wild Pokemon record, 140 bytes after the co-op result record: each one's look, map, where it was last seen, level and age in the list; zeros when none is kept, as in every older save) and comes back when a save is loaded, so each is put back on its map as after leaving it, until caught or knocked out. A damaged record, or an entry outside the game's own bounds (no Pokemon, a species, map group, elevation or movement the game has not), is read as none kept; the launcher's save checker refuses such a save (`flash.py`). Tests: `--selftest-shiny` (12 new checks: none kept is zeros, three kept written and read back in their order of age through the save's own paths, the same bytes when saved again, damaged and out-of-bounds records refused, a full list of eight whole), `tests/test_owe_keep_record.py`. Real game (2026-11-04, engine `7f9b56bf`'s `tools/battle_env/shiny_check.py` keep-restart, Linux test build): four shinies kept on Route 101, the game saved in Oldale Town and ended; a new game process Continued from that save (nothing made shiny) and, back on Route 101, put all four back where they were last seen, each drawn shiny - a new process knows them only from the save. Not run on Windows yet (the feature-checks job runs every shiny_check case from the next run on that engine). |
| MS-145 | (update 2026-11-05; **the Windows check's comparison**, engine `6b75d6b6`) | Windows run 284's feature-checks job failed in `shiny_check.py`'s keep-restart: the Binacle put back on Route 101 after the restart stood at (21, 17) and the check wanted (22, 21), where it was first seen. A kept shiny's record follows it as it wanders (`OWEKeep_Track`), so the save holds where it last stood and the restart puts it back there: the game was right; the check compared the first-seen place, and passed only when the first one put back had not moved before the player left (as in run 280). | **The check fixed**, the game unchanged: test builds log each kept one written into the save (`owe keep: saved ... batch B`; PC test builds only, no GBA line moves, the ROM unchanged), and keep-restart compares every one put back with the last write's entries. L (the Classic 2D test build, the Windows job's steps): keep-restart five times, all passed; in three of them the first one put back had wandered before the save (the old comparison would have failed, as on Windows), and in all five every one put back stood where the save had it; keep passed; the ROM unchanged. Windows: **passed** in the next full run's feature checks (run 288) and in every full run since (297, 310, 322). |
| MS-39 | (update 2026-11-04; **fixed**, PC only, the GBA ROM unchanged; the owner's instruction of 2026-11-04: Mega Rayquaza should work) | Mega Rayquaza could not be reached: Rayquaza could not learn Dragon Ascent, the move its Mega Evolution needs (not in its Gen 6 learnsets, no tutor, no script). | **Fixed** (engine `d6e3072e`): Rayquaza learns Dragon Ascent at level 1, the level the Gen 8 and Gen 9 learnsets give it, so the summary screen's move relearner and Fallarbor's Move Relearner offer it to any Rayquaza, and with it (and the Mega Ring) Rayquaza Mega Evolves, holding any item but a Z-Crystal. | MS-55 | (update 2026-11-04; options) | Looked at again for a PC-only fix. The game keeps the cursor row whenever a new screen arrives, and cannot tell a new page from a refresh of the same one: the titles of the Tower's pages name their floors ("Floors 1-6", "Duo: floor 12"), so resetting the row on a title change would also move it while paging through the floors. | Options: (a) the launcher marks a screen that is a new page (a flag in the screen's four spare bytes, read only by a game that says it can: an ABI addition in both repositories, then the game resets the row on it); (b) reset on a title change and accept the paging (not done: it changes how the Tower's pages page); (c) leave it (cosmetic: nothing is chosen without A, and the help line names the row). Not changed; (a) is the one that would not change anything else. |

A question's page now opens on "No, keep it". Older games and launchers keep the old behaviour (the launcher strips the mark; an older bridge would refuse the line, so it is written only for a game that has the marker). Tests: the bridge's unit tests (4,590 checks, 0 failed; the screen passed only with the capability, checked as an ordinary one), the Windows (MinGW) 2D and 3D builds compile, the app's (the codec, the session, the pages, the manifest and bridge.txt; the whole suite 3,086 passed, 22 skipped); a real game through the launcher (L, the Tower UI scenario): every page opened on row 0 (before the fix, in the same scenario, "Floors and bosses" on row 2, the Tower Shop on row 1, Hyper Training on row 2). Not yet on Windows. |
| MS-192 | Medium (Windows CI's Battle Tower Duo proofs; **fixed** 2026-11-04 in the proof, app: this change; found by windows-package run 273) | Run 273's Battle Tower jobs duo, mega and join failed: each Duo battle ended (its room closed) and the proof then waited 30 minutes for both launchers to say `battle_finished`. Since MS-190 the launcher saves on the host as every co-op battle ends (its status moves on to `saving`, then `saved`), and a Tower floor is a ladder result, so the game's result script asks for a host save of its own after it: each player's revision goes up by two after a Duo battle (1 to 3 on Linux), and the proof never saw both launchers at `battle_finished` at once. The join scenario also expected floor 11's Duo page to ask for a save first (the last Duo battle's award changed the party after its save, MS-60): that award is now in the host saves already, so the page counts who is ready ("Floor 11: 0/2 ready") - the save the player was asked for is no longer needed. The game and the launcher work as MS-190 means them to. | **Fixed** in `scripts/multiplayer/native_launcher_tower_proof.py`: a launcher is back from a battle at `battle_finished` or its save after it (`saving`, `saved`) once that save is done; after the award the proof waits until both players' revisions stand still with no save running (then reads them for the closing save); floor 11's page may count the ready instead of asking for a save. Linux: the duo and join scenarios passed with it (real games through real launchers; join's revisions 3, 6 and 9 after its three Duo battles, its floor 11 page "Floor 11: 0/2 ready"); a first join run without the page's change failed there, as above. Windows: the next run. |
| MS-141c | (update 2026-11-04; **four players**, and **Windows**) | The minigames between a world's players had run with two (Berry Crush, Pokemon Jump) and three players (Dodrio Berry Picking), on Linux only; a world holds four. | **Four players run** (L: real games and launchers on one machine, the Classic 2D game of engine `49b12c51`, whose 2D code is today's `7f9b56bf`'s; app: this change, `native_launcher_minigame_proof.py --players 4` with make_tower_saves.py's new crush-c and -d at Rustboro's and Slateport's Pokemon Center 2F, jump-c, -d and dodrio-d): each friend joined in turn ("Join Crush Host 1/4", "2/4", "3/4"), the host's page offered "4 of 4 players: start?", the four games were told their places 0 to 3; **Berry Crush** passed (the host leading, three members, every game's results and play-again question, 12,236 link frames through the host), **Pokemon Jump** passed (every game showed every other player's six jumps, the same score, 1,260, and six swings cleared by all four in every game; 7,423 frames), **Dodrio Berry Picking** passed (the same results table in all four games, each player's Berries counted; 10,639 frames); every game back on the field with no communication error, then the saves, a fresh Continue and the store's integrity check. Pokemon Jump's first two four-player runs failed in the proof, not the game: it focused each game's window as the round began, which took up to 0.84 s with four games, and the vine caught every Pokemon at its first swing; the proof now focuses during the names (GameIntro) and takes no picture while the vine swings, and the two-player Jump passed again after the change. **Windows** (run 277, GitHub's windows-2022 runner, Windows game processes, not a player's PC): Berry Crush and Pokemon Jump between two players and Dodrio Berry Picking between three passed, the first Windows run of MS-141c; the Windows job now also runs each with four players. Run 280 (its second attempt): the two-, two- and three-player jobs passed again; the four-player Dodrio Berry Picking and Pokemon Jump jobs failed in the proof's checks, not the games (every game played, the same results or score in all four, every play-again answered, every game back on the field with its link closed): each needed every game's reading to have caught a phase of a second or less (EndLink, GameOver) while the proof's pictures, about a second each on Windows, held up every game's reading. The proof now requires each game's lasting phases and records the short ones when seen (app: this change); the four-player jobs **passed** in the next full run (288) and in every full run since (run 322: Berry Crush, Pokemon Jump and Dodrio Berry Picking with four players). Under AddressSanitizer (L, the Classic 2D game of `7f9b56bf`) the four-player Berry Crush showed only MS-97's two known reads in each game and Dodrio Berry Picking nothing. |
| MS-161 | (update 2026-11-04 evening; **measured on Windows**) | How long a host's start takes to open and check a world with many saves had been measured on Linux only. | A repeatable measure, `tools/perf/world_open_bench.py`: a test world of two players with 300 distinct saves each, built through the checkpoint store's own API from synthetic saves, opened cold and checked three times. Linux: 1.32 to 1.60 s (2.19 ms a save, a soak running beside it). **Windows** (GitHub's windows-2022 runner, not a player's PC; the new world-open-bench job, in every Windows run from app `7307edd`): **0.94 to 0.95 s** (1.57 ms a save). The check still grows with every save; options (b) and (c) above stay the owner's. |
| MS-161 | (update 2026-11-05; **option (b) built**, the owner's decision #41: validated caching, save integrity before speed) | An open still read every save twice (the ownership replay and the handler-history replay), about 1.6-2.2 ms a save, growing with every save. | **Built** (app `d67f368`, its reader pin `fd2eec3`; `docs/world-open-cache.md`): each save's accepted reading is remembered in a file beside the world (`world.sqlite3.save-readings`), keyed by the SHA-256 of the save's bytes that the open computes itself, and bound to the reader's code (`READER_FINGERPRINT` over flash.py, ownership.py, trade_friendship_observations.py and save_readings.py: a test fails until it is updated, and a new pin discards older files), the file's format, the Python version, the save profile and the world. Only accepted readings are kept, never a refusal; the whole file is sealed (a torn, edited or foreign file is ignored and rebuilt); it is written only after the replays that use it pass, and one that cannot be written leaves the full check. Every replay (order, ownership, trades with their exact-swap check, transfers, handler history, reservations, resets), SQLite's integrity check and every save's stored hash still run on each open. Linux (600 saves, `tools/perf/world_open_bench.py`): an open with the readings 0.39-0.52 s instead of 0.70-0.96 s, with handler history 0.73-0.85 s instead of 1.54-2.06 s; the first open after an update about 0.1 ms a save slower. Tests: `tests/test_save_readings.py` (25 tests, 43 subtests: the same readings or refusals as the reader, 19 kinds of damaged or foreign file, unwritable paths, copied or restored worlds, three processes at once), nine deliberate faults each caught; the 66 affected test files 1,035 passed. **On Windows** (run [327](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42092492022), app `e60aba0`): the 600-save world opened and checked three times with the remembered readings, the save path's tests and the app's whole suite passed. |
| MS-118 | (update 2026-11-04 evening; **AddressSanitizer**) | The earlier chunk versions (engine `9f41e9f3`) had been checked for drawing (each one brought back built again and compared) but not for memory errors. | The long walk (20 crossings between Mauville City and Route 117) in a fresh AddressSanitizer build of the 3D test build at engine `7f9b56bf`, the terrain check on: **no report** in 6,296 frames, 1,882 chunks brought back from an earlier version, 0 differed, 983,058 skipped-sync checks, 0 stale; the twelve-town loop in the same build: 360 visits in one process, no report. Linux only. |
| MS-118 | (update 2026-11-04 night; **the mesher's face sort**, engine `586a6156`) | Each chunk's build sorts its shell faces (about 30,000 in a big chunk) before merging them, and the sort was 40% of the mesher's time: its keys packed each face's fields into 128 bits, and most chunks' needed 66 to 74. | **Faster, the same meshes**: a face's two ends go into its key as their lengths (the same order: each end follows its start in the key), which fits most chunks' keys in 64 bits, and such keys are sorted as one word with the face's index (12 bytes a pass instead of 20). Every chunk's mesh is byte-identical over 2,009 chunk inputs the game wrote out (the Route 117 walk by Mauville, and 121 each at 12 places from Fortree and Sootopolis to Routes 111 to 123), the mesher alone 10-21% faster on them; in the 3D game's four-crossing walk 6-14% less meshing a chunk, the frames over 16 ms as before within the runs' spread (those are the deferred chunks' builds and the atlas). Still open as above; L only, not yet in a Windows run. |
| MS-118 | (update 2026-11-05; **the atlas's drawing**, engine `daeed7a0`; and Windows run 286) | The atlas (every metatile in view drawn into three textures: whole, lower layer, upper layer) is drawn again whole when the neighbour pages turn at a crossing and whenever the map's palettes change, which is **each step of a screen fade**: the walk's game redraws it 7 times as its scene fades in. Its drawing called two functions for each pixel (the tile's colour number, then its colour), decoded a metatile's tiles three times (the whole, then each layer) and copied each slot's box a pixel at a time. | **Faster, the same pixels**: a tile is drawn a row at a time from its palette's colours converted once a pass; a slot drawn from its own cell takes the whole metatile from its two layers (the upper over the lower: a drawn colour is never 0); the box is copied a row at a time; the alpha masks are built in a register. Byte-identical: the code before and after side by side over 20,000 random metatiles (every tile, flip and palette) and 300 random scenes (375,584 slot draws: the three atlases, the present and updated slots, both masks); the engine's atlas test now checks the drawing against the per-pixel functions and a whole pass slot by slot (it passes with the code before too, and fails with a flip or the layers' order broken). The 3D test build's seam walk (Mauville, Route 117, Route 118, running; three runs each, alternating; `PKMN_DIORAMA_LOG=1` now gives each redraw's parts): a redraw's drawing **3.67 ms before, 2.29 ms after** (median of the fade's 18; the crossings' 6: 3.48 to 2.17); the rest unchanged: clearing about 0.95 ms, the cells' rules 2.4 ms at a crossing only, the upload 1.9 ms (software OpenGL). Windows run 286 (Mesa llvmpipe, engine `586a6156`): the face sort and the seam walk **passed** (160 chunks brought back, none differed; 36,866 skipped-sync checks, none stale). **Still open**: several new chunks at once; a crossing's atlas sync (about 7 ms all told here); and a screen fade's redraws (about 5 ms a step here). Options for the fade, each changing what is drawn a little, so not chosen: (a) the fade applied in the 3D shaders to an atlas drawn unfaded (no redraw; the GBA fades in 5-bit steps, a shader would blend in finer ones), (b) redraw on every other fade step (half the work, the 3D view's fade in coarser steps), (c) as now. L only for the drawing; not yet in a Windows run. |
| MS-118 | (update 2026-11-05; **the cells' rules resolve**, engine `d9a2db62`) | The 3D view's rules resolve (every one of its 6,561 cells: its terrain, structures, pixel objects, heights) runs whenever the grid moves, which is **on every step** while walking (98 times in the seam walk's 1,080 frames, 1.65 ms each here, up to 3.7) and at every crossing, not only at crossings as written above. Most of it was two look-ups through whole lists for each cell: all 500 structure overrides, and all 6,561 cells for each measured band and each pixel object's cell. | **Faster, the same cells**: the overrides are indexed once by layout and cell (their order kept within a cell, so the first that matches is found, as before) and the grid's cells by their source for the length of a resolve (the first in the grid's order, as before); the pixel objects already checked are kept in a set. Byte-identical: the resolve before and after side by side on 383 snapshots the game wrote out (the seam walk's 98 and 285 at the 14 places of the Windows geometry tour: towns, routes, caves, the Safari Zone, the Battle Frontier; 2,512,863 cells), every resolved cell the same; the resolve 2.1-2.2 times faster there (2.13 to 0.99 ms, 1.89 to 0.85 ms); check-diorama passed. L only; not yet in a Windows run. |
| MS-118 | (update 2026-11-05; **the three changes together**) | What the face sort, the atlas's drawing and the rules resolve change in the 3D game, measured together. | The seam walk before (`7f9b56bf`'s four files) and after (`6b75d6b6`), three runs each, alternating: the syncs' total about 6% less (1,620-1,722 to 1,548-1,580 ms), the atlas with the cells' rules about 20% less, the meshing about 8% less; frames over 8 ms 40-57 to 36-37, over 16 ms 6-7 to 4-7. **Still open**: the crossings' frames over 16 ms, which are their new chunks being built and the buildings' sync (about 3 ms each crossing), with the options above. L only (software OpenGL). |
| MS-118 | (update 2026-11-05; **the buildings' sync**, engine `c8ba843f`) | The buildings' sync rebuilds their triangles (about a megabyte, 38,000-41,000 vertices in the seam walk) whenever the grid moves a step; it uploaded them every time and hashed them byte by byte to tell whether they had changed (the sun's depth map is kept until they do), and the hash was about 60% of that sync: 2.3 ms a step here, up to 4.2. About half the rebuilds (46 of the seam walk's 98) bring back the same triangles. | **Faster, the same triangles**: a copy of the last ones uploaded is compared instead (an exact comparison, not a 32-bit hash): the buffer is uploaded only when they differ, and the depth map's generation moves exactly when they differ, as before; no buildings in view (rebuilt every frame) counts as the same as none. Checked: the old hash's decision and the new one side by side in a temporary build, with the buffer read back after every rebuild (the seam walk: 98 rebuilds, the generation moved at the same 52, the buffer always the triangles built; Granite Cave, no buildings: 263 rebuilds, it moved once, as the hash did); that readback is now part of `PKMN_DIORAMA_TERRAIN_CHECK=1`, which the Windows seam walk sets (this machine: 98 rebuilds and 385 in the cave, 0 differed). The buildings' sync over the walk **239-243 ms before, 95-98 after** (three runs each, alternating, and one more after; 0.84 ms a rebuild instead of 2.3, the largest 2.3-2.5 ms instead of 3.5-4.2); the syncs' total 1,720-1,795 to 1,573-1,598 ms in the alternating runs (the one more: 1,697, its meshing longer; the meshing varies by about 100 ms from run to run). check-diorama passed; the MinGW 3D build compiles. L only (software OpenGL); not yet in a Windows run. |
| MS-118 | (update 2026-11-05; **the chunks' signature**, engine `bc57e464`) | Timed inside the terrain sync (a temporary build): a map crossing builds 30-35 chunk inputs, and signing them (each one's cache key: the cache, the earlier versions and the checks find a mesh by it) took as long as building them, 3.0-3.5 ms of the crossing's 10-15 ms (12 ms of the first frame's 168). The signature fed FNV-1a its input's fields a byte at a time, and the game is a 32-bit program, where FNV-1a's 64-bit multiply costs several instructions for every byte: about 0.1 ms an input. | **Faster, the same keys**: the same bytes (the same fields, little-endian, in the same order) are written through a cursor of the function's own and hashed by MurmurHash3's x86 128-bit function (32-bit arithmetic, 16 bytes a step), its low 64 bits the signature; nothing keeps a signature beyond the game's run. Checked: the old function beside the new on the 2,009 chunk inputs the game wrote out at 14 places (1,775 distinct signatures each; every pair of inputs equal under one exactly when under the other), and 280,576 single-byte changes of them (random bytes of every input, every byte of the header and three cells of 40): the new signature changed exactly when the old did; 98.7 to 24.7 us an input. The seam walk with the terrain check: 160 chunks brought back from an earlier version, none differed; 28,718 skipped-sync checks, none stale. Three runs each, alternating: the same chunks built (418-421 and 419-423), the terrain sync 839-858 to 724-764 ms over the walk, a crossing's 10-12 ms to 7-9; frames over 8 ms 27-32 to 23-26. check-diorama passed; the MinGW 3D build compiles. L only (software OpenGL); not yet in a Windows run. |

Checked in a temporary build that filled the rows below with garbage before each redraw and, after its upload, cleared everything and drew again to compare: 69 redraws (the seam walk, a north-south walk, the 20-crossing walk, the scene started by seven seams; no neighbour page to all three), none differed. The clearing 1.0 to 0.38 ms a redraw with no neighbour page (most fade steps), 0.99 to 0.80 with two, the same with three (every row in use). check-diorama passed; the MinGW 3D build compiles. L only. |

| MS-118 | (update 2026-11-05 early morning; **the chunks' signature leaves out the cells' gameplay elevation**, engine `67e39839`) | Profiled on the recorded chunk inputs (gprof), a chunk's build goes 42% to merging its faces (most of it their sort), 18% to the build itself, 13% to the shell, 8% to sorting its spans; and of one recorded walk's 557 builds, 72 made the chunk's mesh again unchanged, a quarter of that walk's meshing (155 of 606 ms). In each, the chunk's input differed from the one before only in cells' gameplay elevation: an elevation-0 cell takes its neighbours' (`rules.c`), which changes as the grid moves, and the chunk's signature hashed it though the mesher never reads it. | **Improved** (engine `67e39839`; PC only, the 3D view): the signature hashes every field the mesher reads and not the gameplay elevation; with `PKMN_DIORAMA_TERRAIN_CHECK=1` each chunk kept though its gameplay elevation changed is built again and compared (the Windows seam walk reports it and fails on one that differs). L: the 2,009 recorded inputs built again with random gameplay elevation, four times each: the same meshes byte for byte and the same signatures; 7.9 million single-byte changes elsewhere change the new signature exactly when they change the old one; the checked seam walk: 86 chunks kept with another gameplay elevation built again, 0 differed (131 brought back from an earlier version, 0 differed; 36,922 skipped-sync checks, 0 stale); the seam walk, three runs each before (`88e58c7d`) and after, alternating (an AddressSanitizer walk running beside them): chunks built 412-415 to 368-374, the meshing 395-430 to 342-350 ms, the terrain sync 762-813 to 690-729 ms, syncs over 8 ms 31-37 to 17-24, over 16 ms 3-5 to 2-4 (software OpenGL; whole frames, mostly the software renderer, unchanged). **Options, not done**: (a) build a crossing's new chunks on worker threads within the same frame (the same pictures at the same frame; each worker needs its own scratch, about 11 MB of faces and spans plus its vertices, in the 32-bit game's address space, and the mesher's statics made per worker: not done now, a stability risk weighed against the gain); (b) the face sort tuned further (perhaps 10-20% of the meshing); (c) a crossing's on-screen builds spread over frames (chunks a frame or two late: a visual call, the owner's). **W: Windows run 293** (only the 3D graphics job, engine `67e39839`, app `c4d5c94`; GitHub's windows-2022 runner through Mesa llvmpipe, not a player's PC or graphics card): the seam walk exited normally, 131 chunks brought back from an earlier version (0 differed), 32,787 skipped-sync checks (0 stale), 86 chunks kept with another gameplay elevation built again (0 differed), the buildings' buffer read back after 98 rebuilds (0 differed); the Mega battles, the geometry probe and the benchmark passed too. |
| MS-118 | (update 2026-11-05 evening; **the owner's directive of 2026-11-05**: no drawing under a whole fade, builds spread by time, the crossings' pop; engine `7b839ec1`) | What the earlier changes left was a crossing's own work: walking over a seam turned the atlas's neighbour pages round (page 0 is always the map the player is on), so the slots of the chunks by the seam changed and the atlas was redrawn; and the cells past the edge of every map took the current map's border block (Mauville's water against Route 117's trees), so the far cells changed at each crossing: a visible pop and more chunks built. A screen faded out whole was still drawn in full each frame, and the builds that may wait were a fixed 2 and 4 a frame. | **Done** (engine `7b839ec1`; each part has a switch to compare): (1) the atlas's regions: each neighbouring map's tileset keeps its region of the atlas while it is in view, so a crossing turns the pages round but not the slots, and the atlas's signature is the art its regions are drawn with (`PKMN_DIORAMA_STICKY_PAGES=0` turns it off); (2) the nearest map's border: a cell past every map takes the border of the nearest map within two connections that shares a tileset already in use (`PKMN_DIORAMA_NEAREST_BORDER=0`); (3) builds by time: the builds that may wait (off screen, kept from the map behind) are made while the terrain sync has spent under 3 ms, up to 6 and 8 a frame, at least one every 8 syncs (`PKMN_DIORAMA_BUILD_BUDGET_US`; 0: the fixed 2 and 4); (4) a screen faded out whole (every palette the fade's colour) is cleared to that colour and the scene is not drawn; the camera follows on and the atlas keeps its art until the screen shows again (`PKMN_DIORAMA_DRAW_FADED=1` draws as before). Mail: (a) keep refusing (the player takes it off first); (b) carry it: the host copies the Mail's words into a free Mail slot of the receiving save and points the Pokemon at it, refusing the trade when the receiver's Mail slots are full. (b) for Eggs follows the games and needs no gameplay call beyond confirming that the hatcher becomes the original trainer; recommended when Eggs are wanted in trades. **Decided 2026-11-05** (the owner, `docs/owner-decisions.md` #39): both allowed, every valid Pokemon and Mail datum kept, trades atomic and recoverable with nothing duplicated or lost: option (b) for both, to build. **Built 2026-11-05** (app `fb7f8bd`; engine `23887292`): Mail travels with its Pokemon in every exact world trade (the sender's party Mail slot cleared as ClearMail clears it, its 36 bytes copied to the receiver's first free party Mail slot, the received record pointing at it; refused before anything moves when the Mail is damaged or the receiver has no free slot). L: `tests/test_trade_eggs_mail.py` (40) and the 108 affected test files; `--selftest-breeding` 94/94 (10 new: the old way's Bad Egg, another trainer's shiny Egg hatched in the party and in the PC's last slot, a plain one staying plain, the player's own unchanged, a damaged record refused); the GBA ROM byte-identical. **Real two-game trades of Mail and Eggs passed** (2026-11-05, Linux, the 3D test build of engine `23887292`, two real native games through real launchers and a real world host; app `ce2af0f`, `0cbe0df`, `4f9d219`: `native_launcher_newcomers_proof.py --scenario trade-mail` and `trade-egg`, `trade_eggs_mail_evidence.py`): Mail (runs mail-3, mail-4): a Wingull holding Wave Mail for a Treecko holding Wood Mail, applied in 10-16 s, each received Pokemon pointing at Mail slot 0 holding exactly its sender's 36 bytes, two Mails before and after, each held once, every other slot and the PC mailbox unchanged; then the Treecko for a Zigzagoon one way (the sender's slot left as ClearMail leaves it, the Mail in the receiver's first free slot); each Mail read in the game (START > POKEMON > MAIL > READ, pictured: the screen's Mail is the save-block slot the Pokemon names, its bytes the host's); the trade cut at four points (a launcher stalled 8 s at its confirm; cut before the commit: cancelled, Resolve interrupted trade, nothing committed, the trade made again; cut after the commit: Resolve verified it, the reopened game at the post-trade revision read the Mail; the game stopped 20 s after the commit: applied when it ran again): each Mail ended on exactly one side. Eggs (egg-party-2/-3, egg-box-3/-4): a shiny Togepi Egg traded for a Mudkip arrived byte-identical and hatched in the receiver's party after 50-61 steps ("Togepi hatched from the EGG!") and, moved to the PC by the test build's hook, in a PC box after 47-64 steps ("PC EGG HATCHED!"): in the host's save no Bad Egg, the checksum right, the receiver its original trainer (ID, name, gender), species, personality, IVs, moves and shininess kept; the host's ownership record took the hatcher's ID; the hatched Togepi traded on with everything kept; a returning copy of the unhatched Egg (the sender's older save) refused by the host's approval (`pokemon_owned_here`, nothing written). Closing saves, a fresh Continue and the store's integrity passed in every run; no app or engine fault found (four faults of the proof scripts fixed: an Egg fixture's count, PLAYERS opened blind, a stale pick, the reset chord). On Windows (run [336](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42129167609), engine `14187f20`, app `87f5862`, GitHub's windows-2022 runner, real Windows game processes): **passed**: the Mail trades with the packaged Classic 2D game (both Mails byte for byte, all three read in the game's Mail screen, the one-way trade into the first free slot) and the Egg trades with the Classic 2D test build (hatched in the party, and in a PC box after a reset by Ctrl+R and the test hook, 64 steps; the hatcher its original trainer; the old unhatched copy refused), integrity, closing saves and a fresh Continue in each. Not shown: a player's PC, a receiver with no free Mail slot in a real game, the trade cut once both confirmed, an Egg reaching the PC through the in-game PC menu, a returning copy through the launcher's screens. On Windows (run [327](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42092492022), engine `b270e14a`, app `e60aba0`): the Windows build's engine self-tests (`--selftest-breeding` with the hatcher re-keying), the breeding job, the app's whole suite and the form and evolution trade proofs passed; no Egg or Mail trade has been made on Windows yet. |
| MS-195 | Low (save integrity's report; **fixed** 2026-11-05, app: this change; found by Windows run 294) | A world whose database could not be opened at that moment (another process still holding it, an I/O error, a full disk) was reported as damaged: the store's open called every SQLite error `storage_corrupt` ("checkpoint database could not be opened safely") and named no cause. Windows run 294's kill test met it when it reopened a world its server, killed through a virtual environment's launcher, still held ("disk I/O error": the test's fault, fixed in the test), but a player's launcher would have said the same. | Fixed: as in a transaction, only SQLite's `SQLITE_CORRUPT` and `SQLITE_NOTADB` are damage; anything else is `storage_error` and names SQLite's error ("checkpoint database could not be opened now (SQLITE_IOERR_SHMLOCK)"). Nothing retries by itself: the player opens the world again. L: `tests/test_storage.py` (injected errors at the first statement; a lock held elsewhere; a non-database file and a damaged checkpoint still damage). W: the kill test passed in Windows run 296 (smoke); the store change **passed** in runs 317 and 322's `app-tests` (the app's whole suite on Windows, none failed). |
| MS-196 | Low (save integrity, a power cut; **fixed** 2026-11-05, engine `9edbceed`; found by the save-integrity review) | The native game writes its whole save (one 128 KB file holding both of the game's save slots) to `pokeemerald-mp.sav.tmp` and renames it over the save, so a game that crashes never leaves a half-written save; but it did not flush the file's data to the disk before the rename. A rename can reach the disk before the data of the file it names (NTFS and ext4 journal their own records, not the data), so a power cut or a system crash in the seconds after a save could leave an empty or garbage file where the save was. Not seen; found reading the save path. | Fixed (PC only; the GBA ROM unchanged): the data goes to the disk first (`FlushFileBuffers` on Windows, `fsync` elsewhere), then the rename (`MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH` on Windows, as before): after a power cut the old save or the new one is there, whole. Best effort: a disk that cannot flush (some network shares) still saves as before and the log says so once; a write and flush of 100 ms or more is logged ("a slow disk"). L: the shiny check's keep-restart (a save, the game closed, a second game continuing from it) in the Classic 2D test build under strace (one `fsync` of the temporary file, 0.8 ms here, before the one rename of each save) and in the Windows test build under Wine (`FlushFileBuffers`, `MoveFileExA`; no flush warning): both passed; the Windows games compile (MinGW). W: the feature checks' keep-restart with this engine **passed** in runs 310 and 322 (Windows game processes). A power cut itself cannot be shown here. The app's own files (live checkpoints, display settings, saved servers) already flush before they replace. |
| MS-12 | (update 2026-11-05; **its remaining cases checked: not reached by the launcher**, no change) | The fix of 2026-11-03 left these to stop the bridge: another operation's CANCEL, a second CANCEL, or any other command arriving while a command the game has not answered is outstanding. Each needs the launcher to send a further save command while the game still runs no frames, more than 32 s after the first (12 s for the command, 20 s for its CANCEL). Read against the launcher (app `abcde6e`): every save command comes from four places, a checkpoint (`live_checkpoint.py`: the autosave, Save, enrollment, the saves around a co-op battle and a Tower floor), a trade (`live_trade.py`), a trade's effect (`trade_effect_session.py`) and a battle's admission (`battle_admission_session.py`'s party query). A checkpoint whose command timed out ends in `save_recovery_required`: `_save_failed` then forbids every later save of that game session (`can_save`, `_autosave_safe`), so no checkpoint follows; a trade, a trade's effect and a battle start from the player's choice in the running game, and a game that runs again answers its outstanding command and then the waiting CANCEL at its next frames, before any choice can be made. The recovery controls that may arrive meanwhile (BIND, REBIND_TRADE, QUERY_TRADE, QUERY_EMERGENCY, PROMOTE_EMERGENCY_EXPORT, QUERY_EFFECT_RECORD) are let through by the bridge as before. | No change: the bridge's rule (one outstanding command; anything else stops it) stays the protection of the game's single save mailbox. Would need a change if a future feature sends save commands on its own timer (the launcher's companion forgets a timed-out command: `_dispatch_save_control` drops it from `_save_pending`); recorded in `docs/outstanding-issues.md`. |

| MS-197 | Low (Windows; **fixed** 2026-11-05, app: this change; found by the app's whole test suite on Windows, run 304) | The world's shared world, friendship and wager stores each keep one SQLite connection to a file of their own beside the world's database. A store whose file was refused as it opened (another world's data: "belongs to another world") or that failed to open raised its error but left its connection open until Python's garbage collector came round; and the world server, which opens the three in turn, left the ones it had already opened when a later one was refused (the server and its stores refer to one another, so only the collector frees them). On Windows a file a process holds open cannot be deleted, moved or replaced while that process lives, so after such a refusal the world's files stayed locked until the launcher closed; Linux allows it, so it was not seen there. The suite's first Windows run met it as its tests' temporary folders could not be removed ("[WinError 32] The process cannot access the file because it is being used by another process"). | Fixed: a store refused or failing as it opens closes its connection before the error goes on, and a server whose later store is refused closes the stores it had opened; the error, the files and everything else unchanged. L: `tests/test_store_open_failures.py` (a refused shared world, friendship store and wager store each close their one connection, and the file still opens for its own world; a world server whose wager store is refused closes the shared world and friendship store it had opened): the four failed before the change and pass after; the app's whole suite. W: **passed** in runs 317 and 322's `app-tests` (the app's whole suite on Windows, none failed). |

| MS-61 | (update 2026-11-06; **the proofs' reset key, pressed once just after a trade, dropped by the game**; fixed in the proofs, app: this change; the game and the launcher unchanged) | Windows run [347](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42179727165)'s multiplayer `busy-4` failed: New Friend 3's first soft reset never opened its question; the proof had pressed the reset key once, 0.1 s after that player's trade applied, and waited 60 s. The game takes the key only where START would open the menu (the free field, nothing running) and drops it elsewhere, by design (the engine's `multiplayer_soft_reset.h`); just after a trade the field may not be back. A launcher-side refusal would have opened the Soft reset page with its reason; nothing opened. Run 338 passed the same entry (its player had confirmed the trade first, so the field was back sooner). An agent read the whole log: no runner stall, the battle beside it ended normally. | **Fixed** in the proofs: `reset_once` waits for the game's field before the key (`on_field`), and `open_reset_page` presses again when nothing opened within 10 s (up to three presses), a window that opened and waits for its launcher being waited for without another press (`tests/test_proof_soft_reset_key.py`: 4 new). W: run [352](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42191272507)'s busy-4 **passed** (10:12-10:27 UTC, GitHub's Windows runner). |

The launcher refuses a Save while another operation holds the game's save (busy; right after a trade the emergency save is armed again) and a later press may retry - the fix of 2026-10-24 (app `f830795`) pressed again only in the harness's closing save; the newcomers proof's `save_all` pressed once, through the window's request (which drops a refusal silently). Run 338 passed the same entry (its Save landed outside the moment). | MS-165 | (update 2026-11-06; **the fix was incomplete: found on Windows, fixed**, app: this change, PC only, the game and the ROM unchanged) | The first Windows run of MS-165's test (run [349](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42180289917): the multiplayer job's new game-freeze entry, the host player's game process suspended for 10 s, 20 s into the two new characters' rival 1v1) **failed**: the battle was held for recovery 2.3 s after the game ran again. The fix of 2026-10-28 gave the game 15 s for each host command, but the launcher's battle observer (MS-62: while its launcher holds no host command it PEEKs the game every few milliseconds) still waited the companion's own 5 s. The game stopped with a PEEK out; 0.1-0.25 s into the stop the host handed the launcher the next command, which waited behind the PEEK; 5 s in, the PEEK was given up and the command went to the stopped game. When the game ran again it held two battle commands (the PEEK carries the sequence the next command carries; the game's bridge stops its link on a second outstanding command, and a late PEEK answer reads as uncorrelated to the companion): the link closed, the launcher left the world, and the host held the battle for recovery for both players. Linux had passed by chance (one run, its stop outside this window); any PC whose game stops for 5 to 15 s while the observer looks at it could meet it. No party or save changed. | **Fixed** (app, PC only): a PEEK waits for the game's answer as long as a host command (`battle_observer.py`, `battle_session.GAME_ANSWER_SECONDS`, read at each PEEK), so a command handed over during a stop goes to the game after the PEEK's answer, one command at a time. `tests/test_battle_observer_stall.py` (a model of the game's mailbox, one command at a time): 3 passed, and 2 of them fail with the PEEK's old wait. The newcomers proof's `--freeze-at-peek` (new) stops the game while its launcher has a PEEK out (the window run 349 met by chance); the game-freeze entry uses it. L (two Linux Classic 2D games at engine `5b18ebf1`, a real host and launchers, battle-trade with `--freeze game:0:battle:10 --freeze-at-peek`): with the PEEK's old wait the battle was held for recovery exactly as in run 349 (the same refusal, the frozen player's DELIVER never answered); with the fix, two runs **passed** end to end (the battle to its result, both trades, the closing saves, a normal close): the first run's stop met no host command (the burst of commands ended as it began); the second's proof stopped the game again until one did (its second stop): the command handed over 0.26 s into the stop waited behind the PEEK, which waited the stop's 10.0 s, and went to the game 0.2 ms after the PEEK's answer, answered in 84 ms; the battle went on to its result. The app's battle suites passed. W: run [352](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42191272507) (engine `5d9c2aa4`, app `70f5def`, GitHub's Windows runner): the game-freeze entry **passed** with the window met at the first stop: a host command handed to the frozen launcher 0.255 s into the 10 s stop, the battle on to its result (61 s), the trades after it. Not yet: two real PCs. |

(2) The player's battle exp bar (`UpdateHealthboxAttribute`) read the next level's experience at level 100, one past the growth rate's row of `gExperienceTables` (the next row's first value, or past the table for the last growth rate). | The gameplay plan's first eighth in the UBSan 3D test build (`soak` ubsan shard 0: a script moving an object not on the map; any battle with a level-100 Pokemon). | **Fixed** (under PORTABLE, each with its `#line`): the write skipped when the object is not on the map; at level 100 the exp bar is empty, as it was (0 of 1), without the read. | MS-205 | Low (the PC games; **fixed** 2026-11-06, engine `bc038550`, PC only, the GBA ROM unchanged; found by the UBSan build's run of the gameplay plan) | Three places where upstream code leans on what the GBA does, found by UBSan (`SANITIZE=1`): (1) `VerticalShakeTwice` (`pokemon_animation.c`, a Pokemon's animation, met in the Hall of Fame; shard 1) works out `(var6 - var7) * var5 / var6` for every entry of its table but -2, and the table's end `{-1, 0}` divides by zero. The release builds checked (Linux and MinGW, read in their machine code) place the division after the end's check, so it is never reached there (on x86 a division by zero would stop the game); UBSan reports the C code's order. (2) The native UI's Easy Chat screen reads the selected group's word count before any group is chosen, and `InitEasyChatScreenWordData` allocated its word data with `Alloc`, so the count was whatever the heap held and the screen read `selectedGroupWords` far past its 270 entries (index 458; shards 2 and 3). (3) `ApplyColorMap` (`field_weather.c`) builds the mask of the palettes to tint with the time of day as `((1 << numPalettes) - 1) << startPalIndex`; for all 32 palettes the GBA's shift gives 0 and the mask every palette, x86's gives 1 and the mask none (the Linux and MinGW release builds, read in their machine code: `shl %cl` keeps the count's low five bits). So on a map with daylight in clear weather, the tint the clock moves on to while the player stays on the map (each in-game minute of dawn and dusk, `OverworldBasic`'s update) and the weather's last step back to its normal colours were not drawn on PC until the next map change or fade, which tints correctly (shard 3). Seen on screen (the Classic 2D test game standing on Route 104 from 20:00): without the fix the picture did not change in 10 minutes but for its animated tiles; with it, it darkened a level by the second minute and again by the eighth. | The gameplay plan's eighths in the UBSan 3D test build (`ubsan` shards 1-3). | **Fixed** (engine `bc038550`, under PORTABLE, each with its `#line`): (1) the table's end skips the division (its amplitude is not used there); (2) the word data allocated zeroed; (3) the mask for 32 palettes is every palette, as on the GBA. L: the ROM unchanged (`f214735b...3387`), the line check (3 files, 0 moved GBA lines), the MinGW 3D and 2D and Linux 3D test builds (no warning in the changed files), check-native (9 suites, 0 failures); UBSan's eighths with the fix: 1, 2 and 3, where its sites were (455, 452 and 452 passed, none failed, none of its sites reported; shard 1's first game through the Hall of Fame to the ending again) and 6 (450 passed), the known `HandleAction_ActionFinished` reads only; the 56 self-tests in the same build (all passed); the tint on screen before and after (TEST_LEDGER). Not on Windows (no UBSan there); the next full run carries it. |

In the Windows 3D game built here from engine `bc038550` with the CI's toolchain (Ubuntu 24.04's MinGW, GCC 13.2) the table ends the program's `.bss` and entry 255 lies in the import table: `FillWindowPixelBuffer` took `SDL_GL_DeleteContext`'s address for the window's tile buffer and bytes of `SDL_FreeSurface`'s and `SDL_GL_CreateContext`'s addresses for its size, and wrote into SDL2.dll's code: the game closed. **Reproduced** under Wine 9.0 with that build (the field debug scene running the script): exception 0xc0000005 writing 0x7b468a40, in `CpuFastSet` <- `FillWindowPixelBuffer` <- `ClearStdWindowAndFrame` <- `SaveConfirmSaveCallback` <- `SaveGameTask` (the game's crash.log). In the Linux builds and the Windows Classic 2D game the entry falls on memory that is zero in play (the benchmark's frame times; the zeroed tail of the last page), so nothing was written there. The Windows CI never met it: its runs of the shipped 3D game (the Tower's screens, a battle and trades between players) reach no attendant's save, its minigames play the Classic 2D game, and its gameplay check plays the 3D test build (another layout). The prepared installers (runs 338, 344, 354) were built the same way from earlier engines; whether their 3D game's table lies the same way was not checked (their symbol lists are run artifacts this machine cannot download). | `PKMN_DEBUG_SCENE=field PKMN_DEBUG_DIALOG=save` (engine `d93170ef`'s debug scene) in the Windows 3D build, under Wine; any attendant's save in that game | **Fixed** (`window.c`, under PORTABLE with each `#line`): every function that takes a window id returns at once for an id past the table, as `PutWindowTilemap` and `ClearWindowTilemap` already did. L: the same Windows build with the fix, under Wine: the dialog asks "Would you like to save the game?" and the game runs on (exit 0); UBSan's Berry Crush run with the fix: no report; the ROM unchanged, the line check, the builds, check-native. | MS-214 | Low (save integrity's message; **nothing lost**, checked 2026-11-07; found by testing the host's disk filling up) | When the host's disk is full, a player's save to the world (the launcher's Save now, an in-game save in a world, the autosave) is refused: the world's database rolls the commit back (`SQLITE_FULL`), the server answers `storage_error` and keeps the session, the world keeps the previous save whole. The player's launcher keeps the save it was sending on that PC (the pending export, written before the upload and removed only by a commit), marks the session's saving as needing recovery and says "This save needs recovery. Close the game and reopen through Host Game or Join Game."; reopening sends exactly those bytes again, which commits once the host has space. The message does not say why: while the host's disk stays full, every reopen ends the same way, and only the logs name `SQLITE_FULL`. | A full disk on the host's PC (the world's folder: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\PokemonPrivate`). | Open, low: the message only (no progress is lost). Option: a status of its own for a refused commit with `storage_error`, worded "The host's PC could not store this save (its disk may be full). Your progress is kept on this PC and is sent again when you reopen the world." in the launcher and the in-game menu. Verified (L, a real tmpfs filled to its last byte, a real server, store and client over TCP): the commit refused (`SQLITE_FULL`), the session answering, the head still the previous revision, `integrity_check` ok while full; after freeing space the same commit (same upload id) committed as the next revision, a third save too, the store reopened: ok. The client's side by reading `live_checkpoint.py` and `launcher_session.py` and `tests/test_live_checkpoint.py`'s exact retries. |
| MS-214 | (update 2026-11-07; **fixed**, app `9e9524f`; the launcher only, no engine change) | The message of a save the host refused because its world could not store it. | **Fixed**: the server's `storage_error` reaches the session as the upload's `CheckpointError` (Save now, the in-game save in a world) or as the autosave's own status (`autosave.py` keeps the code `storage_error` instead of "checkpoint_uncertain"); the session's saving stops exactly as before (the save being sent stays pending on this PC; every later save is refused until the world is reopened), and its status is "save_host_storage" instead of "save_recovery_required": the launcher and the in-game session window say "The host's PC could not store this save (its disk may be full). Your progress is kept on this PC: close the game and reopen the world through Host Game or Join Game once the host has space.", the in-game save's banner "Host PC could not store it. / Kept on this PC: reopen later." Only the first failure's cause decides it: an earlier uncertain save keeps the general message, and every other way saving stops (a host restart, a trade's recovery) is unchanged; the refusal codes the in-game menus read are the same. Checked (L): `tests/test_launcher_save.py` (a Save now refused for the host's storage: the status, the refusal of the next save, the status after the game closed, the text; another refused commit keeps the general status) and `tests/test_launcher_autosave.py` (an autosave refused for the host's storage): the two new host-storage tests fail without the change; the whole app suite (GitHub's `python-tests` run 669 on ubuntu-24.04: passed; on this machine all passed but 11 window tests run without a display, which passed on one). W: the app's tests in run [367](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42340462396) passed (3,345 passed, 156 skipped; the three new tests among those run: their files skip nothing), and its installer, the one prepared, carries the change. A full NTFS disk on Windows: run [375](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42373037907)'s app tests (a 64 MB virtual disk filled to its last byte on GitHub's Windows runner, `tests/test_real_full_disk.py`): the commit refused with `storage_error` (SQLITE_FULL), the head and the database intact, the same commit accepted once there was room, the store reopened intact. A host's own PC: not tried. | Fixed (L and W) |
| MS-215 | Medium (save integrity: progress a player could lose by following the launcher's advice; **fixed** 2026-11-07, app `8a4b96d`, the launcher only; found by testing the player's own disk filling up) | When the disk of the PC that plays is full (not the host's), a save to a world (Save now, an in-game save in a world, the autosave) cannot write its journal (the 128 KB save and `pending-export.json`, written before anything is sent): the coordinator cancelled the game's export and raised the disk's error; nothing was sent and the world kept the previous save. The launcher then stopped saving for that game and said "This save needs recovery. Close the game and reopen through Host Game or Join Game." - but nothing had been kept to recover: closing the game, as told, ended the progress since the last save, which only the running game held. And the host-loss saver still pointed at the cancelled export, so a host loss before the world was reopened would ask the game to promote an export it had let go, which the game refuses with a permanent unsaved hold, instead of making an emergency save (the same after any failure before the journal whose CANCEL the game acknowledged). With room for the 128 KB but not the journal, that save file was left behind. | The player's disk full during a world save (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\PokemonPrivate`). | **Fixed** (app `8a4b96d`): when the journal cannot be written, the coordinator cancels the export itself; once the game has acknowledged the CANCEL and no journal stands, it removes the save bytes written before the failure and refuses the save as definitely as a busy game (`local_storage`, safe to retry). Saving goes on: the launcher and the in-game session window say "This PC could not store the save (its disk may be full), so it was not sent. Keep the game open: your progress since the last save is only in the game until a save works. Free some space on this PC, then press Save now, or wait for the next autosave.", the in-game save's banner "This PC could not store it. / Free some space, save again.", and the autosave tries the same base again a minute later (not at every poll: each try freezes the game while its save is read). A journal that stands after a write error (its folder's sync failed) keeps the uncertain path, as before. Whenever an export is cancelled with the game's acknowledgement and no journal stands (nothing of it was sent), the host-loss saver forgets it, so a host loss from then on makes a new emergency export; a CANCEL without a reply leaves it as it was. The case where the disk fills after the world committed (the receipt cannot be written) is unchanged: its journal stands, the save is in the world, and reopening resolves it exactly. Checked (L): a real 2 MB tmpfs filled to its last byte under the save folder (the test suite's strict ROM endpoint and the real store; TEST_LEDGER "Save integrity: the player's own disk full"): before the change the disk's error, the game released, nothing sent, and with 128 KB free a stray save file; after it `local_storage`, nothing left, and the same coordinator saved once space was freed. Tests: `tests/test_live_checkpoint.py`, `tests/test_autosave.py`, `tests/test_launcher_save.py` (the new ones fail without the change); the 66 test files touching these modules: 1,200 passed, 6 skipped; the whole suite on GitHub's ubuntu-24.04 runner (`python-tests` run 674): passed. W: the app's tests in run [369](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42356351245) passed (3,351 passed, 156 skipped; the six new tests among those run: their files skip nothing), and its installer, the one prepared, carries the change. A full NTFS disk on Windows: run [375](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42373037907)'s app tests (the same virtual disk): the save refused as `local_storage` with nothing left and the next one saved once there was room; a disk filling once the world had the save resolved exactly once there was room. A player's own PC: not tried. | Fixed (L and W) |

| MS-217 | High (Windows: a player whose Windows user name has an accent or another script; **fixed** 2026-11-07, engine `7b9f6bfa`, PC only, the GBA ROM unchanged; found by reading the game's file calls) | The Windows games could not open a file whose path holds a character outside the PC's code page. SDL2main gives `main()` the command line as UTF-8, and the C library (msvcrt) opens files with the ANSI calls, which read a path in the system code page (1252 on an English PC). The launcher installs the game for the user and keeps the player's files under `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, so every path it gives the game (`--save`, `--bridge`, `--config`) holds the Windows user name: for a name such as José or 测试 the game started on erased flash ("No save file ... starting with erased flash"), without its link to the launcher and its settings. No CI path held such a character, so nothing showed it. | Start the Windows game with `--save` in a folder named with an accent (Wine 9.0 with code page 1252 behaves as Windows does). | **Fixed**: the Windows games carry an application manifest whose activeCodePage is UTF-8 (`tools/pc_port/win32`; Windows 10 1903 and later honour it, earlier versions ignore it as before), so the C library's file calls take UTF-8 as SDL gives it. L (Wine 9.0, code page 1252, a UTF-8 locale): before, `--save` under "José" and "Zoë-测试" said "No save file" (an ASCII path loaded 131,072 bytes); after, all three load in the Classic 2D and the 3D game; a probe linked the same way: GetACP 1252 -> 65001 with the manifest, `fopen` of the UTF-8 path failed -> opened. The MinGW builds, `check-game-sections` for both, the Linux build unchanged. W: run [377](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42376370610) (engine `7b9f6bfa`, GitHub's windows-2022 runner, code page 1252): both games loaded the save's 131,072 bytes from `D:\a\_temp\José Zoë 测试\character.sav`, and the 60 self-tests passed; the check now runs in every full run. End to end in run [380](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42381341564) (the full run, every one of its 92 jobs passed): the installation check installed the program into a folder named "José 测试", both players' profiles under it, and played as two players would (characters made with New Game, the Solo world, a hosted world the friend joined, every save; in the Classic 2D game, the runner having no graphics driver for the 3D one), no error recorded; the self-tests job's step passed again; its installer, the one prepared, carries the fix. The fault itself was shown under Wine only (the check passes over games without the manifest). Not on a player's PC; a Windows older than 10 1903 keeps the fault. | Fixed (L and W) |
| MS-218 | Medium (save integrity: a character's earlier versions; **fixed** 2026-11-07, app `b77cb84`, the launcher only; found by reading the launcher's save code for anything ordered by the PC's clock) | A character keeps its 40 newest earlier saves for Characters > Restore an earlier version (`history/`), each named with the UTC time it was written, and the oldest names were deleted first. When the PC's clock goes back - set back by hand (as players do for the game's time-based events), a flat clock battery, a wrong date corrected - every new entry was named before the ones already there: once 40 were kept, each new version was deleted as soon as it was written, and a copy made with Back up now ("A copy of the current save was kept.") was deleted at the next save; the restore list showed the older versions first. The current save was never touched. Nothing else the launcher keeps is ordered by the clock (a world's saves are ordered by its revisions). | A character with 40 earlier versions, the PC's clock set back a year, then more saves and Back up now: none of them kept. | **Fixed**: a new entry is named with the clock's time, but never at or before the newest entry kept: after the clock went back it is named one second after the newest one, so the names stay in the order the entries were written, the oldest written are deleted first and the restore list shows the newest first (an entry written after such a change shows that time, not the clock's). Back up now pressed again with no new save in between keeps its one copy (before: a second one when pressed a second or more later). The files already kept keep their names. L: the repro (40 kept, the clock set back a year: before, the 4 newest versions and the copy deleted; after, kept and listed newest first); 4 new tests (`tests/test_character_library.py` `HistoryClockTests`: the 3 about the clock going back and Back up now fail without the change); the 6 test files using the library, 94 passed (TEST_LEDGER "Save integrity: a character's earlier versions when the PC's clock goes back"). | MS-219 | Low (multiplayer: the Battle Tower's solo battles and the eight-place championships' computer matches; **fixed** 2026-11-07, app `bfc288e`, the host only; found by reading the host's code for decisions made by the PC's clock, after MS-218) | The host settles these battles by time: a command the game never started is void after 120 s, a started battle that never reports is a loss after 24 hours (`tower_service.py`, `championship8.py`). Both were measured on the host PC's clock, so a change made to that clock counted as time passed: the clock set a day forward while a friend's Tower battle ran (by hand, as players do for the game's time-based events; or Windows' time sync correcting a clock a flat battery had left behind) counted the battle as a loss at once, and the win the game then reported was refused; the clock set a day back kept a command no game started open a day longer, the player's Tower closed meanwhile ("A Tower battle is still open"). Every other timer of the host and the launcher (sessions, autosave, trades, world events) already used a clock that never jumps. | The repro: a solo Tower battle started on the real host service, the PC's clock set a day and an hour forward: before, "forfeit", and the reported win "forfeit" too. | **Fixed**: the Tower's records and the eight-place championships take their time from a host clock (`steady_clock.py`) that reads the PC's clock and a clock that never jumps but counts the time the PC sleeps (Windows' tick count, Linux's boot time); a difference between the two of more than a minute between two readings is a change made to the PC's clock and is left out from then on. Time asleep still counts; a change made while the host was closed counts as before (the clock is the PC's again after a restart); the rules themselves (120 s, 24 hours) are unchanged. L: the repro before and after ("started", then "won"); 10 new tests (`tests/test_steady_clock.py` 7, `test_tower_service.py` 2, `test_championship_eight.py` 1): without the change in the host's wiring the wiring test fails, and with a clock that leaves no change out 6 of them fail; the 62 test files using the world, the Tower, the championships or the server, 1,008 passed (TEST_LEDGER "The host's clock and changes made to the PC's clock"). | MS-220 | Medium (save integrity: two launchers on one player's files; **fixed** 2026-11-07, app `6af8b76`, the launcher only; found by reading what stops a second copy of the launcher, after MS-218) | The launcher runs one game at a time ("A game is already running.") and keeps its record of the player's characters (which one last played which world account) in memory while it runs, but nothing stopped a second launcher on the same files. A second click on the desktop icon while the first was still opening (it takes a few seconds) started another menu that knew nothing of the first's game: a second game of the same character could run beside the first's, each saving the character (the progress of the one that closed first replaced as the character's latest by the other's; it stays in the character's history and in its world), and each launcher's record of the characters was written over the other's. | Start the launcher twice on one profile: both stay up, each with its own menu. | **Fixed**: the launcher holds a lock on `launcher.lock` in the player's private folder while it runs (`launcher_instance.py`: `msvcrt.locking` on Windows, `flock` elsewhere); the operating system lets it go when the process ends, however it ends (Exit, a crash, End task). A second launcher on the same files finds it held, says "EmeraldTogether is already open on this PC. Its window may be behind other windows or minimized on the taskbar." (a message box on Windows) and ends before it reads or writes anything of the player's. A lock file that cannot be used starts the launcher as before; another profile (another Windows user) has a lock of its own; the relay server command is unaffected. | MS-221 | Medium (save integrity: a game its launcher left running; **fixed** 2026-11-07, app `310e470`, the launcher only; found by following MS-220's one uncovered case, a launcher that crashed) | The launcher holds what its game needs to save into its world - the companion's connection and the lease on the character's save file (`character.sav.mirror.lock`) - but a game it had started did not end when the launcher ended without closing it (a crash, or End task on EmeraldTogether.exe in Task Manager's Details; its menu window did end, its input closing). The game played on disconnected ("Multiplayer bridge disconnected" in its log): nothing it saved could reach its world (the next start puts the world's save back and keeps the newer local one aside in the save's `.checkpoint-recoveries` folder: there is no offline progress; read from the code, not played), and with the launcher's locks gone, EmeraldTogether started again opened a second game of the same character on the same save file beside it. | Start a Solo game, end the launcher process alone (SIGKILL; `taskkill /F` without `/T`), start EmeraldTogether again and open the same world: two games of the character. | **Fixed**: each game the launcher starts (the play session's, the world registration's, the New Game's) ends with the launcher, however the launcher ends (`game_lifetime.py`). Windows: the game joins a job object the launcher holds, set to end its processes when its last handle is closed; Windows closes the launcher's own handle as the launcher ends, and the handle is not inheritable, so the game never holds one. Linux (the rehearsal platform): the game asks for SIGKILL when the thread that started it ends (`prctl PR_SET_PDEATHSIG`); the launcher starts each game from the thread of the session that waits for it. The game keeps what it last saved, as when its window is closed (its save file is replaced whole, never written in place); where this cannot be arranged the game starts as before. L: the repro through the play check's Linux entry with the Linux Classic 2D game: before, the Solo game kept running 8 s after its launcher was killed and the launcher started again opened a second game of the character on the same `character.sav` (both running); after, the game ended with its launcher and the next start's game was the only one. `tests/test_game_lifetime.py` (7 new: real processes, a stand-in launcher ended outright: its game ends, also when started from a session thread; without the module it outlives the launcher; a game that ends first leaves the launcher running), copies without the spawn's request or without the prctl call fail them; the play and New Game runtimes' tests check the spawn asks for it (2 new). The installation check's new step (`ci_play_check.py --launcher-ended`) rehearsed on Linux: the game ended within 0.3 s of its launcher, the world played on after the next start. W: run [391](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42408249412) (the app's tests and the installation check, engine `7b9f6bfa`, app `b9cb1b1`): 3,389 passed, 160 skipped, 12,034 subtests (run 389's 3,381 and 159 with MS-221's nine: eight passed, the one for elsewhere than Windows skipped, so the four with real processes ran on the job object), then the full-disk step 3 of 3; the installation check's new step: **passed**: the launcher (`taskkill /F` without `/T`) ended outright while its Solo game ran, and the game ended with it within 0.3 s; started again, World 1 played on from the same save (Save now: revision 2 to 3), the character's latest save the world's, Exit code 0, no error recorded (the menu window's own end, required from `fe21aad` on, was not checked in this run).
