Tried 2026-11-10 in the 3D test build, neither kept: the four cells of the wall's two rows under the PC's rectangle (2 x 3 at (6, 0)) left unflattened (`FlattenResolvedCells`; the cell dump: only those four cells change, their structure kept), then also the PC's own ground patches left out; each changed a few pixels and left the notch - the cell right of the PC shows a low orange block with black over it either way, so the wall's panel is missing there for another reason (the lobby's cells all lie flat; not traced further; scratchpad `ms227notch/`). **The League lobby's notch fixed 2026-11-11** (engine `440a11cc`, the building generator only, PC only): traced further, the lobby lies flat, its back wall a painting on its floor, and the PC stands there as the Center's piece found by its own pixels (`pc1f_pc_bare`, its bare twin); the twin left out the Center's floor marks round the PC (`_floor`) but kept the floor its drawing hides (`_under~behind`), as the Center draws it: three flat quads laid the Center's wall top over the lobby's own wall at (6, 0), (7, 0) and (7, 2), black over its trim (a scratch build without the PC's model, or without only those quads, showed the wall whole). A piece standing bare in another room now leaves out both; the same decal had laid a line of the Rustboro Gym's grey floor under its statue's feet at seven other gyms' doors (gone too), and Dewford Hall's table and the Briney room's chest and case carried theirs out of sight. L: 5 of 1,873 models change, only those triangles; every other model and every placement's ground patches draw the same texels; pictures before and after at 14 places; a new test (fails with the old rule); the Windows 3D game built (TEST_LEDGER "The Pokemon League lobby's notch in 3D"). W: run [426](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/42953525474) built it into its Windows 3D game and through every 3D check of the run, which passed whole (the tour's heights the Linux build's; not pictured) - the installer CI-426. Found at full size on 2026-11-10 (window pictures, engine `8b5856e9`), the same kind: the Battle Factory's battle room - orange dashes on the floor beside its side walls and the walls' red stripe a sawtooth at their foot (its hand-made corner pieces, `battle_room_specs.factory_hall`; not traced further); the Fortree Gym - the rotating gates' arms lie as dark bars on the floor, and blocks of the pale green squares stand beside them where the 2D game draws the squares flat (the room read off its cells stands them as things); the Seafloor Cavern's way in under the sea - the submarine drawn faint behind the player where the 2D game draws it whole at the top of the room, the rock ring round it a block high. Looked at again on 2026-11-10: the Fortree Gym's gates are **as drawn** - the 2D game draws the rotating gates' arms as flat dark bars on the floor too (seen from above), and the 3D view lays them so; the pale green squares beside them (metatiles 0x233, 0x23a and 0x23b) are blocked cells, which stand low as the room's other furniture does (the 2D drawing shades them as small raised blocks). New Mauville's door is **as drawn** - the opening's pixels over its shutter are see-through in both of the cell's layers (the GBA shows its black backdrop there), and the game's 3D view shows black there too (a window picture); only the geometry check, which paints such pixels magenta, counted them as a hole. Pacifidlog's houses: **explained, the diorama camera only** - a plain room's back wall is one upright panel of its two drawn rows (`plain_room`, 2 triangles), and the two rows it stands on are laid with the room's floor; from the diorama camera (pitched 52 degrees) that floor shows over the wall's top, the wall's own top pixels (black in the drawing, over the trim) a strip under it. The default (adventure) camera, at 42 degrees, does not see it (window pictures from (3, 4) and (5, 3)). The same in every room the general tileset's plain rooms model so (House 3 and 4, Dewford's hall, Wanda's house, Rustboro's houses, Briney's). Options: lay the cells a back wall stands on black, as the room's outside (the renderer would need the wall's cells marked in the models' export), or give the wall a top running back over its rows, laid with its drawing's top (black). **The back walls' strip fixed 2026-11-11** (engine `a79bc263`, the building generator only, PC only): not Pacifidlog's houses alone - from the diorama camera the floor showed over the back wall of Mr. Briney's house, Brendan's and May's houses, Rustboro's flats, Dewford's hall and the other rooms with one; and the second option was not kept, the top row of most rooms' walls being white, beige or the outline's dark, not black (a lid of it two cells deep). Each wall piece now carries a flat black quad over the floor it hides at 45 degrees, where the drawing over its first row is the room's outside (a wall standing free in a room keeps the floor behind it), over a doorway only under the lintel (the recess keeps its floor and its stairs), a quarter pixel over the floor and stopping half a pixel behind the wall's foot; it reads one black texel, the wall's own or a spare one its page keeps already. From the moment its battle handoff failed (`_fail_save`, the admitted entry still held) until that cancel had ended, the launcher offered "Abandon battle" - `_abandon_targets` counted the pending admission as a held battle - and its status read "battle_recovering". Abandoning closes the game and starts it again from its host save, so progress the host had not saved would be lost (MS-60's harm). The window lasts as long as the cancel (well under a second on the runners so far). | Windows run [433](https://github.com/kashkevin/personal-pokemon-game-emgba3d/actions/runs/43023225819)'s stale-save job (job 130541400837), round 2: the host entered first, the friend's Enter was refused ("Not saved: close menu to save"); 20 ms later the host's launcher offered Abandon (saving stopped, its game held), and the proof stops at the first such state ("MS-60 reproduced"). Runs 427 and 432 passed it: the cancel had ended before the proof looked. On Linux: the stale-save proof with each neutral cancel slowed by 3 s (a wrapper around `AdmissionCleanupSession.run`, not the code). | **Fixed** (`launcher_session.py`: `_abandon_targets` offers nothing while the entry is being cancelled neutrally - `_admission_cleanup_active`: the cleanup `_schedule_entry_cleanup` started and has not ended, or `cancel_battle_entry` running; a cancel that fails leaves the battle held (`_battle_recovery_failed`) and abandonment is offered then, as before; the status reads "admission_canceling" to the cancel's end, whatever is published meanwhile). L: the slowed proof before the fix ended in round 2 "MS-60 reproduced" (the host's launcher `admission_canceling` offering Abandon, `pending-battle-admission.json`, its game held); after it, passed end to end (the host's launcher `admission_canceling` without Abandon for 3 s, then `admission_canceled`, nothing held, both saving); 2 new tests (`test_launcher_admission_cleanup.py`; both fail on the launcher before the fix); the launcher, recovery, abandonment, GUI, exit and autosave tests 146 passed (19 skipped). | MS-244 | Low (the 3D view with the voxel trees, F8 - not the default, the 3DS trees; found 2026-11-12) | With the voxel trees the 3D game takes about a gigabyte more memory than with the default trees on software OpenGL (Mesa's llvmpipe, which the Linux checks and the Windows CI's 3D job use): at Petalburg City 1,058 MB resident against 253 MB, the C library's main heap 850 MB against 50 MB; over the tour's first 40 places up to 1.9 GB of address space against 0.8 GB (the Windows build is a 32-bit program, large-address-aware: 4 GB); the AddressSanitizer test build, which needs more, runs out of address space at some places with them. Cause traced: the trees are one 2,424-face model drawn once per tree (about 900 trees in the scene at Petalburg City, 800 to 1,400 at the woods and routes round it), again for the sun's shadows - millions of triangles a frame, which the software rasterizer bins in 64 KB blocks (237 MB of them live at the peak; over 100 GB allocated and freed while one place was drawn), and whose freed memory the heap keeps. Not a leak: it settles. On a graphics card the triangles are the card's work and this memory is not taken (not measured here: no GPU). | Press F8 in the 3D view at Petalburg City and watch the game's memory (Task Manager). | **Decided 2026-11-13: B** (`docs/owner-decisions.md` #58: detailed voxel trees nearby, progressively cheaper models farther away, simpler shadow geometry too; the memory to be measured, the seen-faces option alone not enough; priority 5). The options as written: Options: A. draw only the faces an eye above and south of a tree can see (the existing `PKMN_DIORAMA_TREE_FACES=seen`: 35% fewer triangles; opt-in because about 0.1% of the picture's pixels change shade, by up to 12 of 255, where faces tie); B. draw the distant voxel trees with fewer faces, or as the default trees; C. leave it - the voxel trees are an option, the default trees are not affected. |

With one rendering thread (`LP_NUM_THREADS=1`, as MS-244's first measurement) the memory over 40 places -27% resident from the far camera (1,893 to 1,376 MB) and -26% from the adventure camera (930 to 691 MB), -38% at six wooded places (1,176 to 734 MB); with llvmpipe's own threads at those six, 682 and 692 MB as built against 761 and 1,080 MB as before in two runs (the default trees 274 MB) - the whole trees' peak unsteady there, the built trees' not. No growth on revisits (Route 119 and Petalburg City in turn, five times each: 569 to 583 MB from the second visit on). **W (run 450, Mesa 26.2): 583 MB at the peak as before and as built alike** - no saving shown there (why is not established; the address space at most 956 MB of 4,096). Not as low as the default trees: every tree on the far camera's picture is as large there as the player's and stays whole. An option, the owner's: coarser trees past ~10 tiles from the player as well - measured 665 MB resident from the far camera (one thread), at the cost of that camera's outer trees drawn visibly coarser. The AddressSanitizer sweep: 529 of 529 places drawn, no crash (10:05-14:31 UTC, stopped once by this session's worker restart after 135 places and resumed into the same folder): one game process stopped on ASan's out of memory - a 2 MB allocation inside Mesa after its 28th place, Route 119's Weather Institute 1F; the next process drew on from the 2F - against 14 in the same sweep of `e6c15824` (which left 74 places undrawn; 16 more in their rerun); the other 22 processes that ended between places did so where that sweep's did (a cutscene, a facility's rooms), the probe going on from the next place. Besides, 43 reports in 22 processes' logs, every one a read of 2 or 4 bytes past the end of constant data, none a write: all of MS-97's kinds at tables the earlier sweep named, and one table it had not (the decoder's read-ahead past `sFrontierSquares_EmptyBg_Tileset`, the Frontier squares transition; KNOWN_ISSUES MS-97's update of 2026-11-13). W: the next full run (the step reworked: the default trees for scale, the revisits; it fails on a regression or on memory kept on every visit). |
